### DEV-031 — Full-corpus ingestion run (Track F); DEV-029's "Apollodorus untouched" claim was wrong — `_PAGE_HEADER_LINE`'s widened class also strips Apollodorus's own title line

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 3 (Full Corpus) — Track F (ingestion run) + Track G (verification) |
| **Original Plan** | `TODO-stage3.md` F2 expected console output `Skipping 260 of 260…` for Apollodorus (proving DEV-029's cleaning change was byte-identical for it) followed by fresh inserts for the 5 new sources. G4 pre-authorized the remedy if that invariant broke: `clear_source_chunks()` + re-embed once, logged as a DEV entry. |
| **What Changed** | Ran `python main.py` against all 6 sources (env: `ingestion/.venv`, `.env` at repo root). Apollodorus printed `Skipping 236 of 260…`, not `260 of 260` — row count came out as 284, not 260. Root-caused by diffing `clean()` output byte-for-byte between the pre-DEV-029 version (recovered via `git show ddce25f:ingestion/loader/text_cleaner.py`) and the current version, both run against the real `apollodorus_bibliotheca_frazer1921.txt`: the first divergence is character 0 — DEV-029's widened `_PAGE_HEADER_LINE` (`^[A-Z\s]+$` → `^[A-Z\s,()'\-]+$`, added to strip punctuated preamble titles like `"OVID, METAMORPHOSES"`) now *also* strips Apollodorus's own title line, `"APOLLODORUS, THE LIBRARY (BIBLIOTHECA)"` (which the old, narrower regex left in place — it contains a comma and parens the old class didn't match). DEV-029's claim that Apollodorus was untouched only checked the footnote-marker regex change; it never re-verified the page-header regex change against Apollodorus specifically. Stripping that line shifts every downstream character offset by ~39 chars, desyncing chunk boundaries for a stretch of the document until the chunker naturally resyncs on a later paragraph break — producing 24 chunks with new `content_hash`es that `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` inserted as *extra* rows (stale + fresh side by side for the same `passage_ref`s) rather than replacing the old ones, since the constraint dedupes exact matches only. Confirmed via `SELECT passage_ref, count(*) ... HAVING count(*) > 1` (e.g. `1.6.3` had 5 rows) and inspecting two rows directly — same passage, different chunk boundaries, both attributed correctly but overlapping. Remediated per G4's pre-authorized path: user confirmed proceeding, then `clear_source_chunks(conn, 'apollodorus-bibliotheca')` (260 old rows deleted) followed by a re-run, which re-embedded Apollodorus fresh under the corrected cleaning logic — landed back at exactly 260 rows (the shift this time resynced to the same total, though individual chunk boundaries/hashes differ from the original Stage-2 embedding). Confirmed idempotent: a third `python main.py` run skipped all 3037 rows across all 6 sources with no changes. |
| **Reason** | The new page-header behavior is correct — Apollodorus's title line is exactly the same class of preamble boilerplate DEV-029 already strips for the other 5 sources (Ovid's `"OVID, METAMORPHOSES"` is the direct precedent, covered by `test_preserves_all_caps_section_titles_after_first_structural_marker`). The bug was in DEV-029's *verification*, not its code: the claim "never matched the footnote regex either before or after" was true but incomplete — it didn't check the page-header regex against Apollodorus at all. This is exactly the gap Track G's `G4` (Apollodorus immutability re-run check) was written to catch, and it did. |
| **Impact** | No code or schema change — the DEV-029 regex behavior stands as correct; this entry only corrects the record and documents the DB remediation. Final state, all verified against the live DB: **3037 total chunks** — Apollodorus 260, Theogony 57, Homeric Hymns 126, Iliad 1112, Odyssey 724, Ovid 758 (`TODO-stage3.md` G1). `G2`'s corrected per-source fallback-ref audit (see DEV-030's sibling fix in `TODO-stage3.md`) found exactly one suspicious row per source (two for Theogony) — all confirmed legitimate metadata-preamble fallback chunks (translator credit + source URL, before each file's first structural marker), zero extractor misses (`G2`). Spot-checked 6 real refs across all 6 sources against their actual passage content — all correct (`G3`, e.g. Iliad `9.114` embassy-gifts passage, Theogony `116` cosmogony hills passage, Odyssey `24.520` Athena-invocation passage). `G5`: single `embedding_model = text-embedding-3-large` across all 3037 rows; `EXPLAIN` confirms `Index Scan using narrative_chunks_embedding_hnsw_idx` is used for the halfvec-cast similarity query. `TODO-stage3.md` F1/F2/G1–G5 marked done; the Stage-3 "Done when" bar (all 6 sources indexed, non-zero, real passage refs) is met. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-13 |

### DEV-032 — Raw `[N]`-shaped passage markers were leaking into stored/embedded `narrative_chunks.content`; stripped post-ref-resolution in the chunker, all 6 sources re-embedded

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 3 (Full Corpus) — `chunker/text_chunker.py`, post-Track-F data quality |
| **Original Plan** | Neither `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §4` nor any test ever specified whether the raw scraped marker (`[90]`, `[1.1.1]`, etc.) should be stripped from a chunk's stored `content` once its `passage_ref` is resolved — the plan only describes using markers to *compute* offsets/refs (`"the chunker uses these to assign the most-recently-seen ref to each chunk"`). `text_chunker.py`'s `chunk()` builds `chunk_text` by directly joining sentence slices from the marker-preserved cleaned text (a DEV-029 prerequisite — extractors need the markers present to compute offsets) and never removed them afterward. |
| **What Changed** | User manually inspecting query output noticed markers embedded mid-sentence in stored content (e.g. Iliad `9.114`'s chunk contained `"...Argive Helen. [141] \"And if we return to Achaean Argos..."`). Quantified across the live DB: 90%+ of chunks in the 5 non-Apollodorus sources contained at least one leaked bare `[N]` marker (avg 1.2–2.1 per chunk, up to 7 in one chunk); Apollodorus was affected identically in kind but far less visibly (its `[book.chapter.section]` markers only land once, at a chunk's own start, since it marks section starts rather than every verse line). Fixed in `chunker/text_chunker.py`: added `_EMBEDDED_MARKER = re.compile(r"\[+(?:E\.)?\d+(?:\.\d+)*[a-z]?\]\s*")`, applied via `.sub("", ...)` to the joined chunk text (plus `.strip()`) right after `passage_ref` is resolved via `_nearest_ref`, so ref-computation still runs against the original marker-intact text (offsets/sentence-overlap logic untouched) and only the *stored* text is post-processed. The regex was built and validated against every real bracket shape actually present in the 6 corpus files (checked via `grep -ohE '\[[^]0-9][^]]*\]'` and similar) before wiring it in: matches bare `[90]`, dotted `[1.1.1]`/`[E.1.1]`, trailing-letter `[E.6.15a]`/`[929a]`, and the `[[219]`/`[[713]`-style doubled-open-bracket OCR glitches found in the Ovid file (`\[+` — one-or-more leading brackets) — while leaving genuine translator editorial brackets untouched (`[Jason]`, `[Medea]`, `[Zeus speaking:]`, `[from Euboea]`, and a full-sentence stage direction in Apollodorus's Epitome), since none of those start with a digit or `E.`. Added `test_embedded_markers_stripped_from_stored_chunk_text` and `test_editorial_brackets_are_not_stripped` to `test_text_chunker.py` (48 tests passing total). Dry-run against all 6 real corpus files confirmed: identical chunk counts to before (260/57/126/1112/724/758 — the fix changes chunk *content*, not chunk *boundaries*), zero leftover digit-brackets in any chunk, both editorial-bracket examples survived verbatim. Cleared and re-embedded all 6 sources (not just the 5 visibly-affected ones, for consistency — Apollodorus had already been re-embedded once this session per DEV-031, so the marginal cost of doing it again was small). Post-re-embed: same 3037-row total and per-source counts as DEV-031's baseline, zero leftover markers confirmed via DB query, idempotent re-run confirmed (`Skipping N of N` for all 6), `G2` fallback-audit counts unchanged from DEV-031's verified baseline. |
| **Reason** | This directly undermines ADR-014's stated goal — `passage_ref` exists precisely so the raw scraped marker doesn't need to appear in user-facing text, but the marker was leaking into `content` anyway, which is both (a) noise in the text actually sent to the embedding model, and (b) a real risk of a visible artifact (`"...[141] \"And if..."`) if `RagAgent` ever quotes `content` verbatim in a synthesized answer — exactly the kind of rough edge the product's "clean, attributed citation" positioning is meant to avoid. The bug predates this session (Apollodorus has had the same issue since Stage 2), but was low-visibility there since Apollodorus's markers are sparse (once per section); DEV-029 made it far more damaging by adding 5 sources whose markers appear on *every verse line*, raising the leak rate from roughly 1-in-260 chunks to 90%+. |
| **Impact** | `chunker/text_chunker.py`: new module-level `_EMBEDDED_MARKER` pattern; `chunk_text` now passes through `.sub("", ...).strip()` before being stored on the `Chunk`. No `Chunk`/`SourceConfig`/DB schema change — `content_hash` recalculates naturally since `content` changed for virtually every chunk across all 6 sources (full re-embed, not incremental — same order of cost as DEV-029/DEV-031's runs, well under $1 total for the whole corpus at `text-embedding-3-large` pricing). `test_text_chunker.py` extended (46→48 total ingestion tests when combined with earlier stage counts). No impact on `passage_ref` values themselves (unchanged) or on chunk boundaries/counts (unchanged) — this is a content-cleanliness fix only. Downstream: Stage 6's `RagAgent`/`ConflictSynthesizer` (not yet built) can now quote `content` directly without risk of a stray `[141]`-style artifact reaching a user-facing answer. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-13 |

### DEV-033 — `passage_ref` was a misleading point value; now a containment range (`"9.114-9.161"`) with per-sentence refs in `metadata.sentence_refs`; full re-ingest

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 3 (post-verification data quality, follow-up to DEV-032) — `chunker/`, `loader/`, `pipeline/`, all 6 sources re-ingested |
| **Original Plan** | No doc ever specified what a chunk-level `passage_ref` *means*. In practice it was the nearest marker at or before the chunk's start — a lower bound presented as if it were the chunk's location. ADR-014 promises "citations a reader can verify against a real edition." |
| **What Changed** | Manual verification (post-DEV-032) showed the point ref is accurate only to "somewhere at or after the marker": two sequential non-overlapping Iliad chunks (then-ids 4365/4366) both carried `9.114` while content deep in the second truly belongs to line ~141+. Fixed per the user-approved plan: **(1)** `narrative_chunks.passage_ref` itself becomes an honest **containment range** — `"start-end"` where end = (first marker after the chunk's end) minus 1 line, e.g. `"9.114-9.161"`; collapses to a bare point when the chunk sits inside a single marker interval; keeps the `"Author, Work"` fallback when the chunk *starts* before any marker (including preamble-straddling chunks). The stored form always repeats the full prefix (`"9.114-9.161"`, parseable); classical elision (`Il. 9.114–161`) is Stage 6 display formatting. **(2)** New shared helper `ingestion/loader/ref_ranges.py` (`nearest_ref`, `range_end`/`range_end_info`, `format_range`) — the chunker uses it now; Stage 4's A4 segmentation must reuse it so `variant_claims`/`relationships` provenance shares one notation. Decrement rule: only when the next marker's final dot-component is a plain integer **and** its prefix matches the nearest ref ≤ chunk end (so a chunk straddling a book boundary with markers on both sides legitimately gets a cross-prefix range like `"1.595-2.15"`); otherwise fall back to the last marker *inside* the span. **(3)** **Containment exception (documented, not hidden):** the fallback cases (cross-book/hymn boundary with no next-book marker inside the chunk, end of file, non-decrementable shapes like `E.6.15a`) return an end the content may extend past by up to one marker interval — understating, never fabricating. Counted per source by the new audit: Apollodorus 40, Theogony 1, Hymns 14, Iliad 21, Odyssey 18, Ovid 22. For Apollodorus the "exception" rows are in fact *exact*, not understated: its markers open `book.chapter.section` prose sections, so content between markers belongs to the last-started section — the fallback end and the minus-1 end coincide semantically. The understatement risk is real only for verse-line sources, and there only at book/hymn boundaries and EOF. **(4)** Sentence-level precision: `Chunk` gains `sentence_refs` (one `{"ref", "start", "end"}` entry per stored sentence, char offsets into the stored chunk text), persisted as `metadata.sentence_refs` (JSONB — no schema migration; `passage_ref` is TEXT, notation-agnostic). Stored text is now built by stripping `_EMBEDDED_MARKER` **per sentence before joining** (offsets accumulate during the join, so alignment holds by construction; marker-only sentences are dropped with no ref entry); stored bytes can differ trivially from DEV-032's strip-after-join, irrelevant under full re-ingest. `Chunk` also gains `end_offset` (not stored in DB) so offline audits can re-derive whether a chunk's end came from the fallback. **(5)** New measure-only diagnostic `ingestion/scripts/overlap_report.py` (dry run, no DB): per-source chunk-overlap ratios — Apollodorus mean 0.23 / p90 0.34 / 17.8% of chunks >30% overlapped; Theogony 0.30/0.46/37.5%; Hymns 0.26/0.37/28.8%; **Iliad 0.37/0.62/57.4%**; Odyssey 0.29/0.47/38.5%; Ovid 0.27/0.47/32.2%. `OVERLAP_SENTENCES` deliberately unchanged — decision deferred until the user weighs these numbers. **(6)** Cleared and re-ingested all 6 sources (old point-format rows must not coexist with range rows). |
| **Reason** | The point-shaped ref *looked* precise but was only a lower bound — directly undermining ADR-014's core promise, and about to be inherited by Stage 4's extraction, which stamps `passage_ref` provenance onto `relationships`/`variant_claims`. A range whose end is derived from the *next* marker is a true containment claim ("all chunk content lies within these lines"), matches classical span citation practice (`Il. 9.114–140`), and its precision ceiling equals marker density — so the user's planned swap-in of 5-line-density transcriptions mechanically tightens every range and sentence ref with no further code change. |
| **Impact** | `ingestion/loader/ref_ranges.py` new; `chunker/text_chunker.py` reworked as above (`_nearest_ref` moved to `ref_ranges.nearest_ref`); `pipeline/embedding_pipeline.py` metadata gains `sentence_refs`; `scripts/overlap_report.py` new. Tests 48→69 (`test_ref_ranges.py` new ×13; chunker +7 incl. containment-range, sentence-ref alignment, overlap-ref stability, marker-only-sentence, preamble-straddle; pipeline +1), all offline/green. DB after re-ingest: same 3037 rows and per-source counts as DEV-031/032 (chunk *boundaries* unchanged — only ref semantics and stored-text assembly changed); ranges/points/fallback per source: Apollodorus 223/36/1, Theogony 55/0/2, Hymns 124/1/1, Iliad 1107/4/1, Odyssey 723/0/1, Ovid 744/13/1 — poetic sources are overwhelmingly ranges, as expected (a ~1500-char chunk spans 15–35 verse lines); fallback counts unchanged from DEV-031's audited baseline. Zero malformed same-prefix ranges (end ≥ start verified); 150 cross-prefix boundary-straddle ranges corpus-wide (legitimate, spot-checked `"1.595-2.15"` Hephaestus/dream-of-Agamemnon straddle). The finding's own case now reads `9.114-9.161` with successor `9.141-9.181` (tightens to ~`9.114-9.140` once denser transcriptions land). `metadata.sentence_refs` non-empty on all 3037 rows; offsets spot-checked against `content` in the DB. Idempotent re-run confirmed (`Skipping N of N` ×6). Downstream: **Stage 4 A4** must carry each segment's end boundary and stamp `format_range(segment_start_ref, range_end(...))` (TODO-stage4 amended); **Stage 6** RagAgent citation rendering consumes `metadata.sentence_refs` for quoted excerpts with the chunk range as fallback (TODO.md amended); ADR-014 carries an amendment section; CLAUDE.md Data Model updated. **Pending user input:** which works get 5-line-density transcriptions (drop into `ingestion/corpus/`, update `file_path` in `source_registry.py` if renamed, re-clear + re-ingest just those sources; extractors expect the same bare `[N]`/`BOOK N` shapes). |
| **Date** | 2026-07-13 |

### DEV-034 — Paragraph-aligned chunking: chunk boundaries snap to marker boundaries; `passage_ref` is the paragraph's corpus-native range; cross-chunk overlap dropped (supersedes DEV-033's union-range chunking)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 3 (follow-up to DEV-033, same day) — `chunker/text_chunker.py` reworked, all 6 sources re-ingested |
| **Original Plan** | DEV-033 kept the 1500-char sentence-window chunking and made `passage_ref` an honest containment range over whatever the window happened to span. |
| **What Changed** | User review of DEV-033's output found (a) **duplicate ranges** — several chunks inside one large corpus paragraph all cited the same range (e.g. two Iliad chunks both `3.38-3.75`), and (b) **too-broad ranges** — chunks straddling paragraph boundaries produced multi-paragraph unions (`9.114-9.161`). Root cause for both: chunk boundaries ignored the corpus's own marker boundaries. Direction set by the user: *as narrow as possible; the ranges present in the corpus texts are precise enough* (which also **retires DEV-033's pending 5-line-density corpus swap** — corpus-native granularity is accepted as the precision floor). Decisions made via explicit user choices: **(1) one chunk per marker interval ("paragraph")** — verified against the real files that every `[N]` marker opens a prose paragraph covering verse lines N..(next−1); **(2) paragraphs longer than `CHUNK_SIZE * 1.2` split into ~CHUNK_SIZE sentence windows *with* `OVERLAP_SENTENCES` carried between sub-chunks** (intra-paragraph overlap costs nothing in ref precision since all sub-chunks cite the same paragraph range); **(3) no overlap across paragraph boundaries** (paragraphs are self-contained rhetorical units — the thing sentence-overlap was compensating for). `chunk()` now slices the cleaned text at marker offsets (plus a preamble segment before the first marker, which forms separate `"Author, Work"` fallback chunks — no chunk ever straddles the first marker anymore), sentence-splits within each slice, and stamps `format_range(nearest_ref(seg_start), range_end(refs, seg_end - 1))` — both endpoints exact by construction (start markers are no longer "nearest ≤ a mid-paragraph start"). Marker-only segments emit no chunk. `ref_ranges.py`, pipeline, and `Chunk` fields are unchanged from DEV-033; `sentence_refs` retained (entries all carry the paragraph's start marker — alignment audits + forward-compatibility). |
| **Reason** | A citation reader trusts a range to be both *correct* and *specific*. DEV-033's unions were correct but not specific (and let distinct chunks share one citation); the corpus's own paragraph segmentation is exactly the granularity a reader can act on — e.g. the old `3.38-3.75` case turned out to contain a `[58]` marker the union was blurring past, so the paragraph-aligned ref is the *narrower* `3.38-3.57`. Apollodorus improves the same way in the point direction: chunk = section, cited as the bare section (`1.1.4`), matching `Apollod. 1.1.4` citation practice. |
| **Impact** | DB re-ingested (all 6 cleared first): **3,524 chunks** — Apollodorus 427, Theogony 91, Hymns 173, Iliad 1,195, Odyssey 905, Ovid 733 (was 3,037 under window chunking); sizes p50 ≈ 400–1,100 chars, max ≈ 1,800. Refs: Apollodorus **all points** (sections); poetic sources overwhelmingly paragraph ranges (Iliad 1,167 of 1,195), points only for single-interval collapses and book/hymn-final paragraphs + EOF (where the DEV-033 containment-exception fallback now surfaces as a bare start point — 50/1/33/25/24/15 rows per source, exact for Apollodorus's section markers). **Duplicate refs now exist only where one paragraph exceeded the cap and split**: 29/4/4/46/21/56 ref groups (+195 total extra rows) — the corpus's own precision floor, accepted explicitly. User's cases verified in DB: `3.38-3.57`, `9.114-9.140`, `9.141-9.161`, one row each. Cross-chunk redundancy measured by `scripts/overlap_report.py` fell from mean 23–37% (57% of Iliad chunks >30% overlapped) to mean 1–3% (>30% share: 2–6%, all intra-paragraph sub-chunks) — this **resolves the deferred `OVERLAP_SENTENCES` decision**: the constant stays 2 but now applies only within split paragraphs. Tests: `test_text_chunker.py` reworked to paragraph semantics (one-chunk-per-paragraph, oversized-split-with-shared-ref, no-cross-paragraph-overlap, preamble-separation, marker-only-segment tests); suite at 70 passing. Idempotent re-run confirmed. Cross-prefix straddle ranges no longer occur in `narrative_chunks` (chunks can't cross book boundaries); `ref_ranges.py`'s straddle handling stays for Stage 4's A4 multi-interval segments. Docs: ADR-014 Amendment 2; CLAUDE.md Data Model, `TODO.md` Stage 6 note updated. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-13 |

### DEV-035 — ADR-014 correction: `sentence_refs` does not carry citation precision beyond the chunk's `passage_ref` (documentation-only, no code/data change)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 3 (documentation correction, follow-up to DEV-033/DEV-034) — `docs/adr/adr-014-passage-ref-citation-notation.md`, `docs/TODO-stage4.md`; no code or DB change |
| **Original Plan** | DEV-033/ADR-014 Amendment 1 point 5 described `metadata.sentence_refs` as "the actual precision instrument for Stage 6's `RagAgent` when it quotes an excerpt" — implying a sentence's ref could be more specific than its chunk's `passage_ref`. Amendment 2 (DEV-034) then listed point 5 among the amendment's "stand unchanged" items without re-examining it against the new paragraph-aligned chunking model it was itself introducing. |
| **What Changed** | A contradiction-analysis pass flagged that Amendment 2's own model (one chunk = exactly one marker interval) makes point 5's premise impossible: there is no marker between a paragraph's start and end for an individual sentence to resolve to differently, so every sentence in a chunk necessarily gets the same `ref`. User verified this directly against the live DB with two queries: (1) grouping `sentence_refs` entries by chunk and counting distinct `ref` values — zero chunks anywhere in the corpus have more than one; (2) comparing every non-null `sentence_refs[].ref` against its own chunk's `passage_ref` start — zero mismatches. Confirmed on both `homer-iliad` and `apollodorus-bibliotheca`. **Decision: keep the `sentence_refs` implementation exactly as-is (its `start`/`end` offsets remain genuinely useful for locating/highlighting a quoted sentence within stored `content`) and correct only the documentation's claim about what the `ref` field provides.** ADR-014 gained a "Correction (2026-07-14)" section: restates why the precision claim can't hold, reframes `sentence_refs`' real value as offset-tracking (not citation precision), updates the Stage 6 Implementation Checklist item to cite at the chunk level via `passage_ref`, and separately clarifies Amendment 1 point 1's "collapses to a point when inside a single marker interval" wording (ambiguous once Amendment 2 made "single interval" the universal per-chunk case — the actual collapse condition is narrower: decremented end equals start, i.e. adjacent markers one line apart; most poetic-source chunks still render as multi-line ranges, e.g. Iliad 1,167/1,194 marked chunks). Also pointed Amendment 2 at its own superseded exception-count numbers (Amendment 1's `40/1/14/21/18/22` vs. the post-DEV-034 `50/1/33/25/24/15`), and swapped `TODO-stage4.md`'s illustrative range example off the specific `"9.114-9.161"` union DEV-034 demonstrated was too broad for chunk-level ranges (A4's own multi-interval grouping is a legitimately different, wider use case — the note now says so explicitly). |
| **Reason** | An ADR asserting a capability the implementation cannot provide is worse than one that's silent on it — a future Stage 6 implementer following the original checklist wording would have built sentence-level citation logic that could never produce a citation different from the chunk-level one. Correcting the claim, not the code, was the right scope: the offset-tracking mechanism has real independent value (excerpt highlighting) and needed no change; only the surrounding prose overclaimed what it does. |
| **Impact** | No schema, code, or stored-data change. `docs/adr/adr-014-passage-ref-citation-notation.md`: Status line, Implementation Checklist item, Amendment 2 point 4, and new "Correction" section. `docs/TODO-stage4.md`: three stale `"9.114-9.161"` example references replaced with the current narrower shape, one with an explanatory note distinguishing A4's segment-grouping from chunk-level ranges. `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/TODO.md`'s Stage 6 note already described the corrected (offsets-only) understanding accurately as written during DEV-034 and needed no change. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-14 |

### DEV-036 — Track A built: A4 segmentation groups marker intervals (not blank-line paragraphs) up to a size cap, since `clean()` doesn't preserve blank lines

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 4 (Track A: `ingestion/extraction/` — A1–A8 all built) — `extraction/segmentation.py` design; no DB/schema change |
| **Original Plan** | `TODO-stage4.md` A4 says to "group whole sections between consecutive ref boundaries" so a full genealogical statement isn't split mid-claim, without specifying the grouping mechanism. The natural reading — group text between blank-line paragraph breaks, which may span several markers — turns out to be unimplementable: `text_cleaner.clean()`'s `_BLANK_LINE_RUN` step (present since Stage 2, reaffirmed by `test_collapses_blank_line_runs_to_a_single_newline`) collapses every run of blank lines down to a single `\n` before the passage-ref extractors or A4 ever see the text, so no double-newline signal survives cleaning for A4 to key off — the same cleaned text the chunker consumes has already lost that structure. |
| **What Changed** | `segment()` groups consecutive **marker intervals** instead (the same atomic unit DEV-034's paragraph-aligned chunker treats as one chunk), greedily folding subsequent intervals into the current group while the cumulative span stays under a new `SEGMENT_SIZE = 3000` constant (2x the chunker's `CHUNK_SIZE`) — deliberately coarser than the chunker's one-interval-per-chunk granularity, per DEV-034's own note that A4's grouping is "a legitimately different, wider use case." A single interval already over `SEGMENT_SIZE` still becomes its own segment (no further splitting — A4 doesn't need `_split_windows`-style sub-chunking, unlike the RAG chunker). The interval containing content before the first marker (the preamble) is never merged forward, so it doesn't swallow the next marker's precise ref into an `"Author, Work"` fallback — mirrors the chunker's existing preamble-separation behavior. `passage_ref` is stamped via `format_range(nearest_ref(refs, group_start), range_end(refs, group_end - 1))`, reusing `ref_ranges.py` exactly as directed, unchanged from the original instruction. |
| **Reason** | The blank-line reading of "whole sections" was never actually viable once `clean()`'s existing behavior is accounted for; a marker-interval size cap achieves the same goal (keep a multi-marker genealogical statement together, stay meaningfully coarser than `narrative_chunks`) using the one structural signal that does survive cleaning. |
| **Impact** | `ingestion/extraction/segmentation.py` (A4) new, alongside the rest of Track A: `schema.py` (A1, adds a `source_id` field to `ExtractedRelationship`/`ExtractedVariantClaim` beyond A1's original two-field sketch — both fields use `pydantic.json_schema.SkipJsonSchema` so instructor never shows them to the model; stamped mechanically post-parse exactly as DEV-021 established for `passage_ref` alone), `known_aliases.json` + `claim_type_normalizer.py` (A2/A2b), `entity_resolver.py` (A3), `claim_extractor.py` (A5, `instructor.from_anthropic` + `EXTRACTION_MODEL`/`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`), `conflict_detector.py` (A6), `run_extraction.py` (A7, `build_candidates`/`write_output` split from `main()` so the pipeline stays testable against inline fixtures without touching `corpus/` or a real DB), `requirements.txt` (A8: `instructor`, `rapidfuzz`, `anthropic` added and installed). `ingestion/extraction/output/` gitignored (generated, unreviewed candidates — Track B promotes rows out of it, nothing in it is committed). 50 new offline tests (`test_schema.py`, `test_entity_resolver.py`, `test_segmentation.py`, `test_claim_type_normalizer.py`, `test_conflict_detector.py`, `test_claim_extractor.py`, `test_run_extraction.py`), all mocked per `TECH_GUARDRAILS.md` (no live LLM/DB calls); full suite 107/107 passing. Track A does not touch `LangChain4jConfig.kt` or any `@AiService` (DEV-004 doesn't apply). Track B (run extraction against the real corpus + human review) is next; A4's `SEGMENT_SIZE` is a starting value, not tuned against real text yet — B1's Apollodorus prompt-tuning pass is where that gets a first real signal. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-14 |

### DEV-037 — Track D built: D7 (`EntityAlias`) is blocked on Track C6/`V14`, not independent as the parallelization guide states; `kotlin-allopen`/`kotlin-noarg` wiring completed

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 4 (Track D: JPA entities + repositories, D1–D6 complete, D7 blocked) — `domain/entity/`, `repository/`; `buildSrc` |
| **Original Plan** | `TODO-stage4.md`'s parallelization guide states Track D has "no dependency on anything else in this stage" and is independent of A/B/C. Stage 1's `TODO-stage1.md` A4 (marked `[x]`) called for `buildSrc` to add `kotlin-gradle-plugin` + `kotlin-allopen` so JPA entities wouldn't need to be hand-written `open`/no-arg — `kotlin-allopen` was in fact added as a `buildSrc` dependency back then, but the plugin was never actually *applied* in `blame-zeus.kotlin-conventions.gradle.kts` (no entities existed yet to need it), and `kotlin-noarg` was never added at all. |
| **What Changed** | **(1)** Completed the Stage 1 wiring: added `kotlin-noarg` to `buildSrc/build.gradle.kts` alongside the existing (unused) `kotlin-allopen`, and applied both `kotlin("plugin.allopen")`/`kotlin("plugin.noarg")` in `blame-zeus.kotlin-conventions.gradle.kts`, targeting `jakarta.persistence.Entity`/`Embeddable` — lets entity classes stay plain `class` with constructor `val`s rather than hand-written `open class` + manual no-arg constructors. **(2)** Built D1–D6 (`Source`, `EntityRecord`, `Relationship`, `Myth`/`MythParticipant` with `@EmbeddedId`, `VariantClaim`, `NarrativeChunk`) plus their repositories, verified against the real V1–V9 schema via the existing Testcontainers `FlywayMigrationTest` (`ddl-auto: validate` passes) and a new `RepositoryQueryTest`/`SourceRepositoryTest` exercising real queries (theta-join for `VariantClaimRepository.findByEntityNameIgnoreCase`, composite-key round-trip for `MythParticipant`, read-only `content_hash`/`embedding_model` columns on `NarrativeChunk`). **(3)** D7 (`EntityAlias` → table `entity_aliases`) was written, then empirically confirmed to break every `@SpringBootTest`-based test in the module: `entity_aliases` doesn't exist yet (`V14`/Track C6 isn't built — C6 itself depends on C2's finalized entity name list, which depends on Track B), and Hibernate's `ddl-auto: validate` checks every mapped entity's table at context bootstrap regardless of whether it's queried, so one entity mapped to a missing table fails the *entire* shared Spring context (confirmed: all 7 `FlywayMigrationTest` cases failed with `SchemaManagementException` the moment `EntityAlias.kt` existed). `EntityAlias.kt` was reverted; D7 stays undone until `V14` lands. |
| **Reason** | The parallelization guide's "Track D has no dependency on anything else" is true for D1–D6 but not D7 specifically — an `@Entity` mapped to a nonexistent table isn't a "write it now, seed it later" situation the way the SQL-only tracks are; it fails startup for every test in the module immediately, not just tests that touch that table. Better to name the exception explicitly than leave the next implementer to discover it by breaking the whole suite. |
| **Impact** | `buildSrc/build.gradle.kts`, `buildSrc/src/main/kotlin/blame-zeus.kotlin-conventions.gradle.kts` (allopen/noarg wiring — applies to `telegram-bot` too, but inert there with no `@Entity` classes yet). New: `domain/entity/{Source,EntityRecord,Relationship,Myth,MythParticipant,VariantClaim,NarrativeChunk}.kt`, `repository/{Source,EntityRecord,Relationship,Myth,MythParticipant,VariantClaim,NarrativeChunk}Repository.kt`. `NarrativeChunk.embedding` left entirely unmapped (DEV-025's `ContentRetriever`/Python pipeline own it; `ddl-auto: validate` only checks mapped columns, so an unmapped column is invisible to it, no `@Transient` needed); `content_hash`/`embedding_model` mapped `insertable = false, updatable = false` (DB-generated / Python-only-writer respectively). New tests: `repository/SourceRepositoryTest.kt` (fulfills Track G1 for real, since `V9`'s 6 real rows already exist — no fixture seeding needed) and `repository/RepositoryQueryTest.kt` (hand-seeded fixture rows covering the other 5 repositories, since `V10`–`V12` seed data doesn't exist yet — not a substitute for Track G's dedicated per-repository tests once Track C lands). Full `core-api:test` suite: 21/21 passing (7 `FlywayMigrationTest` + 6 `SchemaIntrospectorTest` + 2 `SourceRepositoryTest` + 6 `RepositoryQueryTest`). `TODO-stage4.md`'s Track D directory note and parallelization guide annotated to flag D7's real dependency on C6/`V14`. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-14 |

### DEV-038 — Track B run: `run_extraction.py` hardened for resilience (per-segment checkpointing, failure isolation, incremental `--source` runs) + `max_tokens`/dotenv/jupyter fixes

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 4 (Track B — run extraction + human-review scaffolding) — `ingestion/extraction/{checkpoint.py (new), run_extraction.py, claim_extractor.py}`, `requirements.txt`, `notebooks/`; no DB/schema change |
| **Original Plan** | A7's `run_extraction.py` (per `TODO-stage4.md` and DEV-036) was a straight-line loop — iterate sources → segment → extract → resolve → detect → `write_output` — accumulating results only in memory and writing once at the very end. B2 says "run against all 6 ingested sources" as a single shot. This shape has three failure modes against ~1,204 real API calls: **(a)** no failure isolation — any segment whose `extract_facts` raised (e.g. the `IncompleteOutputException` a genealogically dense segment like the Titanomachy throws at `max_tokens=4096`) propagated straight out of `build_candidates`, so `write_output` never ran and every already-extracted segment (and its API spend) was lost; **(b)** no resumability — a killed / crashed / rate-limited / budget-exhausted run restarted from zero; **(c)** no incremental path — no way to run one source, review it, then run the next. Additionally: `max_tokens` was 4096; `load_dotenv()` sat inside the `__main__` guard, *after* the top-level `extraction.claim_extractor` import that reads `EXTRACTION_MODEL`/`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` at import time (so `python -m extraction.run_extraction` crashed with `KeyError: 'EXTRACTION_MODEL'` before `main()` ran); and `requirements.txt` never declared Jupyter even though ADR-004's pipeline design mandates the B1/B5 review notebooks. |
| **What Changed** | **(1)** New `extraction/checkpoint.py` — append-only JSONL (`extraction/output/.checkpoint.jsonl`, gitignored), one line per segment attempt keyed by `(source_id, start_offset)` (`start_offset` is unique per source, unlike `passage_ref` which repeats for oversized-paragraph sub-chunks); later lines override earlier ones on replay, so a segment that failed once and later succeeded reads back as `ok`. **(2)** `build_candidates` now: loads the checkpoint, **skips any `ok` segment** (reuses cached facts, no API call), **retries `failed`/never-attempted** segments, and wraps each `extract_facts` in try/except so one bad segment logs `[FAILED] …`, records a `failed` checkpoint line, and continues instead of aborting the run; failures are collected into `ExtractionResult.failed_segments` and reported at the end. **(3)** Incremental `--source <id>` CLI flag (repeatable) → `active_source_ids`: `build_candidates` still walks **all** sources every call for output assembly (so `write_output` always reflects every source ever checkpointed — running sources one at a time must not discard an earlier source's output), but only spends API calls on uncached segments belonging to active sources; non-active uncached segments are skipped silently (not attempted, not marked failed). **(4)** `max_tokens` 4096 → 16000 in `claim_extractor.py` (Anthropic's documented safe non-streaming default; the failure was deterministic so tenacity retries never helped). **(5)** `load_dotenv()` moved to module top, before the `claim_extractor` import. **(6)** `jupyter`/`ipykernel` added to `requirements.txt`. **(7)** Per-segment progress printing (`… -- extracting…` / `-- cached` / `[FAILED] …`) with `flush=True` so piped/backgrounded logs update live instead of block-buffering until exit. **(8)** Two review notebooks built: `notebooks/01_test_extraction.ipynb` (B1 — prompt tuning on Apollodorus, with a resumable per-segment `results` dict, an `importlib.reload` cell, and a model-comparison section) and `notebooks/02_verify_conflicts.ipynb` (B5/B6/B7). |
| **Reason** | 1,204 real, paid API calls across the corpus — a single failed segment must not discard the whole run's spend, a restart must resume rather than redo, and the user's chosen source-by-source review workflow requires incremental runs that accumulate. The `max_tokens` bump was a concrete blocker (a real dense-segment failure), not speculative. |
| **Impact** | `checkpoint.py`, `run_extraction.py`, `claim_extractor.py`, `requirements.txt`, `notebooks/` changed/new (all committed: `d817741`, `35dc49e`). Tests: `test_checkpoint.py` new (7 — round-trip, failed-entry, override-on-retry, distinct keys, blank lines, parent-dir creation) and `test_run_extraction.py` extended (failure-is-skipped-not-fatal, rerun-skips-`ok`-retries-`failed`, `active_source_ids` gating, one-at-a-time accumulation); full ingestion suite **118 passing**, all mocked at the API/DB boundary per `TECH_GUARDRAILS.md`. **B2 is complete and validates this machinery**: the full corpus ran successfully via incremental `--source` invocations — 1,204/1,204 segments `ok`, 0 failures (Apollodorus 115, Theogony 23, Hymns 52, Iliad 393, Odyssey 290, Ovid 331), producing 2,594 entity / 7,406 relationship / 7,429 variant_claims candidates (all `trust_tier=3`, pre-review). Operational note for a clean re-extract: `.checkpoint.jsonl` persists between runs by design, so a from-scratch redo means deleting it (or the relevant source's lines) first. Known unaddressed risk (surfaced to the user, not yet fixed): `write_output` blind-overwrites `variant_claims_candidates.json` every run, so a re-run after B5 promotions would clobber `trust_tier=1` decisions — mitigation is to finish all B2 runs before B5, or copy the reviewed file aside. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-14 |

### DEV-039 — Extraction model switched to `claude-sonnet-5` (from ADR-008's Opus 4.8) via swap-after-eval; recorded here because `.env` is gitignored

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 4 (Track B — extraction model choice) — local `.env` only (`EXTRACTION_MODEL`); no code/schema/committed-file change |
| **Original Plan** | ADR-008 §2 selected **Claude Opus 4.8** as the offline seed-extraction model ("strongest tier" for the attribution-critical work). `EXTRACTION_MODEL=claude-opus-4-8` is what `TODO-stage4.md` A5, `.env.example`, `claim_extractor.py`'s docstring, and `requirements.txt`'s comment all state. |
| **What Changed** | The user set `EXTRACTION_MODEL=claude-sonnet-5` in their local `.env` after the B1 notebook comparison (Opus 4.8 vs Sonnet 5 on the Apollodorus tuning set). Observation driving it: relationship/parentage extraction quality on the smaller model was good; `variant_claims` were unstable on **both** models (a task-shape difficulty — dense/ambiguous disagreement extraction — not purely model size). Decision: use `claude-sonnet-5` now to produce the minimal working seed at lower cost across ~1,204 calls, lean on the B5 human-review gate + B6 hand-adds for `variant_claims`, and revisit claim quality later (possibly re-extracting just the claims on a stronger model). **No code change** — `claim_extractor.py` reads `EXTRACTION_MODEL`, so it is model-agnostic; only the env var moved. |
| **Reason** | ADR-008 §5 ("Swap-after-eval discipline — the recommended *targets*, not a mandate to swap blind: run the gold set on whatever is configured, then swap where eval shows weakness") explicitly sanctions exactly this. The B5 per-row review gate + `trust_tier=1` promotion means model choice never touches the trust guarantee — a weaker model just means more candidates rejected in review and more hand-adds, not lower-quality seeded data. |
| **Impact** | `.env` is gitignored, so **this entry is the only committed record that the Stage 4 seed candidates were extracted with `claude-sonnet-5`, not ADR-008's Opus 4.8** — provenance that would otherwise be invisible to any future reader (the docstrings / `.env.example` / A5 still name Opus 4.8 as the ADR-of-record default; left as-is, flagged by A5's DEVIATED tag). Full corpus already extracted on sonnet-5 (see DEV-038): 2,594 / 7,406 / 7,429 candidates. **B7 diagnostic is favorable for the smaller model**: both *measurable* cross-source floor conflicts were detected unaided — Aphrodite parentage (5 sources) and Achilles death (4 sources) → **2/2** (Io remains structurally single-source, always hand-added, per B7's definition). The 7,429 raw `variant_claims` candidates (more rows than relationships) is the pre-review pile B5 must work through — the "claims need more work" concern lives there (review-burden / noise), not in floor-conflict recall. No schema/DB impact; re-extraction on any model just regenerates candidate JSON. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-14 |

### DEV-040 — Track B3/C: V10 seeds the full reviewed entity set (~1,968 rows, not ~60-100); `entities.subtype` added as a first-class column; V10-V12 generated by a new `ingestion/seedgen` package

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 4 (Track B3 spot-check + Track C2-C4: `V9_1`, `V10`-`V12`) — `core-api/src/main/resources/db/migration/{V9_1,V10,V11,V12}__*.sql`, `core-api/.../domain/entity/EntityRecord.kt`, new `ingestion/seedgen/` package + `ingestion/tests/test_{sql_literals,migration_writer,entities_gen,canonical_edge,relationships_gen,variant_claims_gen}.py` |
| **Original Plan** | `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s `V10` row and `TODO-stage4.md` C2 estimate ~60-100 curated entities (7 primordials, 12 titans, 13 olympians, 9 heroes, ~10 monsters/key mortals) for V10, hand-merged from spot-checked candidates. `entities.type`'s extraction was assumed to already conform to the 8-value CHECK enum (A1's `ExtractedEntity.type` docstring: "must match entities.type CHECK values"); no `subtype` column was planned. |
| **What Changed** | **(1) B3 spot-check finding**: `entities_candidates.json`'s `type` field did not conform to the CHECK enum in practice — 115 distinct free-text strings (`god`, `goddess`, `deity`, `Nereid`, `place`, `warrior`, etc.), zero exact matches for `primordial`/`olympian`/`other_god`. The user manually reviewed and reclassified all 2,594 raw candidates by hand into `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` (1,968 rows survive — ~450 place/animal/author/etc. rows dropped as non-agents), every row's `type` now valid against the CHECK constraint, zero duplicate names, plus a new `subtype` field (138/1,968 rows) preserving the fine-grained label (`Nereid`, `river god`) the type collapse would otherwise discard. **(2) Scope decision**: rather than curate this reviewed set further down to the plan's ~60-100 estimate, `V10` seeds the **full 1,968-row confirmed set** — chosen because it keeps 6,093/7,406 relationship candidates and 6,905/7,429 variant_claim candidates resolvable (both endpoints/subject present) versus discarding ~90%+ of already-reviewed extraction work under the smaller estimate; the plan's `Done when: entities >= 60` criterion is a floor, not a cap, so this doesn't violate it. **(3) Schema addition**: `subtype` promoted to a real column (`V9_1__add_entities_subtype.sql`, `ALTER TABLE entities ADD COLUMN subtype TEXT`, following the `V8_1`-`V8_4` additive-migration precedent) rather than folded into the existing (also-sparse) `domain` column, per explicit user decision — keeps the two concepts (sphere-of-influence vs. fine-grained kind) distinct. `EntityRecord.kt` gained a matching `subtype: String? = null` field. **(4) Generator**: given the scale (1,968 / ~6,000+ / low-thousands rows, each needing FK-safe `INSERT...SELECT` subqueries), `V10`-`V12` are generated, not hand-written, by a new `ingestion/seedgen/` package (`sql_literals.py`, `migration_writer.py`, `entities_gen.py`, `canonical_edge.py`, `relationships_gen.py`, `variant_claims_gen.py`, `__main__.py`; run via `python -m seedgen`). Key design points: (a) `entity_fk()`/`sql_literal()` use `psycopg2.extensions.adapt()` for escaping rather than hand-rolled quote-doubling — a real bug was caught and fixed here (adapt's `QuotedString.getquoted()` defaults to Latin-1 encoding without a live connection, silently mojibake-ing any non-ASCII entity name; fixed by setting `.encoding = "utf-8"` explicitly before decoding, with a regression test); (b) V11's contested-relationship collapse (`canonical_edge.py`) groups on the same direction-aware subject convention as `extraction.conflict_detector._RELATION_TO_CLAIM` (imported, not re-derived) — `parent_of`'s subject is the child (to_name), since parents are what vary across sources — so real cross-source contradictions (e.g. a child with 3 competing named parents across 2 sources) are actually caught, unlike a naive from_name-keyed grouping; ties within a group prefer the highest-priority spine source (`apollodorus-bibliotheca` > `hesiod-theogony` > `homer-iliad`), falling back to most-corroborated-then-alphabetical when no spine source claims the group; relation types outside `parent_of`/`married_to`/`killed_by` (no `claim_type` mapping exists) pass through as literal edges with exact-duplicate collapse only, mirroring `conflict_detector.py`'s own scope; (c) V12 only ever emits `trust_tier=1` rows and re-normalizes `claim_type` against a freshly-loaded `claim_type_aliases` snapshot at generation time (not the value baked into the candidate JSON), plus non-blocking diagnostic warnings for missing floor conflicts (`--strict` flag to hard-fail) and near-duplicate `claim_type` strings lacking alias coverage (e.g. `notable_claim`/`notable claim`/`notable_deed` — flagged, not auto-fixed). |
| **Reason** | (1) is a real extraction-prompt gap the B3 spot-check step was designed to catch, exactly as intended. (2) trades a larger, less-hand-curated `entities` table for preserving ~90% more already-reviewed relationship/conflict data — judged the better trade given `variant_claims` depth (not `entities` table size) is what the eval gold questions and product concept actually reward. (3) keeps two genuinely different concepts (broad sphere vs. fine kind) in separate columns rather than overloading one. (4) hand-writing ~1,968 + ~2,655 + N `INSERT` statements with correct FK-subquery resolution and contested-edge dedup is impractical and error-prone at this scale; a tested generator makes the seed data reproducible from the reviewed JSON and re-runnable if review output changes (before first apply — see the idempotency caveat in `seedgen/__main__.py`'s docstring). |
| **Impact** | New: `V9_1__add_entities_subtype.sql`, `V10__seed_entities.sql` (1,968 rows), `V11__seed_relationships.sql` (2,655 rows, collapsed from 7,406 candidates — verified live: zero children have >1 distinct `parent_of` parent, zero spouses have >1 distinct `married_to` partner, zero victims have >1 distinct `killed_by` killer, confirming no `WITH RECURSIVE` branching risk), `V12__seed_variant_claims.sql` (0 rows today — B5 review hasn't run yet; expected, not a bug). `EntityRecord.kt` gained `subtype`. `ingestion/seedgen/` package + 44 new tests (162/162 ingestion suite passing). Verified two ways: (a) all 18 migrations applied cleanly in true Flyway semantic order against a scratch Postgres database via manual `psql` (no Java runtime available in the agent's environment at the time); (b) subsequently confirmed for real via the user's own `./gradlew :core-api:test --tests "*.FlywayMigrationTest"` run (7/7 passing) once Java became available — see DEV-041 for a real regression that same full-suite run surfaced. `TODO-stage4.md` C1-C2 checked off; C3/C4 remain open pending B4 spot-check confirmation and B5/B6 review (V12 will stay empty until B5 promotes real rows to `trust_tier=1`). Root-level scratch files from the manual B3 pass (`entities_candidates_fixed.json` etc.) are untracked and unaffected by this entry. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-15 |

### DEV-041 — `SchemaIntrospector`'s vocabulary-column query was alphabetical-with-limit; V11's real relation-value diversity silently dropped `parent_of` from the text-to-SQL prompt

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 4 (follow-up to DEV-040, caught by `./gradlew :core-api:test` once Java became available) — `core-api/src/main/kotlin/com/blamezeus/coreapi/config/SchemaIntrospector.kt` |
| **Original Plan** | `SchemaIntrospector.vocabularies()` (built pre-Stage-4 seed data) queries `SELECT DISTINCT <column> FROM <table> ORDER BY 1 LIMIT 50` for `relationships.relation` and `variant_claims.claim_type`, on the assumption (per A1's `ExtractedRelationship.relation` docstring: "parent_of, married_to, killed_by") that the vocabulary was small — alphabetical order with a limit was never expected to matter. |
| **What Changed** | DEV-040's `relationships_gen.py` (by design, matching `conflict_detector.py`'s own scope) inserts relation types outside the 3-type map as literal edges with no collapsing. Real extraction output turned out to contain **131 distinct `relation` strings** in the seeded `V11` (a long tail of one-off free-text labels like `abandoned`, `abductor_of`, `gave_scepter_to`), even though `parent_of` (1,284 rows), `killed_by` (495), and `married_to` (305) remain overwhelmingly dominant by frequency. Alphabetically, `parent_of` ranks 86th of 131 — past the `LIMIT 50` cutoff — so the live `SchemaIntrospectorTest > prompt emits live distinct values for vocabulary columns` test failed: the text-to-SQL prompt's `relation values:` hint no longer contained `'parent_of'` at all, replaced by 50 alphabetically-early rare values. Fixed by changing the query to `GROUP BY <column> ORDER BY count(*) DESC LIMIT 50` — frequency order instead of alphabetical, so the load-bearing canonical values surface within any limit regardless of how much rare free-text vocabulary extraction produces. |
| **Reason** | The alphabetical-with-limit query was a latent assumption that held only while the vocabulary was small; it was never stress-tested against real extraction-scale data until DEV-040 seeded the full un-collapsed relation vocabulary. Frequency ordering is the more correct general behavior for a "show the model example values" prompt hint regardless of vocabulary size, and doesn't require re-litigating DEV-040's decision to keep the long-tail relation types (which are genuine, if rare, narrative data — e.g. `gave_scepter_to`, `companion_of` — not noise to discard). |
| **Impact** | `SchemaIntrospector.kt`'s `vocabularies()` query only. Full `core-api:test` suite (27 tests) passes after the fix; this was caught specifically because `SchemaIntrospectorTest`'s `@Transactional` test asserts on **live** DISTINCT values against the real Testcontainers-seeded schema (V1-V12), not a mock — exactly the kind of regression that test is designed to catch. No impact on `ingestion/seedgen` or the generated migration files themselves; this was purely a `core-api` prompt-construction bug exposed by real seed-data volume. Gold questions Q6/Q7/Q9 (all depend on the LLM correctly using `parent_of` for lineage `WITH RECURSIVE` queries) were the concrete risk this fix closes. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-15 |

### DEV-042 — B5 review started minimal-scope-first (3 floor-conflict groups only, not all 841); two misclassified rows caught; `'birth'` claim_type gap found and closed; Track G repository tests written

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 4 (Track B5/B6 partial completion + Track G2/G3) — `ingestion/notebooks/02_verify_conflicts.ipynb`, `ingestion/extraction/output/{entities_candidates_confirmed_v1,variant_claims_candidates}.json`, `core-api/src/main/resources/db/migration/V9_2__add_birth_claim_type_alias.sql`, `core-api/.../repository/{EntityRecordRepositoryTest,VariantClaimRepositoryTest}.kt` |
| **Original Plan** | B5 calls for reviewing "every row" in `variant_claims_candidates.json` (841 distinct `(subject, claim_type)` groups) before any promotion; Track G2/G3 repository tests were still unwritten placeholders. |
| **What Changed** | **(1) Deliberate scope-narrowing**: rather than complete the full 841-group review before moving on, the user reviewed only the 3 floor-conflict groups (Aphrodite parentage, Io parentage, Achilles death — 73 rows) to unblock downstream implementation now, deferring the remaining ~838 groups. Explicit, acknowledged deviation from B5's literal "every row," accepted because it still satisfies B6 and the stage's own `Done when` bar. **(2) Two rows caught and demoted** during a spot-check of the bulk-approved groups: Aphrodite "child of Otreus"/"claims to be daughter of Otreus... (a false claim made to Anchises)" (`hesiod-homeric-hymns`) — the extractor's own claim_value text names this as the *lie* Aphrodite tells while disguised as a mortal seducing Anchises in the Homeric Hymn, not a competing parentage tradition; and a `ovid-metamorphoses` row conflating a Venus-speaking-about-Ino passage into an implied "Neptune is her kin" parentage claim, which no actual tradition supports. Both demoted back to `trust_tier=3`. **(3) `'birth'` claim_type gap**: the canonical Hesiod *Theogony* sea-foam origin story for Aphrodite (`docs/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s own worked V12 example, and what gold Q13's `required_authors: [Hesiod, Homer]` checks for) was extracted under `claim_type='birth'`, not `'parentage'` — a label with no `claim_type_aliases` row, so it never joined the `parentage` group the user was reviewing and was invisible during that pass. Found by spot-checking why `hesiod-theogony` never appeared among Aphrodite's promoted sources despite being the textbook counter-example. Fixed by adding `V9_2__add_birth_claim_type_alias.sql` (`'birth' -> 'parentage'`, following DEV-022's standing "append via follow-up migration" pattern) after confirming `'birth'` is a small (8 rows across 4 subjects: Dionysus, Adonis, Aphrodite, Athena), semantically coherent unusual-origin-story bucket, not overloaded with unrelated content — then promoting only Aphrodite's sea-foam row (not the co-located "born in Cyprus, called Cytherea" row, which is a birthplace/epithet detail, not a parentage claim) to `trust_tier=1`. **(4) `Io` missing from V10 entirely**: discovered `--strict` regeneration failed on the Io floor conflict despite 10 Io rows being promoted, because `Io` itself never made it into `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` — her raw extraction type was the ambiguous compound `"mortal/nymph"`, which fell through the B3 reclassification pass. Added back with `type='mortal'` (she is the mortal daughter of the river-god Inachus prior to her transformation). **(5) Track G2/G3 written**: `EntityRecordRepositoryTest.kt` (findAll >=60, case-insensitive name lookup) and `VariantClaimRepositoryTest.kt` (the stage's literal `Done when` bar — Aphrodite/Io/Achilles each have >=2 distinct promoted claim_values, `trust_tier=1` on every seeded row) against the real Testcontainers-seeded `V10`/`V12`, no fixture seeding needed. |
| **Reason** | (1) is a pragmatic, explicitly-acknowledged trade — implementation work elsewhere doesn't need to wait on exhaustive review of all 841 groups when the 3 mandatory floor conflicts are the hard product requirement. (2)-(4) are exactly what B5's per-row review step and the `--strict` regeneration gate are *for* — catching misattribution and pipeline gaps before they become permanent seed data — validated by actually exercising them against real data rather than trusting a clean-looking row count. (5) closes the stage's own completion criterion with tests that exercise the real reviewed data, not a mock. |
| **Impact** | `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json`: 1,969 rows (Io added). `variant_claims_candidates.json`: 71 rows promoted to `trust_tier=1` after the 2 demotions and 1 new promotion (net: 73 - 3 + 1 = 71). New `V9_2__add_birth_claim_type_alias.sql`, applied to the running dev DB directly (`docker exec ... psql`) so `seedgen`'s live `claim_type_aliases` read picks it up. Regenerated `V10` (1,969 entities), `V11` (2,658 relationships), `V12` (44 variant_claims) via `python -m seedgen --strict` — clean exit, no missing-floor-conflict warnings. Full verification: `./gradlew :core-api:test` 34/34 passing (27 pre-existing + 7 new Track G2/G3 tests) against the real Testcontainers-seeded schema through `V12`; `ingestion` suite 162/162 passing. `TODO-stage4.md` Track G2/G3 checkboxes now closeable; B5 remains explicitly partial (3/841 groups) — full review deferred, not abandoned. |

### DEV-043 — B4 spot-check found spelling-variant entities fragmenting relationship data (Cronos/Cronus, Athene/Athena, Ocean/Oceanus each seeded as two separate entity rows); `entity_aliases` alone cannot fix already-split data

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 4 (Track B4 spot-check finding, deeper than B4's own scope) — `ingestion/extraction/known_aliases.json`, `ingestion/extraction/output/{entities_candidates_confirmed_v1,relationships_candidates_cleaned,relationships_flagged_for_review,variant_claims_candidates}.json`, `ingestion/seedgen/__main__.py` |
| **Original Plan** | B4 is a light spot-check of `relationships_candidates.json` ("sanity-check a handful of rows"); `known_aliases.json` entries feed both A3's extraction-time entity resolution and V14's hand-curated `entity_aliases` runtime lookup table (per its header comment). No process step re-validates already-generated `entities`/`relationships` candidate data against newly-discovered aliases. |
| **What Changed** | While spot-checking, the user found three spelling variants the extraction pipeline never merged: `Cronos`/`Cronus`, `Athene`/`Athena`, `Ocean`/`Oceanus` — each pair seeded as **two separate `entities` rows** in the already-generated `V10__seed_entities.sql` (e.g. `Athena` typed `olympian`, `Athene` typed `other_god`, with 18 of "Athena's" relationship rows attached to the `Athene` id instead — invisible to any query resolving by the canonical `Athena` id). The user correctly diagnosed that adding these three pairs to `known_aliases.json` (done first) does **not** fix this: that file only affects (a) a *future* extraction re-run's in-memory dedup, and (b) V14's *runtime* alias lookup, which helps a user's query resolve "Athene" → the Athena entity but does nothing to reunite data already split across two distinct entity IDs at seed time. Checked all of `known_aliases.json`'s other ~25 pairs against the confirmed entity set — only these 3 have a duplicate-row problem; the rest (Jupiter/Zeus, Hercules/Heracles, etc.) are Roman/Greek pairs where only one form was ever extracted as an entity, so no fragmentation exists there. Fix applied at the candidate-JSON layer, before `V10`-`V12` generation: (1) dropped the 3 minority-spelling rows from `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` (keeping whichever row had the richer data — `Cronus` already had `generation=1` set, `Cronos` didn't); (2) remapped `from_name`/`to_name` in `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (140 rows: 76 Cronos, 18 Athene, 46 Ocean) and in the deferred `relationships_flagged_for_review.json` (8 rows, plus its `entities` summary field) to the canonical spelling; (3) remapped `subject_name` in `variant_claims_candidates.json` (46 rows, none yet promoted to `trust_tier=1`, so no runtime-visible impact but fixed before it could bite a future B5 promotion). Also updated `seedgen/__main__.py`'s default `--relationships-file` from the now-nonexistent `relationships_candidates.json` to `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` — the user's B4 pass split the original file into `_raw`/`_cleaned`/`_flagged_for_review` variants (6,026 of 7,406 rows kept; 203 held out for later manual review per an ambiguous-direction check), so the generator needed to point at the post-spot-check file going forward. |
| **Reason** | `entity_aliases` (V14) and `known_aliases.json` solve query-time/extraction-time name resolution; they were never designed to reconcile data already partitioned across two primary-key rows by an earlier extraction pass. The only correct fix for already-fragmented seed data is to merge at the source (candidate JSON) before the FK-resolving generator runs again — exactly what `seedgen`'s name-based `entity_fk()` resolution makes possible: once both files agree on one spelling, regeneration naturally consolidates every edge onto one entity id, no special-case merge logic needed in the generator itself. |
| **Impact** | `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json`: 1,966 rows (3 dropped). Regenerated via `python -m seedgen --strict`: `V10` 1,966 entities, `V11` 2,496 relationships (down from 2,658 -- reflects both the merge and B4's 1,380-row cleanup, `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` has 6,026 candidates vs the prior 7,406 raw), `V12` unchanged at 44 (none of the demoted names were promoted). Verified directly in the generated SQL: zero remaining references to `Cronos`/`Athene`/`Ocean` in `V11`; all edges now attach to the canonical entity (`Athena` 31 refs, `Cronus` 25, `Oceanus` 50). Full re-verification: `./gradlew :core-api:test` 34/34 passing, `ingestion` suite 162/162 passing. B4 itself remains open in `TODO-stage4.md` (203 rows still held in `relationships_flagged_for_review.json` for later manual review) but its spot-check has already paid for itself by catching this. Worth a broader pass later: this was found by checking `known_aliases.json`'s existing pairs, not an exhaustive fuzzy-duplicate scan of the full ~1,966-entity set — other undiscovered spelling variants may still exist outside that list (done as a triage scan in DEV-044). |
| **Date** | 2026-07-15 |

### DEV-044 — extraction candidate JSON files moved under source control; fuzzy-duplicate triage scan saved for later review

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 4 (review-workflow / tooling) — `.gitignore`, `ingestion/extraction/output/*.json`, new `ingestion/extraction/output/entities_fuzzy_duplicates_flagged_for_review.json` |
| **Original Plan** | `.gitignore` blanket-excluded `ingestion/extraction/output/` (DEV-036: "generated, unreviewed candidates — Track B promotes rows out of it, nothing in it is committed"), on the original ADR-004 model where the candidate JSON was a throwaway extraction artifact and only the promoted rows (in `V10`-`V12`) were durable. |
| **What Changed** | **(1) JSON files now tracked** (committed `46a8a18`): B3-B6 review turned out to edit these files directly and substantially — the hand-reclassified `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json`, B4's `relationships_candidates_{cleaned,flagged_for_review}.json` split, per-row `trust_tier` promotions in `variant_claims_candidates.json`, the DEV-043 spelling-variant merges — so "nothing in it is committed" no longer held: that review work is real, effortful, and was living only on the user's disk with no history. `.gitignore` narrowed from excluding the whole directory to excluding just `.checkpoint.jsonl` (3.5 MB mechanical re-run cache) and `.DS_Store`; all 11 candidate/review JSON files are now tracked so review edits get git history and diffs. **(2) Fuzzy-duplicate triage scan**: ran the `rapidfuzz` name-similarity check (threshold 88, the same one A3's `entity_resolver` uses) over the current 1,966-entity confirmed set — the broader pass DEV-043 flagged as still-owed — annotated each of the 29 candidate pairs with both entities' type and downstream reference counts (relationships + variant_claims), ranked by a triage heuristic (same-type + both-referenced + score), and saved to `entities_fuzzy_duplicates_flagged_for_review.json` (naming mirrors `relationships_flagged_for_review.json`). Explicitly **not** a diagnosis: unlike DEV-043's three pairs (confirmable against `known_aliases.json`), most of these are ambiguous — genuinely-distinct minor figures with coincidentally similar Greek names (e.g. `Atreus`/`Catreus`) look identical to real duplicates to the heuristic, so each pair still needs a source-text check. `Eileithyia`/`Ilithyia` is the one high-confidence duplicate (alternate transliterations of the same childbirth goddess); the rest are triage-ordered leads, not confirmed bugs. No entities were merged from this scan — results saved for a later manual pass, no seed regeneration. |
| **Reason** | (1) The candidate JSON stopped being throwaway the moment human review began writing durable decisions into it; version-controlling it is just matching the tracking policy to how the files are actually used, and gives the multi-session B4/B5 review an audit trail. (2) Closing DEV-043's open "broader pass" item, but honestly scoped — an automated similarity scan can *rank* suspects, it can't *decide* duplicates the way a `known_aliases.json` cross-check could, so it's filed as review input rather than acted on. |
| **Impact** | `.gitignore` narrowed (committed `46a8a18` along with the 11 now-tracked JSON files, +212k lines — mostly the large candidate files). New untracked `entities_fuzzy_duplicates_flagged_for_review.json` (29 ranked pairs) awaiting a later review pass alongside B4's 203 flagged relationships and B5's ~838 remaining conflict groups. No code, schema, migration, or seed-data change; no test impact. `CLAUDE.md` gitignore/tracking behavior of `ingestion/extraction/output/` is now the narrowed form. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-15 |

### DEV-045 — C5 (V13 myths) required three hero/monster entities missing from the seed (Perseus, Medusa, Eris); hand-added to the confirmed set and V10 regenerated, mirroring DEV-042's Io fix

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 4 (Track C5/C6 + C-verify) — `ingestion/extraction/output/entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json`, `core-api/src/main/resources/db/migration/{V10__seed_entities,V13__seed_myths,V14__create_entity_aliases}.sql`, `core-api/.../FlywayMigrationTest.kt` |
| **Original Plan** | C5 seeds five canonical myths (Judgment of Paris, Abduction of Persephone, Perseus and Medusa, Polyphemus episode, Arachne) with a `myth_participants` row per participant, "depends on C2 (entity names)" — the standing assumption being that C2's confirmed entity set already contains every figure a canonical myth needs. |
| **What Changed** | Writing V13 surfaced that three participants central to the *required* myths were absent from the confirmed entity set (and therefore V10): **Perseus** the hero (only `Perseus son of Nestor`, an unrelated Homeric minor character, and his mother `Danae` existed), **Medusa** the Gorgon (only a generic `Gorgo` and `Graeae`), and **Eris** goddess of discord (absent entirely). Without them, "Perseus and Medusa" and "The Judgment of Paris" could not have sensible participant rows. Fixed the same way DEV-042 hand-added Io: appended the three to `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` (`Perseus` type `hero`; `Medusa` type `monster`, subtype `Gorgon`; `Eris` type `other_god`, domain `strife/discord`) and regenerated `V10`-`V12` via `python -m seedgen --strict`. Confirmed first that **zero** relationship candidates reference these exact names, so the add is purely additive — no previously-dangling edges get reconnected. Then wrote **V13** (5 myths + 22 participants via `INSERT ... SELECT ... JOIN entities ON e.name = v.entity_name`, no `source_id` on `myths` per ADR-007) and **V14** (`entity_aliases` table + 27 aliases mirroring `known_aliases.json`, including the DEV-043 already-merged spelling variants `Cronos`/`Athene`/`Ocean` kept as runtime aliases so a user typing the minority spelling still resolves). Flipped `FlywayMigrationTest`'s `entity_aliases table does not exist yet` assertion to a positive table+columns check (C-verify). |
| **Reason** | `entity_aliases` and `known_aliases.json` resolve *names* at query/extraction time; they cannot conjure a missing *entity row* that a myth needs as a participant FK. The only correct fix is to add the entity at the source (candidate JSON) and regenerate — exactly DEV-042's Io precedent — keeping V10 the single generated source of entity rows rather than splitting entity seeding into V13. Adding as canonical-named rows (not aliases) is right because these are distinct mythological figures, not spelling variants of existing entities. |
| **Impact** | `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json`: **1,969** rows (Perseus/Medusa/Eris added to the post-DEV-043 1,966). Regenerated via `python -m seedgen --strict` — clean exit, no missing-floor-conflict warnings: `V10` **1,969** entities, `V11` **2,496** relationships (unchanged — no candidate edges referenced the new names), `V12` **44** variant_claims (unchanged — none promoted). New `V13__seed_myths.sql` (5 myths, 22 participants) and `V14__create_entity_aliases.sql` (27 aliases). Full verification: `./gradlew :core-api:test` **34/34 passing** against the real Testcontainers-seeded schema through `V14`, including the flipped C-verify assertion. Note on the dev DB: contrary to an earlier assumption here, the running dev DB was still only at **V8_4** via Flyway (V9+ had never been applied through Flyway — only the `birth` alias row had been hand-inserted into `claim_type_aliases`, which V9_2's `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` absorbs). So there was **no checksum drift and no reset needed**: Track H's `bootRun` applied all 8 pending migrations (V9, V9_1, V9_2, V10–V14) cleanly forward — "Successfully applied 8 migrations … now at version v14", all `success=t` — preserving the 3,524 paid `narrative_chunks` embeddings. Testcontainers (fresh DB per run) verified the same schema independently. Supersedes the entity-count figures in `TODO-stage4.md` B3/C2 (now 1,969). C5 remains extraction-preferred in spirit; these three were genuine seed gaps, not a curation choice. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-15 |

## Stage 5 — SQL Pipeline (2026-07-15)

### DEV-046 — Track 0 spike: `ChatLanguageModel` → `ChatModel`; multi-bean `@AiService` wiring is `wiringMode = EXPLICIT` + bean-name string, not Spring `@Qualifier`

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | 5 (Track 0 spike, before any production code) |
| **Original Plan** | `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §5`'s `LangChain4jConfig` snippet: `@Bean @Qualifier("routingModel") fun routingModel(): ChatLanguageModel = OpenAiChatModel.builder()...`, with the comment "To swap the chat provider: replace OpenAiChatModel with another LangChain4j `ChatLanguageModel`." The implicit assumption was that a Spring `@Qualifier` on the `@Bean` method is what routes each `@AiService` interface to the correct chat bean when two beans of the same type exist. |
| **What Changed** | Inspected the actual pinned jars (`langchain4j-spring-boot-starter:1.0.0-beta5` sources + transitively-resolved `langchain4j:1.0.0`/`langchain4j-core:1.0.0` GA sources, `langchain4j-anthropic:1.0.0-beta5` sources) rather than relying on possibly-stale docs, per DEV-004. Two findings: **(1)** the model interface implemented by `AnthropicChatModel` (and referenced throughout `AiServicesAutoConfig`/`AiServiceFactory`) is `dev.langchain4j.model.chat.ChatModel`, not `ChatLanguageModel` — the type was renamed between the plan's reference docs and GA 1.0.0. **(2)** Spring `@Qualifier` has **no effect** on LangChain4j's own bean resolution. The `dev.langchain4j.service.spring.AiService` annotation (confirmed by reading `AiServicesAutoConfig.addBeanReference`) only wires a specific bean when `wiringMode = AiServiceWiringMode.EXPLICIT` is set *and* the interface's `@AiService(chatModel = "beanName")` attribute names the target **Spring bean name** (a plain string looked up via `RuntimeBeanReference`, matched against `@Bean` method name unless overridden) — not a `@Qualifier`-annotated injection point. With `wiringMode = AUTOMATIC` (the default) and more than one `ChatModel` bean in the context (Stage 5 always has two: `routingModel` temp 0.0, `synthesisModel` temp 0.3), `AiServicesAutoConfig` throws `IllegalConfigurationException` at startup ("Conflict: multiple beans of type ... found ... specify ... EXPLICIT"). Also confirmed as a side-finding: the beta5 pin only actually applies to the *Spring integration* artifacts (`langchain4j-spring-boot-starter`, `langchain4j-open-ai-spring-boot-starter`, `langchain4j-anthropic-spring-boot-starter`, `langchain4j-pgvector`, `langchain4j-http-client-spring-restclient`); each transitively pulls `langchain4j:1.0.0` / `langchain4j-core:1.0.0` (**GA**, not beta5) for the actual `@AiService`/`@V`/`@SystemMessage`/`@UserMessage`/`AiServices` builder machinery — so those specific annotation shapes match current GA docs exactly (`@V("name")` value-attribute form, `@SystemMessage(String[] value, delimiter, fromResource)` multi-line-array form) and needed no adaptation. Enum-return handling (`QueryRouter` → `RouteDecision`) is fully automatic via `EnumOutputParser`: format instructions ("You must answer strictly with one of these enums: ...") are auto-appended to the prompt and the response is parsed case-insensitively with bracket-stripping tolerance — no extra code required in the `QueryRouter` interface beyond the method signature. `langchain4j-anthropic-spring-boot-starter:1.0.0-beta5` was confirmed to resolve (POM fetched from Maven Central) at the same beta5 coordinate as the other Stage 5 starters, so Track A1 needs no version substitution. |
| **Reason** | Verifying against the actual jars rather than the plan's snippet (which predates GA and predates the two-chat-bean requirement) avoids two runtime failures that would only surface at `ApplicationReady`: a compile-time type mismatch if `ChatLanguageModel` were used (it no longer exists as the implemented interface on `AnthropicChatModel` in this dependency set) and an `IllegalConfigurationException` at startup if `@Qualifier` alone were trusted to disambiguate two `ChatModel` beans for LangChain4j's own (Spring-independent) bean-name-string resolution. |
| **Impact** | Track A2 (`LangChain4jConfig.kt`): declare `@Bean fun routingModel(): ChatModel` / `@Bean fun synthesisModel(): ChatModel` (method name = bean name; `@Qualifier` may still be added for human readability/other Spring consumers but is not load-bearing for LangChain4j). Track C3/C4: `QueryRouter` and `TextToSqlAgent` (both temp 0.0) both declare `@AiService(wiringMode = AiServiceWiringMode.EXPLICIT, chatModel = "routingModel")`; Stage 7's `ConflictSynthesizer` (temp 0.3, not built this stage) will declare `chatModel = "synthesisModel"`. No DB/schema impact. `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §5`'s config snippet is now stale on `ChatLanguageModel`/`@Qualifier`-only wiring — not edited (plan is authoritative/append-only per protocol) but superseded by this entry for any future stage's `@AiService` config work. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-15 |
