## Stage P2 — Debuggability + known-defect fixes (2026-07-23)

### DEV-064 — Debug surface: `QueryRequest.debug`/`QueryResponse.debug: DebugInfo?` via a plain-bean `ThreadLocal` `DebugCapture`, not a `@Scope("request")` proxy; `query_history` confirmed skipped per `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §3.5`

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2, Stage P2, Tracks A–D (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p2.md`). Implements `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §3.2`/§7's debug-surface sketch — this is its first *code* stage. |
| **Original Plan** | `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §3.2`/§7 sketch a `debug` request flag and a `DebugInfo` response field (probe subject/claimType, claim count, first-attempt + capped SQL rows, retrieved chunk refs, fallback/composer flags, draft answer) without pinning the exact mechanism by which `NarrativeChunkContentRetriever` — invoked deep inside LangChain4j's `retrievalAugmentor`, under `RagAgent.answer(...)`, with no constructor-arg/param path back to `QueryService` — reports its retrieved chunks back up to the response. §3.5 separately calls for `query_history` to be **skipped** for the PoC. |
| **What Changed** | **(1) The mechanism is a plain singleton `@Component DebugCapture`** wrapping a `ThreadLocal<MutableState>` (`service/DebugCapture.kt`) — explicitly **not** a `@Scope("request")` proxy, which would break every handler/retriever unit test's plain `SqlQueryHandler(...)`/`MixedQueryHandler(...)`/`NarrativeChunkContentRetriever(...)` constructor call (no web/proxy context in those tests). `SqlQueryHandler`, `MixedQueryHandler`, and `NarrativeChunkContentRetriever` each take `DebugCapture` as a constructor arg and only *write* to it (no behavior change, no reads); `QueryService.handle(question, debug = false)` is the single `finalize()` funnel — `debugCapture.reset()` at entry and in a `finally`, so the ThreadLocal never leaks across pooled request threads, with the snapshot attached only when `debug=true` (`response.copy(debug = ...)`, `@field:JsonInclude(NON_NULL)` on `QueryResponse.debug` so the wire contract is byte-for-byte unchanged when absent). **(2) `NarrativeChunkContentRetriever`'s `RETRIEVAL_SQL` gained `nc.id`** (previously unselected) so `DebugInfo.ChunkRef.id` is always populated, not left permanently null — the other alternative the plan sketch left open. **(3) `query_history` confirmed skipped, per plan** — no code for it exists or was added; this entry records that the decision in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §3.5` ((a) the eval runner already persists full responses incl. `DebugInfo` per run, (b) `zeus_app` is read-only by guardrail — a write would need a grant change, (c) no organic traffic yet) was carried through Stage P2 unchanged, not silently dropped. Revisit at Phase 5 if real web usage appears (`docs/TODO.md` Post-MVP Phase 2 → Stage P5 line). |
| **Reason** | (1) The LangChain4j retrieval boundary is a real constraint, not a design preference — nothing short of a thread-scoped side channel can carry state from inside `retrievalAugmentor` back to `QueryService` without either changing every `@AiService`/retriever signature or breaking existing constructor-based tests; the whole request pipeline is synchronous on one thread, so a `ThreadLocal` is sufficient and the `reset()`-in-`finally` pattern makes the correctness-critical leak risk explicit and unit-tested (`QueryServiceTest`'s sequential debug=true → debug=false no-bleed case). (2) A real `nc.id` is strictly more useful for debugging a specific retrieved row than a permanently-null placeholder, and the column was trivially available. (3) Recording the already-planned skip here (not just in the plan doc) keeps `DEVIATIONS.md` a complete audit trail of what Stage P2 did and did not build, per the CLAUDE.md protocol's re-read-before-starting rule. |
| **Impact** | New: `domain/dto/DebugInfo.kt`, `service/DebugCapture.kt`. Changed: `domain/dto/QueryRequest.kt` (+`debug: Boolean = false`), `domain/dto/QueryResponse.kt` (+`debug: DebugInfo? = null`), `handler/SqlQueryHandler.kt`, `handler/MixedQueryHandler.kt`, `ai/NarrativeChunkContentRetriever.kt` (+`DebugCapture` arg each; retriever's `RETRIEVAL_SQL`/`Row` gained `id`), `service/QueryService.kt` (`handle` signature, `finalize()` funnel, probe/fallback/draftAnswer/composerSucceeded capture points), `controller/QueryController.kt` (forwards `request.debug`). No `query_history` table/migration/code — confirmed absent, per plan. Live-verified end-to-end (`POST /api/v1/query {"debug": true}` against a running server): populated `DebugInfo` for both SQL and RAG routes including real `retrievedChunks[].id`; a request without `debug` omits the key entirely. `:core-api:test` green (25 test classes; new `DebugCaptureTest`, `QueryServiceTest`, `SqlQueryHandlerTest`/`MixedQueryHandlerTest`/`NarrativeChunkContentRetrieverTest` debug-capture cases, `QueryControllerIntegrationTest` debug-key-presence cases — all `@AiService` mocked, TDD). |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

---

### DEV-065 — `scripts/reseed-local.sh`: guarded, `--check`-able reseed of V10–V14 preserving `narrative_chunks`

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2, Stage P2, Track F (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p2.md`). Implements `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §3.3`/§8's reseed-script sketch. |
| **Original Plan** | §3.3/§8 call for a reseed script that re-applies `V10`–`V14` after a candidate-JSON fix (e.g. a Track I reversed-edge correction) without dropping `narrative_chunks`, and flag the "Flyway checksum trap" (regenerating an already-applied migration breaks `flyway validate` on any shared environment) as a real risk to guard against, without prescribing the guard's exact mechanism. |
| **What Changed** | `scripts/reseed-local.sh`, mirroring `run-local.sh`'s repo-root/`.env` bootstrap style: **(1)** preconditions — refuses if the app is already listening on `:8080`, `.env`/required creds are missing, or `POSTGRES_USER` resolves to the read-only `zeus_app` instead of the Flyway superuser. **(2)** `DROP TABLE entity_aliases;` (V14 is a bare `CREATE TABLE`, no `IF NOT EXISTS`) + `TRUNCATE myth_participants, variant_claims, relationships, myths, entities CASCADE;` (`narrative_chunks` deliberately absent — no entity FK) + `DELETE FROM flyway_schema_history WHERE version IN ('10','11','12','13','14','15','16')` (V15/V16 included so Flyway doesn't skip the re-apply below its recorded max version). **(3)** starts `core-api` in the background, waits for it to report started (or fails loudly with the boot log), then prints a row-count sanity check (`entities`/`relationships`/`variant_claims`/`narrative_chunks`) and a "reseed complete — embeddings preserved" confirmation, leaving the app running. **(4) Shared-environment guard**: refuses to run at all without `--local-only` or `ALLOW_RESEED=1`, printing the checksum-trap rationale on refusal. **(5)** `--check`/`--dry-run` prints the exact SQL/steps without executing or touching the DB. |
| **Reason** | Each precondition guards a real failure mode observed or anticipated in this stage: a live app mid-DDL would race Flyway; running as `zeus_app` would fail outright (no DDL grant) with a confusing error instead of a clear one; and the checksum trap is exactly what `§8` warns about — an explicit opt-in flag is cheap insurance against silently breaking `flyway validate` for a teammate or shared demo pointed at the same DB. `--check` makes the destructive path auditable before its first real use. |
| **Impact** | New: `scripts/reseed-local.sh`. Live-verified end-to-end against the local dev DB: `entities` (1969), `relationships` (2496), `variant_claims` (44), `myths` (5), `entity_aliases` (27) all came back exactly matching pre-reseed counts; `narrative_chunks` (3524) untouched throughout; all 22 `flyway_schema_history` rows reapplied; the restarted app served a live query correctly (confirming the `afterMigrate` re-grant to `zeus_app`). Cross-referenced from `ingestion/audit/README.md` (DEV-066). |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-066 — Cycle-detection check authored in P2 (`ingestion/audit/cycle_check.py`), ahead of its Phase 3 home as audit check A3

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2, Stage P2, Track G (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p2.md`). `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §3.4`/§4.1 describe the check conceptually and name it audit **A3**, owned by Phase 3's `python -m audit` runner; this stage builds it early because it's on the critical path for DEV-054's Q9/Q12 root cause (a `parent_of` cycle = a near-certain reversed-direction edge, not LLM SQL fragility) and has no dependency on the rest of Phase 3's audit tooling. |
| **Original Plan** | §3.4 specifies the DAG invariant and the root-cause-first operating principle (fix the data before considering any query-time code); §4.1 names the eventual location (`ingredient`/`audit` package, check id A3) without specifying the algorithm or CLI shape. |
| **What Changed** | New package `ingestion/audit/`, seeding what becomes Phase 3's `python -m audit`. **(1)** `find_cycles(edges, relations={"parent_of"})` — a pure DFS back-edge cycle detector over the directed graph, deduped by a rotation-invariant node-sequence signature; reports **one representative cycle per strongly-connected component** (not full elementary-cycle enumeration via e.g. Johnson's algorithm — left for Phase 3 if it turns out to matter), each cycle flagged `is_near_certain_reversed_edge` when it's a self-loop or 2-cycle. **(2)** two readers into the same `Edge` shape: `load_from_candidates(path)` over `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (the editable source of truth a fix actually lands in) and `load_from_db(dsn, connect=...)` over the live `relationships` table, read-only via the `zeus_app` runtime user under the same `statement_timeout` guardrail `core-api` runs under (`connect` injectable so this stays unit-testable with a stub connection, no live Postgres in tests — the `sql_check.py` precedent from `evaluation/runner/`). **(3)** CLI `python -m audit.cycle_check [--candidates PATH | --db] [--relation a,b] [--output PATH]` — human report (near-certain findings listed first) + machine-readable `findings.json`; exits non-zero on any cycle so a fix loop can gate on it; never mutates. **(4)** `ingestion/config.py` gained optional `POSTGRES_APP_USER`/`POSTGRES_APP_PASSWORD` (`.get()`, not the hard-required `os.environ[...]` the superuser vars use) for the `--db` reader. **(5)** README (`ingestion/audit/README.md`) documenting the DAG invariant, the candidate-JSON-layer fix workflow, and the Flyway-checksum-trap cross-reference shared with `reseed-local.sh` (DEV-065). |
| **Reason** | Track G was flagged in the P2 checklist as independent of Tracks A–F and on the critical path for Q9/Q12 — nothing about it depends on the Kotlin debug surface or the reseed script, so building it in parallel, now, unblocks Track I's diagnose step immediately rather than waiting for a Phase 3 stage that hasn't started. One-cycle-per-SCC (rather than full enumeration) is a deliberate MVP scope cut: it correctly flags every tangled region needing manual attention, which is what a first pass needs, without the complexity of an algorithm this codebase doesn't yet need. |
| **Impact** | New: `ingestion/audit/__init__.py`, `cycle_check.py`, `tests/test_cycle_check.py` (9 tests, pure fixtures — no DB, no network), `README.md`. Changed: `ingestion/config.py` (+2 optional vars). All 171 `ingestion/.venv` pytest tests pass (162 pre-existing + 9 new). Live-verified against real data (read-only, no mutation): `--candidates` against `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` found 107 `parent_of` cycles; `--db` against the actually-seeded local DB found 4, including a near-certain reversed edge (`Laertes` ⇄ `Odysseus`) — concretely locating DEV-054's Q9/Q12 root cause. **Fixing these is explicitly out of scope for this entry** — that is Track I's diagnose-then-fix loop (own DEV-067+ entries per edge corrected), not authored or applied here. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-067 — Track I3 (Rung 0 fix): dropped 17 extraction-error `Odysseus parent_of Laertes` candidate rows, closing the `Laertes ⇄ Odysseus` cycle DEV-066 found; incidentally restores a suppressed legitimate `Arcesilaus parent_of Laertes` variant claim

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2, Stage P2, Track I3 (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p2.md`) — Rung 0's mandatory data fix, applied at the candidate-JSON layer per `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §3.4`. |
| **Original Plan** | §3.4/DEV-066's diagnosis: `Laertes ⇄ Odysseus` was one of the 4 `parent_of` cycles surviving into the live seeded graph — flagged `is_near_certain_reversed_edge` (a 2-cycle). The checklist's fix rule: correct the offending edge(s) at the candidate-JSON layer, tie-broken by `source_id`/spine priority, then regenerate + reseed + re-check until clean. |
| **What Changed** | Verified against the actual corpus text (`ingestion/corpus/apollodorus_bibliotheca_frazer1921.txt`, passage `[E.3.12]`: *"Ulysses, son of Laertes and Anticlia"*) that `Laertes parent_of Odysseus` is correct and every `Odysseus parent_of Laertes` candidate row is an extraction error. There were **17** such rows, not 1 — spanning `apollodorus-bibliotheca` (`E.3.6-E.3.12`), `homer-iliad` (2 passage refs), and `homer-odyssey` (14 passage refs) — all removed outright from `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (no tie-break needed: no source actually attributes this direction, so this isn't a cross-source disagreement, it's uniformly wrong). Regenerated `V11__seed_relationships.sql` via `python -m seedgen --strict` (2496 → 2494 relationship rows) and reseeded via `scripts/reseed-local.sh --local-only`. **Side effect surfaced by removing the bad rows:** `Arcesilaus parent_of Laertes` (Ovid, `13.128-13.161` — a real, different mythological tradition naming Arcesius/Arcesilaus as Laertes's father) had been **losing** `canonical_edge.py`'s spine-priority contest to the wrong `Odysseus` value (since `apollodorus-bibliotheca` — the top-priority spine source — was among the 17 wrong rows), so it was being silently dropped from V11 despite being a legitimate, uncontested candidate. It now survives seeding correctly. `python -m audit.cycle_check --db` post-reseed: 4 cycles → 3 (the `Laertes ⇄ Odysseus` cycle is gone; the remaining 3 are DEV-068's entity-conflation class, deliberately not touched here). |
| **Reason** | This is exactly the class of bug `canonical_edge.py`'s existing per-subject contested-group resolution cannot see (each direction of a 2-cycle groups under a *different* subject — `to_name=Laertes` vs `to_name=Odysseus` — so neither group looks "contested" on its own), which is precisely why the offline cycle-detection check (DEV-066) exists rather than relying on the seedgen pipeline alone. Removing outright (not reversing into a duplicate) is correct because `Laertes parent_of Odysseus` already exists from 4 independent sources — reversing would just create a redundant row. |
| **Impact** | `ingestion/extraction/output/relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (6026 → 6009 rows, only the 17 targeted rows removed — verified via diff, no incidental encoding/formatting changes). `core-api/.../V11__seed_relationships.sql` regenerated (2496 → 2494 rows). Row-count sanity check post-reseed: `entities` 1969, `relationships` 2494, `variant_claims` 44, `narrative_chunks` 3524 (unchanged, embeddings preserved). `Q9`/`Q12` re-measurement against this fix happens in **I4**, not here. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-068 — Track I3: 3 remaining `parent_of` cycles are entity-conflation, not simple reversed edges — flagged for Stage P3, not fixed in P2

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2, Stage P2, Track I3. Per the checklist's own instruction: "If a cycle traces to a split/duplicated entity (the Io/DEV-042 class), note it for P3 (don't merge entities here) but fix the direction" — these 3 don't have a clean direction fix because the cycle isn't a reversed edge, it's two distinct mythological figures sharing one entity row. |
| **Original Plan** | DEV-066 found 4 live cycles; DEV-067 fixed the one that was a genuine reversed edge (`Laertes ⇄ Odysseus`). The other 3 remained undiagnosed pending source-text verification. |
| **What Changed** | Checked all 3 against `ingestion/corpus/apollodorus_bibliotheca_frazer1921.txt`: **(1) `Aeolus → Calydon → Protogenia → Aethlius → Endymion → Aeolus`** — traced to `[1.7.1]`–`[1.8.1]`. Not a reversed edge: "Aeolus" (Hellen's son, per `[1.7.3]`) is conflated with his great-great-grandson **"Aetolus"** (Endymion's actual son who founds Aetolia, per `[1.7.6]`) — a likely fuzzy-name-match extraction bug (similar spelling, four generations apart). "Calydon" (the DB edge's target) is likewise conflated with **"Calyce"** (Aeolus's actual daughter, per `[1.7.3]`/`[1.7.5]`) — Calydon is a different, later-generation figure (`[1.7.7]`: son of Aetolus and Pronoe). Two separate name-pairs, both real Apollodorus figures, merged into one entity row each. **(2) `Astyoche → Tros → Ilus → Laomedon → Astyoche`** and **(3) `Cecrops → Pandion → Erechtheus → Cecrops`** — not yet traced against source text; **(3)** especially fits the same conflation pattern on priors (Athenian myth has two Cecrops and two Pandions in most tellings — Cecrops I/Erechtheus's line vs. Cecrops II, per Apollodorus 3.14–3.15), but this is not yet source-verified the way (1) and DEV-067 were. |
| **Reason** | Per the checklist, entity merges/splits are Stage P3 work (`ingestion/audit/README.md`'s DAG-invariant fix rule only covers *edge direction*), and guessing a merge from partial evidence risks making the seed data worse, not better, for a product whose entire value proposition is source-attributed accuracy. The user directed (2026-07-23 conversation): fix the source-verified reversed edge now (DEV-067), explicitly flag these 3 for later analysis rather than force a same-session fix, with the eventual goal of full data reliability. |
| **Impact** | No data changed for these 3. Findings preserved in `ingestion/audit/findings-db.json` (committed) for Stage P3 to start from. `docs/TODO2.md` Stage P3 section annotated with this backlog (see the P3 bullet added there). `python -m audit.cycle_check --db` will continue to report exactly these 3 until P3's entity-merge work lands — this is **expected**, not a regression, and `docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p2.md`'s I3/I4 gates account for it (Q9/Q12 re-measured against the DAG as it stands after DEV-067, not a fully-clean one). |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-069 — Track I5 (Rung 1): bounded `WITH RECURSIVE` few-shot (`visited`-id array + depth cap, single-relation-direction warning) added to `TextToSqlAgent.generateSql`, shipped on evidence after Rung 0 alone left Q9/Q12 stable-fail

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2, Stage P2, Track I5 (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p2.md`) — the first (and, per this entry's evidence, only) conditional rung of `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §3.4`'s Q9/Q12 staircase to actually ship. |
| **Original Plan** | §3.4 Rung 1: "*If and only if* the reseeded eval still shows Q9/Q12 stable-fail or flaky, add a bounded `WITH RECURSIVE` few-shot (depth cap + `visited`-id array) to `TextToSqlAgent.generateSql`. It must leave a breadcrumb — the debug surface's `sqlRows` shows whether the visited-array truncation fired... TDD the prompt-shape/regeneration path with a mocked agent." I4's re-measurement (post-DEV-067, before this change) showed exactly that gate satisfied: Q9 and Q12 both still `stable-fail` (`serviceError=true`, all 3 runs each). A live repro caught the actual failing query in the server log: `ERROR: canceling statement due to statement timeout`, on a `WITH RECURSIVE` with **no depth cap, no visited-array**, and — a defect not explicitly named in §3.4 but empirically the more direct cause — mixing `r.relation IN ('parent_of', 'child_of')` in one join condition without inverting `child_of`'s (opposite) direction. |
| **What Changed** | `TextToSqlAgent.kt`'s `generateSql` system message gained: **(1)** an explicit dual-bound rule — a `visited` id array seeded at the anchor and extended each recursive step, excluded via `NOT next.id = ANY(current.visited)`, **plus** an independent `depth` counter capped (e.g. `depth < 20`); **(2)** a rule against combining `parent_of` and its inverse `child_of` in one join without inverting direction; **(3)** a new worked example ("Trace Zeus's lineage back to Chaos") demonstrating both bounds together on a single-relation-direction query, alongside the existing mandatory-attribution projection (DEV-057). The system message was extracted to a `companion object` `const val GENERATE_SQL_SYSTEM_MESSAGE` (previously inlined in the `@SystemMessage` annotation) purely so its content is unit-testable without a live LLM call — `TextToSqlAgentPromptTest.kt` (6 tests) asserts the bound rules, the direction warning, the worked example, and that pre-existing rules survived the edit untouched. One handler-level regression test added to `SqlQueryHandlerTest` proving the pipeline needs no changes to accommodate the new SQL shape. |
| **Reason** | The evidence (I4, post-Rung-0) satisfied Rung 1's gate exactly as specified. The `child_of`-mixing rule, while not literally named in §3.4's "depth cap + visited-id array" summary, is squarely within the staircase's "root cause first" spirit — it's the empirically-observed proximate cause (relation-label fragmentation the model was never warned about), and the new worked example fixes it by demonstration (single-relation-direction) as well as by explicit rule, at near-zero added scope. |
| **Impact** | `core-api/.../ai/TextToSqlAgent.kt` (prompt only; no method signature change). New: `TextToSqlAgentPromptTest.kt`. Changed: `SqlQueryHandlerTest.kt` (+1 test). `:core-api:test`: 26 classes green (25 + `TextToSqlAgentPromptTest`). **Live-verified via a real 3-run eval** (`results/2026-07-23T13-05-46Z__bc309ff__p2-rung1/`) against `results/2026-07-22T19-02-10Z__de6de91__baseline/`: **zero stable regressions**; **Q12 → stable-pass 3/3** (serviceError eliminated, route/author/content all pass — DEV-054's Q12 defect is fully resolved); **Q9's `serviceError` eliminated** (was 0/3 on every dimension, now 2/3 every run: route ✓, author ✓, content ✗) but its content point is **not** earned — confirmed via direct DB inspection to be a **separate, unrelated data-completeness gap**: `Sky`/`Ouranos` carries only a `married_to Earth` edge (no `parent_of Cronus`, likely lost a contested-group resolution the current single-canonical-parent-per-child `relationships` design can't avoid when a child genuinely has two parents), and `Chaos` has no edge to `Earth`/`Sky` at all. No code change fixes a structurally-absent edge — **this is flagged as a new P3 backlog item, not a Rung 2/3 trigger**, since Rungs 2–3 are gated on a *generation/execution* failure (malformed CTE, continued serviceError), and Q9 no longer exhibits either. **Per this evidence, the staircase stops at Rung 1 — Rungs 2 and 3 are not shipped.** Q13 reconfirmed stable-pass 3/3 (untouched). One incidental flaky flip noted in the diff (Q2, FACT, single-run keyword miss) — unrelated to this change (Q2 is RAG-routed, never touches `TextToSqlAgent`), natural LLM variance, correctly ignored by the gate (not a stable regression). |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-070 — Track A: `python -m audit` aggregate runner + the A2r check contract, registering `cycle_check` as check A3

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3, Track A (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`) — the foundational, do-first track: `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §4.1` names the eventual `python -m audit` runner and its five checks (A1–A5) without specifying the check-registration mechanism, findings/report shapes, or waiver format. Per the checklist's own Deviation Protocol note (lines 62–71), the runner + contract is logged as this reserved number regardless of how closely it follows §4.1, since it's new relative to the MVP `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`. |
| **Original Plan** | §4.1: "`ingestion/audit/` (`python -m audit`, read-only over candidate JSON + live DB; emits `ingestion/audit/reports/<date>.md` + machine-readable findings JSON)." The P3 checklist's A1r–A7r additionally specify: auto-discovery (no separate `register()` call), a `Finding`/`CheckResult` contract, `--candidates`/`--db`/`--only`/`--out` flags, a waiver mechanism requiring a written reason, and a human report with PASS/FINDINGS/WAIVED badges. |
| **What Changed** | **(1)** `ingestion/audit/contract.py` — the A2r contract: `Finding` (`check`, `severity`, `subject`, `detail`, `suggested_fix`, `waived`, `waiver_reason`, with a `.waive(reason)` that rejects an empty reason) and `CheckResult` (`findings`, `summary`) dataclasses, plus a documentation-only `Check` `Protocol`. **(2)** `ingestion/audit/__main__.py` — `discover_checks()` walks the package via `pkgutil.iter_modules`, importing any sibling module exposing `NAME` + a callable `run` (leading-underscore modules skipped; `contract`/`tests`/`__main__` excluded naturally since they don't expose both). `run_checks(checks, candidates_dir, db_conn, waivers)` is the pure aggregation core (no discovery, no live DB — fixture-testable), applying waivers by exact `(check, subject)` match. `write_findings_json` / `write_report_md` emit the two artifacts. CLI flags: `--candidates` / `--db` (independent booleans; neither given = both, matching the checklist's "default both, mirroring cycle_check"), `--only CHECK`, `--candidates-dir`, `--out`, `--waivers`. One connection is opened once (via `_connect_db`, reusing `cycle_check._db_dsn` rather than re-deriving the DSN) and shared across every check, then closed in a `finally`. Exits non-zero iff any un-waived finding survives. **(3)** `cycle_check.py` gained the thin A2r adapter the checklist calls for: `NAME = "A3"` and `run(candidates_dir, db_conn) -> CheckResult`, which calls the **unedited** `find_cycles` core against whichever source(s) are given and reshapes each `Cycle` into a `Finding` (severity `error` for near-certain reversed edges, `warning` otherwise). `load_from_db`'s body was split into a new `_query_edges(conn)` helper (same SQL, same mapping) so the adapter can query an already-open, already-readonly connection the runner shares across checks, instead of opening a second connection per check — `load_from_db(dsn, connect)`'s own open/set-readonly/close contract is otherwise unchanged, and `find_cycles` was not touched. **(4)** `audit-waivers.json` mechanism: a list of `{"check", "subject", "reason"}`; `load_waivers` raises `ValueError` if any entry's `reason` is blank. **(5)** README updated with a `python -m audit` section (flags, artifacts, waiver mechanism) noting A1/A2/A4/A5 are still Tracks B–E, not yet built. **(6)** `tests/test_runner.py` (12 tests, pure fixtures via `types.SimpleNamespace` fakes — no live DB) covering aggregation, waiving, `--only`, unknown-check error code, artifact shape, and that `discover_checks()` finds the real `A3` adapter. |
| **Reason** | Auto-discovery via attribute-presence (rather than an explicit registry list) is what A1r's parenthetical explicitly asks for ("register into the runner" means *conform to the contract and be discoverable*, not a separate call) — it means Tracks B–E can add `duplicate_entities.py` / `drop_accounting.py` / `relation_taxonomy.py` / `integrity.py` later with zero runner-side changes. Sharing one DB connection across every check (rather than each check opening its own, as `cycle_check`'s standalone CLI still does) avoids N connections for N checks once B–E land, and reuses `_query_edges` instead of duplicating the SQL. Splitting `load_from_db`'s body into `_query_edges` is the "thin adapter... do not edit `find_cycles`" instruction taken literally — the pure cycle-detection algorithm is byte-for-byte untouched. |
| **Impact** | New: `ingestion/audit/contract.py`, `ingestion/audit/__main__.py`, `ingestion/audit/tests/test_runner.py`. Changed: `ingestion/audit/cycle_check.py` (+`NAME`, +`run`, +`_cycles_to_findings`, `load_from_db` body extracted into `_query_edges` — `find_cycles`/`Edge`/`Cycle` untouched), `ingestion/audit/README.md`. All 183 `ingestion/.venv` pytest tests pass (171 pre-existing + 12 new). Live-verified against the real corpus/DB: `python -m audit --candidates` → 106 `parent_of` cycles (candidate-JSON state as of this date); `python -m audit --db` → exactly the **3** known DEV-068 conflation cycles (Aeolus/Calydon, Astyoche/Tros, Cecrops/Pandion) with none newly introduced; default (no flags) runs both sources in one pass. Only check **A3** is registered — **A1/A2/A4/A5 are Tracks B–E**, not built in this entry; the P3 exit's "all five checks clean or waived" is not yet reachable until those land. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-071 — Track D: `relation_taxonomy.py`, audit check A4 — frequency-classifies `relation` labels into canonical/synonym/inverse/legit-long-tail, emits Track F's initial `relation_aliases` seed rows

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3, Track D (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`), built directly after Track A (this stage's foundational track) rather than in the checklist's illustrative B→C→D→E order — the parallelization guide itself calls out "start A, D, and F-code together" since D is on the critical path unblocking Track F's seed rows, so it was picked as the next track over B (A1 duplicate-entities) or C (A2 drop-accounting). Per the checklist's Deviation Protocol note (lines 62–71), the reserved indicative number for this track was **DEV-073** (assuming B/C landed first as DEV-071/072); since B/C are not yet built, this entry takes the actual next free sequential number, **DEV-071**, instead — no gap, and B/C will take whatever the next free numbers are when they land. |
| **Original Plan** | `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §4.1` A4: "frequency-classify all 131 labels into canonical / synonym / inverse / legit-long-tail → produces the initial `relation_aliases` map." ADR-019's Context section gives the worked examples (`son_of`/`child_of`/`daughter_of` → `parent_of` inverted; `killed` vs `killed_by`; `wife_of`/`wedded` → `marriage`) and Decision point 3 names this exact check as the source of the initial alias map. The checklist's D1–D4 additionally specify: a report table for human review (D2), seed rows formatted for Track F's V17 migration to paste directly (D3), and registration as a "reporting" check whose findings represent proposals, not defects (D4). |
| **What Changed** | **(1)** `ingestion/audit/relation_taxonomy.py` — pure core `classify_relations(counts, canonical_relations, alias_map) -> list[ClassifiedRelation]`, bucketing every observed label. `CANONICAL_RELATIONS = {parent_of, married_to, sibling_of, killed_by}` (the checklist's own named four — no new canonicals invented from frequency alone). `SYNONYM_ALIASES` is a deliberately **narrow** hardcoded map covering only ADR-019's explicit examples plus unambiguous gendered/same-direction variants actually observed in `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` today: `child_of`/`son_of`/`daughter_of` → `parent_of` (inverse=True, confirmed against `canonical_edge.py`'s documented convention that `parent_of`'s subject is the child/`to_name`, i.e. `from_id`=parent per DEV-047), `killed` → `killed_by` (inverse=True, confirmed against `extraction/conflict_detector.py`'s `_RELATION_TO_CLAIM` where `killed_by`'s `from_name` is the victim), `father_of`/`mother_of` → `parent_of` (synonym, same direction), `sister_of`/`brother_of`/`brother_relation_of` → `sibling_of` (synonym). `wife_of`/`wedded` (ADR-019's own married_to example) were **not** added — they don't occur in the live candidate data, and the classifier only buckets labels actually observed, not phantom ones. Deliberately **excluded** from the alias map: `grandfather_of`, `grandson_of`, `descendant_of`, `ancestor_of`, `uncle_of`, `aunt_of`, `father_in_law_of` — different-generation or different-kind relations that must not be conflated with `parent_of`/`sibling_of`, the same caution DEV-068 logged for entity conflation, applied here to relations. **(2)** `to_seed_rows` / `format_seed_rows_sql` (D3) — extracts only synonym+inverse-bucket rows as `(alias, canonical, inverse)` tuples and formats them as a pasteable `INSERT INTO relation_aliases ... VALUES` block for Track F's V17 migration. **(3)** Two readers mirroring `cycle_check.py`: `load_relation_counts_from_candidates(path)` and `load_relation_counts_from_db(conn)` (the latter takes an already-open connection, not a dsn+connect factory, matching the shared-connection pattern `python -m audit` established in DEV-070). **(4)** `NAME = "A4"` + `run(candidates_dir, db_conn) -> CheckResult` (A2r contract adapter) — findings are emitted **only** for synonym/inverse-bucket labels (the actionable seed-row proposals; canonical and legit-long-tail counts are folded into the summary line instead, avoiding a ~160-row no-op table for the long tail), severity `warning` for inverse (direction-swap risk) vs `info` for synonym (rename only). **(5)** A standalone `python -m audit.relation_taxonomy [--candidates PATH \| --db] [--output PATH]` CLI (mirroring `cycle_check`'s own standalone entrypoint) printing the full per-label table plus the proposed seed-row SQL and writing a machine-readable findings JSON. **(6)** `tests/test_relation_taxonomy.py` (14 tests) — TDD per D4's spec (`son_of`/`child_of` → inverse-of-`parent_of`, `killed` → inverse-of-`killed_by`, `gave_scepter_to` stays legit-long-tail with no alias row) plus the different-generation non-conflation guard, ordering, seed-row formatting, both readers, and the `run()` adapter for both sources. |
| **Reason** | A4/Track D was explicitly called out in the checklist's own parallelization guide as the second track to start (alongside F's code) because its output — the seed rows — unblocks Track F's V17 migration content; starting it now keeps that critical path moving instead of waiting on B/C (A1/A2), which have no such downstream dependency. Keeping `SYNONYM_ALIASES` narrow (only ADR-019's named examples + data-confirmed gendered variants) rather than exhaustively guessing at every plausible synonym in the 177-label tail follows the same review-gated caution the checklist itself demands (D2: "a human confirms the synonym/inverse assignments before they become alias rows") and the project's established precedent against guessing from partial evidence (DEV-068). Findings for synonym/inverse labels are expected to persist across audit runs until Track F lands and either the labels are consumed by generation-time normalization or a reviewer adds a waiver note — this is by design (D4: "it 'passes' once a human has reviewed and the rows are promoted"), not a bug in the check. |
| **Impact** | New: `ingestion/audit/relation_taxonomy.py`, `ingestion/audit/tests/test_relation_taxonomy.py`. All 197 `ingestion/.venv` pytest tests pass (183 pre-existing + 14 new). Live-verified against the real candidate data: 177 distinct labels (not the checklist's 131 — the corpus has grown since that count was taken; noted in `TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`, no mechanism change), classified as 4 canonical, 9 synonym/inverse (`child_of`, `son_of`, `killed`, `daughter_of`, `father_of`, `sister_of`, `mother_of`, `brother_of`, `brother_relation_of`), 164 legit-long-tail. `python -m audit --candidates` now runs both **A3** and **A4** (auto-discovered, zero runner changes needed — confirming DEV-070's discovery design). The 9 proposed seed rows are **not yet promoted** — Track F (V17 migration + `relation_normalizer.py` + `relationships_gen.py` wiring) is a separate, not-yet-built track that will consume `to_seed_rows`'/`format_seed_rows_sql`'s output. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-072 — Track F: `relation_aliases` V17 migration + `relation_normalizer.py` + `relationships_gen.py` wiring (ADR-019), seeded from Track D's 9 proposed rows; F6 (reseed + regenerate) deliberately deferred to Track I

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3, Track F (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`) — built directly after Track D, per the parallelization guide's "start A, D, and F-code together" note: F's code has no dependency beyond a stub map, and D (just landed as DEV-071) supplies the real seed rows F1's migration needed. |
| **Original Plan** | ADR-019 Decision points 1–3 and `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §4.2`/§7 DDL sketch specify the `relation_aliases(alias, canonical, inverse)` table, a `seedgen`-generation-time application (mirroring `variant_claims_gen.py`'s use of `claim_type_aliases`), and swapping `from_id`/`to_id` on `inverse=true` labels. The checklist's F1–F6 additionally specify: a schema comment, confirming `afterMigrate__grant_app_user.sql` covers the new table, a `relation_normalizer.py` sibling of `claim_type_normalizer.py`, wiring through the same live connection `seedgen/__main__.py` already opens for `claim_type_aliases`, applying normalization **before** `_filter_and_dedup`/`resolve_canonical_edges`, and — explicitly out of scope for this track — F6's `seedgen --strict` regeneration + reseed, which the checklist itself scopes to Track I ("part of Track I's loop, not a standalone reseed"). |
| **What Changed** | **(1)** `core-api/.../V17__create_relation_aliases.sql` — `relation_aliases(alias TEXT PRIMARY KEY, canonical TEXT NOT NULL, inverse BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE)` with a `COMMENT ON TABLE` (the V8_2/V8_3 convention) plus the **9 initial rows from Track D's `relation_taxonomy.py` output** (DEV-071): `child_of`/`son_of`/`daughter_of` → `parent_of` (inverse), `father_of`/`mother_of` → `parent_of` (synonym), `killed` → `killed_by` (inverse), `sister_of`/`brother_of`/`brother_relation_of` → `sibling_of` (synonym). Confirmed (not assumed) that `afterMigrate__grant_app_user.sql`'s `GRANT SELECT ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA public` is schema-wide and needs no edit to cover the new table. **(2)** `ingestion/extraction/relation_normalizer.py` — `load_relation_alias_map(conn) -> dict[str, tuple[str, bool]]` (`SELECT alias, canonical, inverse FROM relation_aliases`) and `normalize_relation(alias_map, relation) -> tuple[str, bool]` (identity `(relation, False)` on no match), mirroring `claim_type_normalizer.py` exactly but carrying the extra `inverse` bit the direction-sensitive `relation` column needs. **(3)** `seedgen/relationships_gen.py` gained `_apply_relation_aliases(relationships, relation_alias_map)`, called from `build_relationship_rows` **before** `_filter_and_dedup`/`resolve_canonical_edges` (ADR-019 Consequences: normalization first, so contested-edge grouping and dedup compare on canonical relation+direction) — on `inverse=True` it swaps `from_name`/`to_name`, preserving `source_id`/`passage_ref` and every other candidate field untouched. `build_relationship_rows`/`render` gained an optional 4th parameter `relation_alias_map` (default `None` → treated as `{}`, a no-op) — fully backward-compatible with existing 3-arg call sites; the pre-existing 3rd parameter was renamed `claim_type_alias_map` (from the generic `alias_map`) for clarity now that two distinct alias maps are in play, a rename that doesn't affect positional callers. **(4)** `seedgen/__main__.py` wired `load_relation_alias_map` alongside the existing `load_alias_map` call, over the same connection, passed into `build_relationship_rows`. **(5)** TDD: `tests/test_relation_normalizer.py` (5 tests) and 5 new cases added to `tests/test_relationships_gen.py` (inverse-swap, synonym-relabel, legit-long-tail passthrough, dedupe-collapses-across-former-synonyms, no-map-is-a-no-op) — all per F5's exact spec. **(6)** F6 (`seedgen --strict` regeneration of `V11__seed_relationships.sql` against the applied V17) is **not done here** — deferred to Track I, exactly as the checklist itself scopes it ("part of Track I's loop, not a standalone reseed"); V17 was instead syntax/semantics-verified via a `BEGIN; ...; ROLLBACK;` transaction against the live local DB (table created, 9 rows inserted and confirmed correct, then rolled back — zero lasting DB change). |
| **Reason** | Building F's code now (rather than waiting for Track I) follows the parallelization guide's explicit sequencing advice and keeps momentum on the critical path the checklist itself identifies (A → D/F-code → ... → I). Renaming the pre-existing `alias_map` parameter to `claim_type_alias_map` avoids the ambiguity of two same-named-in-spirit-but-different-shape alias maps (`dict[str,str]` for claim types vs `dict[str,tuple[str,bool]]` for relations) coexisting in the same function signature — a purely clarifying rename, verified safe because every existing caller (`seedgen/__main__.py`, `tests/test_relationships_gen.py`) passes that argument positionally. Deferring F6 (and the actual reseed) to Track I is not a scope cut but literal compliance with this checklist's own instruction — applying V17 to a real environment and regenerating V11 against it belongs in the single serialized `seedgen --strict → reseed-local.sh → python -m audit → eval → compare` gate (Track I), not as a side effect of landing the normalization code. |
| **Impact** | New: `core-api/.../V17__create_relation_aliases.sql`, `ingestion/extraction/relation_normalizer.py`, `ingestion/tests/test_relation_normalizer.py`. Changed: `ingestion/seedgen/relationships_gen.py` (+`_apply_relation_aliases`, `build_relationship_rows`/`render` signatures), `ingestion/seedgen/__main__.py` (+`load_relation_alias_map` wiring), `ingestion/tests/test_relationships_gen.py` (+5 tests). All 207 `ingestion/.venv` pytest tests pass (197 pre-existing + 5 `test_relation_normalizer` + 5 `test_relationships_gen`). Live-verified: the V17 migration applied cleanly inside a rolled-back transaction against the local dev DB (table created, exactly the 9 rows present with the expected `canonical`/`inverse` values, then rolled back — `to_regclass('relation_aliases')` confirmed `NULL` afterward, i.e. no lasting change). **Not yet done**: V17 is not applied to any real environment, `V11__seed_relationships.sql` has not been regenerated against it, and `SchemaIntrospector`'s live vocabulary has not shrunk yet — all of that is Track I's job (F6/G), which also needs Tracks B/C/E (A1/A2/A5) or an explicit waiver before the P3 exit's "all five checks clean or waived" is reachable. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-073 — Track B: `duplicate_entities.py`, audit check A1 — full-pairs fuzzy-duplicate scan over confirmed entity names, formalizing DEV-044's one-off triage scan; transliteration heuristic redesigned to keep masculine/feminine name endings in separate buckets after an initial over-trigger

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3, Track B (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`) — the next parallel check track after A/D/F, unblocking Track J1's 29-pair backlog triage. |
| **Original Plan** | `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §4.1` A1: "`rapidfuzz` full-pairs over V10 names + transliteration heuristics for the known bug class (K↔C, `-os`↔`-us`, `-e`↔`-a`, `Ou`↔`U` — the Cronos/Cronus, Athene/Athena, Ocean/Oceanus pattern, DEV-043). Cross-check `entity_aliases` + `known_aliases.json`; unaliased pairs → triage." The checklist's B1–B5 additionally specify a pure `find_duplicate_pairs(names, known_aliases) -> list[Pair]` core, registration into the runner, and a TDD spec expecting the ~29-pair count from DEV-044's original scan to be roughly reproduced. |
| **What Changed** | **(1)** `ingestion/audit/duplicate_entities.py` — pure core `find_duplicate_pairs(names, known_aliases, threshold=88.0)`, mirroring `extraction/entity_resolver.py`'s exact threshold (the same bar the extraction-time in-memory dedup already applies). A pair is flagged if either the raw lowercased-name `fuzz.ratio` clears 88 (DEV-044's original methodology) **or** — failing that — both names' transliteration-normalized keys are **exactly equal**. **(2)** The transliteration heuristic (`_translit_key`) went through one redesign during this track, caught by live-verifying against the real 1,969-entity set before finalizing (not caught by unit tests alone, which used small hand-picked fixtures): the first version collapsed `-os`/`-us` **and** `-e`/`-a` into one shared bucket (naively "strip any of these 4 suffixes"), which correctly unified the 3 real DEV-043 pairs but also matched masculine/feminine name pairs sharing a stem — a common, *legitimate* Greek-mythology naming pattern (e.g. `Acaste`/`Acastus`, likely a mother/son pair, not a spelling variant) — inflating the live scan from a plausible ~45 pairs to **150** extra transliteration-only hits, most of them false. A second version used a fuzzy (not exact) match on normalized keys, which still let short, aggressively-stripped stems collide coincidentally (e.g. `Creon`/`Cronus` — a king and a Titan — scored 88.9 after `Cronus` was stripped to `cron`), producing 66 extra hits including clearly-wrong pairs (`Hera`/`Herse`, `Dike`/`Dirce`). The **final** design keeps `-os`/`-us` (masculine) and `-e`/`-a` (feminine, tagged with an `@f` marker so it can never collide with the masculine bucket) as disjoint normalization axes, and requires **exact** key equality rather than a second fuzzy threshold — both changes are individually necessary: disjoint buckets alone still left 66 hits (mostly short-stem coincidences); exact-match alone (without disjoint buckets) would still cross genders. Together they reproduce all 3 real DEV-043 precedents (each normalizes to an identical key, score 100.0 pre-normalization-collapse) while cutting the live extra-hit count to **12**, each a plausible genuine spelling-variant lead (e.g. `Atropos`/`Atropus`, `Alcmena`/`Alcmene`, `Nice`/`Nike` via the `k`→`c` substitution). **(3)** Two readers mirroring the established pattern: `load_entity_names_from_candidates`/`load_entity_names_from_db`, plus `load_known_aliases` (reads `known_aliases.json`, **original casing preserved** — unlike `extraction/entity_resolver.py`'s own loader, which lowercases keys for its different, case-insensitive resolution purpose) and `load_entity_aliases_from_db` (B3's live DB layer, joining `entity_aliases` to `entities`). **(4)** `run(candidates_dir, db_conn)` (A2r contract, `NAME = "A1"`) always reads the static `known_aliases.json`, and additionally merges in live `entity_aliases` rows whenever a DB connection is available (B3's two-layer cross-check), regardless of which name-source(s) are being scanned. A `_known_pair_keys` helper only excludes a known alias pair when **both** sides are actually present as entity names (e.g. `Jupiter`→`Zeus` never suppresses anything, since `Jupiter` was never itself extracted as an entity — confirmed empirically: the live known-pairs set is currently empty, since all 27 `known_aliases.json` pairs that *did* cause duplication were already merged away by DEV-043). **(5)** Standalone `python -m audit.duplicate_entities [--candidates \| --db]` CLI mirroring the established precedent. **(6)** 15 tests (`tests/test_duplicate_entities.py`) per B5's spec plus the gender-disjointness regression guard and a real-data sanity check (`20 <= len(pairs) <= 80`, not an exact 29 — see Impact). |
| **Reason** | The redesign wasn't optional scope creep — it was forced by actually running the check against real data rather than trusting small synthetic fixtures alone (the same "verify against the live tree" discipline this project applies everywhere else). A naive implementation of exactly what the checklist's prose describes (`-os`↔`-us`, `-e`↔`-a` as generic substitutions) would have shipped an audit check that floods every future run with dozens of false leads, defeating A1's purpose as a targeted signal; keeping masculine/feminine endings disjoint and requiring exact post-normalization equality (rather than a second fuzzy pass) is the minimal fix that preserves the 3 real precedents while eliminating the false-positive class, verified by rerunning the live scan after each change. |
| **Impact** | New: `ingestion/audit/duplicate_entities.py`, `ingestion/audit/tests/test_duplicate_entities.py`. All 222 `ingestion/.venv` pytest tests pass (207 pre-existing + 15 new). Live-verified: `python -m audit --candidates` now also runs **A1** (auto-discovered alongside A3/A4) → **45** candidate pairs over 1,969 entities (33 raw-name matches + 12 transliteration-only matches), not exactly DEV-044's 29 — expected drift, explicitly permitted by B5's own "not exact... note any delta" clause: the confirmed entity set has grown/changed since that 2026-07-15 snapshot, DEV-044's original script's exact pairing method isn't preserved anywhere to diff against, and this scan additionally includes the (correctly-scoped) transliteration pass DEV-044 never ran. The live known-pairs cross-check (`known_aliases.json` + `entity_aliases`) currently removes **zero** pairs (confirmed empty), since every alias pair that caused real duplication was already merged away by DEV-043 — expected, not a bug. **Not yet done**: the 45 pairs are triage leads only — Track J1 (deciding merge-vs-reject per pair, editing candidate JSON, adding `entity_aliases` rows) is separate, not-yet-started work. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-074 — Track C: `drop_accounting.py`, audit check A2 — explains the `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` → seeded `V11` drop by reason, reusing `relationships_gen`'s real filter/dedup functions; unexpectedly discovered ~367 candidate missing entities (incl. Nereus, Doris, Styx, Ceto) via the unknown-name drilldown

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3, Track C (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`) — the next parallel check track after A/B/D/F, unblocking Track J2's 203-row backlog and surfacing new missing-entity leads for Track J. |
| **Original Plan** | `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §4.1` A2: "explain the 6,026→2,496 relationship drop by reason (unknown-entity-name, contested-edge collapse, dedupe). Unknown-name drops are where missing/split entities hide (the Io precedent, DEV-042)." The checklist's C1–C4 additionally specify: a pure diff core reusing `relationships_gen`'s actual filter/dedup functions (not re-derived), a per-name unknown-name drilldown feeding Track J, and a reconciling arithmetic (`raw − unknown_name − contested_collapse − dedupe == seeded`) whose non-zero residual is itself a finding. |
| **What Changed** | **(1)** Clarified what "raw" means for this check's arithmetic (see the checklist banner and Reason below): `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (**6,009** rows today), not the literal `relationships_candidates_raw.json` file (**7,406**, the pre-B4-manual-cleanup extraction output) — matching `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §4.1`'s own "6,026→2,496" framing (6,026 was the cleaned-file count before DEV-067 dropped 17 rows). **(2)** `ingestion/audit/drop_accounting.py` — pure core `compute_drop_accounting(relationships, entity_names, claim_type_alias_map)` that calls `seedgen.relationships_gen._filter_and_dedup` and `seedgen.canonical_edge.resolve_canonical_edges` **directly, unmodified** (the checklist's explicit "reuse, don't re-derive" instruction, honored literally — even though both are underscore-prefixed, private-by-convention names, imported across the `audit`/`seedgen` package boundary, because reimplementing their loop bodies would risk exactly the drift the check exists to prevent). Since neither function exposes *which* rows it dropped (only survivors), the three buckets are computed via independent set/count arithmetic around their real outputs, not by copying their internal logic: unknown-name rows via a straightforward membership filter, exact-dup count via `(total − unknown) − len(filtered)`, contested-collapse count via `len(filtered) − len(resolved)`. No `relation_aliases` (Track F) normalization is applied — documented in the docstring: the currently-seeded `V11` was generated before that mechanism existed, so accounting for "what's actually seeded today" must not apply a step that never ran. **(3)** The unknown-name drilldown (C2) ranks every distinct dropped-row name by frequency and emits one `Finding` per name — except the extraction sentinel `<UNKNOWN>` (133 occurrences), which gets a distinct `info`-severity/"not a missing entity, an unresolved extraction placeholder" treatment instead of the standard "investigate for a missing or split entity" `warning`. **(4)** `run(candidates_dir, db_conn)` deliberately does **not** mirror A1/A3/A4's "independent equivalent sources" shape: this check explains a *transformation* (candidate JSON → seeded rows), so `candidates_dir` is always required; when `db_conn` is *also* given, it adds a **drift** check — comparing the live `relationships` row count against what regenerating from the current candidates would produce right now — rather than repeating the same drop-accounting breakdown against a DB read (which has no "why was this dropped" information to recover). `candidates_dir=None` (a `--db`-only run) returns an empty, non-failing `CheckResult` with an explanatory summary. **(5)** 10 tests (`tests/test_drop_accounting.py`) per C4's spec (one-of-each-drop-reason fixture reconciling to zero residual) plus frequency ranking, the sentinel-vs-genuine-name branch, the residual-finding path, and the `run()` adapter (no-candidates-dir, candidates-only, and db-drift cases). |
| **Reason** | The checklist's own prose is internally inconsistent about what "raw" means (C1's header names the literal `_raw.json` file and its old row count, but the three buckets it lists are exactly `relationships_gen.py`'s own filters, which only ever run over the *cleaned* file — `_raw.json` → `_cleaned.json` is a separate, already one-time-only, non-mechanical drop: B4's manual quality review plus the 203-row `relationships_flagged_for_review.json` held-out set, both already documented in DEV-043/044 and not re-derivable by any repeatable arithmetic). Diffing the cleaned file (what `seedgen` actually consumes) against the seeded output is the only reading under which C3's reconciliation equation can actually close to zero using just the three named mechanical buckets — verified directly: it does, exactly (`6,009 − 1,206 − 917 − 1,392 − 2,494 = 0`), and `seeded_count` (2,494) matches the currently-applied `V11`'s real row count precisely. Live-verifying the unknown-name drilldown (rather than trusting a synthetic fixture alone) surfaced a genuinely significant, previously-unnoticed data gap: **367 distinct names** are referenced by dropped relationship rows but absent from the 1,969-entity confirmed set — including major, unambiguous figures like `Nereus` (105 references), `Doris` (64), `Styx` (17), `Ceto` (16), `Thaumas` (15), and `Chiron` (11), none of which are typos or spelling variants of an existing entity (confirmed by direct lookup — zero fuzzy overlap with the confirmed set). This is a substantially larger missing-entity backlog than the single Io case DEV-042 fixed, and is exactly the kind of finding C2 exists to surface. |
| **Impact** | New: `ingestion/audit/drop_accounting.py`, `ingestion/audit/tests/test_drop_accounting.py`. All 232 `ingestion/.venv` pytest tests pass (222 pre-existing + 10 new). Live-verified: `python -m audit --candidates` runs **A2** (auto-discovered alongside A1/A3/A4) → `6,009 raw → 2,494 seeded (unknown_name=1206, exact_dup=917, contested_collapse=1392, residual=0)`, 367 distinct unknown names. `python -m audit --db` (both sources) additionally reports `db: live=2494 (drift=0)` — confirming the currently-applied `V11` exactly matches what regenerating from today's candidate JSON would produce (no undetected staleness). **Not yet done**: the 367 unknown names (minus the `<UNKNOWN>` sentinel) are a new, large triage backlog for Track J — deciding which are genuinely missing entities to add (like Nereus) vs. noise; this entry surfaces the finding but does not add any entities or edit any candidate JSON. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-075 — Track E: `integrity.py`, audit check A5 — alias/participant referential integrity + a re-run of DEV-040's direction invariants; completes the five-check `python -m audit` registration (A1–A5 all live)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3, Track E (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`) — the last of the five audit checks, completing `python -m audit`'s check roster (A1–A5) that Track A's runner was built to host. |
| **Original Plan** | `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §4.1` A5: "every alias target exists; no alias equals a canonical name; `myth_participants` names resolve." The checklist's E1–E3 additionally fold in "DEV-040's invariants re-run after every fix batch" (E2) — a scope-move the checklist itself already made from an earlier draft (`docs/TODO2.md` had originally grouped "DEV-040 invariants" under A3/direction-integrity, not A5; the current, authoritative checklist explicitly reassigns it to A5's "integrity surface"). |
| **What Changed** | **(1)** `ingestion/audit/integrity.py` — **E1**: `find_dangling_aliases` (an `entity_aliases.entity_id` with no matching `entities` row), `find_self_aliases` (an alias string that shadows an existing canonical `entities.name`), `find_orphan_participants` (a `myth_participants.entity_id` with no matching `entities` row). The first and third are defensive re-checks of constraints Postgres's `entity_aliases`/`myth_participants` FK definitions already enforce at the schema level (verified: both tables declare `entity_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES entities(id)`) — implemented anyway, cheaply, as a standing safety net against a future schema change or a superuser bypass, exactly as A1/A3's own "defensive, not currently exploitable" checks already do. **(2)** **E2**: reused `audit.cycle_check`'s own `Edge` dataclass and `_query_edges` helper directly (not re-queried) to re-run DEV-040's three own live-verified invariants from its Impact line — `find_multi_parent_violations` (zero children with >1 distinct `parent_of` parent), `find_multi_spouse_violations` (`married_to`), `find_multi_killer_violations` (`killed_by`) — each keyed on the correct subject side per `canonical_edge.py`'s documented direction convention (`parent_of` keys on `to_name`; `married_to`/`killed_by` key on `from_name`). **Resolved an ambiguity**: E2's "subtype invariants" phrase has no concrete definition anywhere in the docs — DEV-040's own Impact line documents only the three *direction* invariants above, nothing subtype-specific. Interpreted as (and implemented as) a defensive re-check that every live `entities.type` value is still one of the 8 `chk_entities_type` CHECK values (verified against the actual constraint in `V3__create_entities.sql`) — again schema-enforced already, but cheap to reconfirm as a regression gate after any future Track J entity split/merge batch, which is exactly the "re-run after every fix batch" framing E2 asks for. **(3)** `run(candidates_dir, db_conn)` (A2r contract, `NAME = "A5"`) is **DB-only** by design (mirroring A2's precedent for a check with no meaningful candidates-only mode): `entity_aliases`, `myth_participants`, and the seeded-graph direction invariants have no candidate-JSON equivalent to check against, so a `candidates`-only run reports "no db connection given" with zero findings rather than failing. **(4)** 16 tests (`tests/test_integrity.py`) per E3's spec (dangling alias, self-alias, orphan participant each a finding; clean fixture passes) plus the three direction-invariant functions, the type-enum check, and an end-to-end `run()` test against a routing fake connection. |
| **Reason** | Reusing `cycle_check`'s `Edge`/`_query_edges` rather than writing a second relationships-table reader keeps exactly one query/mapping for "read the live relationships graph," consistent with Track A's established reuse discipline (DEV-070's `_query_edges` extraction, DEV-074's direct reuse of `relationships_gen`'s real filter functions). Treating E2's ambiguous "subtype invariants" as the CHECK-enum re-verification (rather than inventing an unstated rule) is the most conservative reading consistent with what DEV-040 actually documented and verified live — the alternative (guessing at some other subtype-specific business rule with no textual basis) risks encoding a rule nobody actually asked for. |
| **Impact** | New: `ingestion/audit/integrity.py`, `ingestion/audit/tests/test_integrity.py`. All 248 `ingestion/.venv` pytest tests pass (232 pre-existing + 16 new). Live-verified: `python -m audit --db` now runs all **five** checks (A1–A5) in one pass; **A5 reports PASS, 0 findings** — `27` `entity_aliases` rows, `22` `myth_participants` rows, and `2,494` `relationships` rows all clean (zero dangling aliases, zero self-aliases, zero orphan participants, zero multi-parent/spouse/killer violations, zero invalid `entities.type` values), confirming DEV-040's original live-verification still holds untouched by every subsequent Track (B/C/D/F) landed so far. `python -m audit --candidates` (no DB) correctly reports A5 as a no-op ("no db connection given"). This completes `python -m audit`'s full A1–A5 roster — the P3 exit's "all five checks clean or explicitly waived" is now a real, checkable state (today: A5 clean, A1/A2/A3/A4 all carry real un-triaged findings pending Track J). |

### DEV-076 — Track I: first fix-loop pass lands `relation_aliases` (Track F) for real — reseed → `seedgen --strict` → reseed → audit → 3-run eval → compare; fixed a Flyway out-of-order bug in `reseed-local.sh` and a staleness bug in A2; surfaced 2 new near-certain reversed-edge leads as a byproduct

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3, Track I (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`) — the first serialized fix-loop pass, run "once for the F/relation_aliases landing" per the parallelization guide, with no Track J candidate-JSON edits in this batch (I2 is a deliberate no-op here). User confirmed proceeding given the real DB mutation + paid LLM eval cost this track involves. |
| **Original Plan** | I1–I7: baseline audit → apply batch edits → `seedgen --strict` → `reseed-local.sh` → audit again (clean or waived) → `runner --runs 3` + `compare.py` vs the P2-accepted run (DATA/MIXED ≥ baseline, zero stable regressions) → commit. `docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p2.md`'s `reseed-local.sh` (DEV-065) was written before V17 existed and only knew about V10–V16. |
| **What Changed** | **(1) Discovered and fixed a Flyway out-of-order bug**: applying V17 required clearing `flyway_schema_history` for V10–V16 while V17 stayed applied (to make its table available for `seedgen` to read), but Flyway then refuses to migrate ("Detected resolved migration not applied to database: 10" ×6) because a *higher* version (17) already applied while *lower* versions are pending is out-of-order by default. Fixed `scripts/reseed-local.sh`: added `relation_aliases` to the drop list (`DROP TABLE IF EXISTS relation_aliases`, alongside `entity_aliases`) and `'17'` to `CLEAR_HISTORY_SQL`, so every reseed now resets and re-applies V17 fresh in-order alongside V10–V16, and made both drops `IF EXISTS` (idempotent, safe to re-run after a partially-failed attempt — which is exactly what happened live: the first corrected attempt still failed on a bare `DROP TABLE entity_aliases` from a prior partial run). Updated the script's own "V10-V16" references to "V10-V17" throughout. **(2) Landed Track F for real** via the necessary two-reseed sequence (a chicken-and-egg the checklist's own I3 note hints at but doesn't fully spell out): reseed once to get V17/`relation_aliases` live → `python -m seedgen --strict` (now succeeds loading the live map, regenerating `V11` 2,494 → **2,369** rows, confirming the ADR-019 "counts stop fragmenting" claim) → reseed again to apply the regenerated `V11`. Verified live: zero occurrences of any of the 9 synonym/inverse labels remain in the regenerated `V11__seed_relationships.sql`; distinct seeded relation vocabulary shrank **124 → 116** (Track G's acceptance criterion, confirmed as a side effect of this pass — `python -m audit --db`'s A4 now reports `0` synonym/inverse candidates, down from 8). **(3) Fixed a staleness bug in `drop_accounting.py` (A2)** surfaced by this same run: `compute_drop_accounting` hardcoded "no relation_alias normalization" (accurate when written, since V17 wasn't applied anywhere yet) and after this landing produced a false "db: seeded-count drift=-125" finding (comparing an un-normalized candidates-side regeneration estimate against the now-normalized live count). Added a `relation_alias_map` parameter, applied via the real `_apply_relation_aliases` first (mirroring `build_relationship_rows`'s own order), and `run()` now loads the live map from `db_conn` exactly like `claim_type_alias_map` already does — drift correctly reads `0` again. 2 new tests added (`test_relation_alias_map_normalizes_before_bucketing_and_can_collapse_dedup`, `test_no_relation_alias_map_is_a_no_op`). **(4) A byproduct discovery, not fixed here**: `cycle_check --db` (A3) went from 3 to **4** live cycles — normalization unified `child_of`/`son_of` labels into `parent_of` for the first time, which let `cycle_check` (which only ever filtered on the literal string `parent_of`) see two **new near-certain 2-cycles** it structurally could not have seen before: `Creon ⇄ Menoeceus` (`Creon child_of Menoeceus` normalizes+swaps to `Menoeceus parent_of Creon`, contradicting an existing literal `Creon parent_of Menoeceus` row, both `apollodorus-bibliotheca`, different passages — mythologically, Creon of Thebes is son of Menoeceus, so the `child_of`-derived direction is very likely correct and `3.6.7`'s literal row is the extraction bug) and `Agastrophus ⇄ Paeëon` (`Agastrophus son_of Paeëon` vs. a literal `Agastrophus parent_of Paeëon`, both `homer-iliad`, adjacent passages — same pattern). The pre-existing `Astyoche ⇄ Tros ⇄ Ilus ⇄ Laomedon` cycle (DEV-068) **disappeared** as an incidental side effect of the same normalization pass. Per Track I's own "never two backlogs in one reseed" discipline, **not fixed in this pass** — logged here as a new Track J lead (exactly DEV-067's precedent, source-verifiable, near-certain reversed edges), left for its own batch. **(5) Ran the eval**: `python -m runner --runs 3 --label p3-relation-aliases` against the live (now-normalized) server, then `python -m runner.compare` against `results/2026-07-23T12-31-46Z__ed2ddcc__p2` (the P2-accepted baseline). |
| **Reason** | The out-of-order bug is a direct, unavoidable consequence of V17 being authored after `reseed-local.sh` (P2 Track F, DEV-065) — fixing the script is required infrastructure, not scope creep, since no future reseed (including every subsequent Track J batch) could succeed otherwise. The A2 staleness fix follows directly from actually landing Track F for real (rather than leaving it as untested code) — exactly the kind of drift the check exists to catch, this time in the check's own hardcoded assumption rather than the seed data. The two new cycles are treated as a *discovery*, not a same-batch fix, specifically because Track I's own discipline (never mix two backlogs in one reseed) and this project's established caution against rushed data-correctness calls (DEV-068's precedent: flag and defer rather than force a same-session fix) both argue for keeping the F-landing batch scoped to exactly what it changed. |
| **Impact** | **DB state** (live, local-only): `entities` 1,969 (unchanged), `relationships` **2,369** (was 2,494), `variant_claims` 44 (unchanged), `narrative_chunks` 3,524 (untouched throughout, per `reseed-local.sh`'s own guarantee), `relation_aliases` 9 rows live. **Code**: `scripts/reseed-local.sh` (V17-aware, idempotent drops), `ingestion/audit/drop_accounting.py` (+`relation_alias_map` param, +2 tests) — 250/250 `ingestion/.venv` tests pass. **Migrations**: `core-api/.../V11__seed_relationships.sql` regenerated (87 insertions / 212 deletions net, only this file changed — `V10`/`V12` byte-identical). **Eval**: `results/2026-07-23T16-03-41Z__fdc32f4__p3-relation-aliases/` — pessimistic aggregate 11/16 (69%, up from p2's 10/16, 62%); **zero stable PASS→FAIL regressions**; `MIXED` **0% → 50%** (Q12: stable-fail → stable-pass); `DATA` unchanged at 1/5 (20%, floor 50%, **unmet in both p2 and this run** — a pre-existing gap, not caused by this batch, and Track F's vocabulary-shrink didn't happen to fix this particular DATA question's failure mode); Q8's `SQL → RAG` route change is a non-regression (was `stable-fail` via `serviceError` in p2, is still `stable-fail` via a RAG keyword miss now — same net classification, different proximate cause). Meets the checklist's exit bar for this batch (DATA/MIXED ≥ baseline, zero stable regressions). **Audit** (`python -m audit`, both sources): A5 clean; A1 (45 pairs), A2 (367 unknown names), A3 (**4** cycles, was 3 — see the new-leads discovery above), A4 (9 candidates-mode proposals, 0 db-mode — expected, matches the seeded state) all carry real, un-triaged findings — none new to the P3 exit's overall gate except the 2 new A3 cycles, which are additive Track J backlog, not a regression in anything this batch touched. **Not done**: candidates + migrations + results not yet committed (I7) — pending explicit confirmation, per this project's standing "never commit without being asked" convention; the 2 new cycle leads are not yet source-verified or fixed. |

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|

| **Original Plan** | Not separately planned — an ad hoc triage of DEV-076's own discovery, following the DEV-067 (reversed edge → fix now) / DEV-068 (conflation → split, defer) precedents this project already established for exactly this decision. |
| **What Changed** | **(1) Source-verified both leads** against the actual corpus text. `Agastrophus ⇄ Paeëon`: `homer_iliad_murray1924.txt` line `[336]` ("Tydeus' son wounded the warrior **Agastrophus, son of Paeon**") and line `[368]` ("stripping... **the son of Paeon**", the same Agastrophus) **agree with each other** — Agastrophus is Paeon's son in both passages. The candidate row `Agastrophus parent_of Paeëon` (citing `11.336-11.367`, the very passage that says "son of Paeon") is a straightforward reversed-edge extraction error, the same class as DEV-067's 17 `Odysseus parent_of Laertes` rows — **not** a second person or genuine disagreement. `Creon ⇄ Menoeceus`: `apollodorus_bibliotheca_frazer1921.txt` `[3.5.8]` ("Creon, son of Menoeceus, succeeded to the kingdom") and `[3.6.7]` ("Menoeceus, son of Creon, would offer himself... slew himself before the gates", the Seven-Against-Thebes self-sacrifice) name **two different people**: Menoeceus the elder (Creon's father) and Menoeceus the younger (Creon's son). Both edges are individually correct in their own context; extraction conflated two people sharing a name into one entity — DEV-068's class, not DEV-067's. **(2) Fixed**: dropped the single wrong `{"from_name": "Agastrophus", "relation": "parent_of", "to_name": "Paeëon", "passage_ref": "11.336-11.367", "source_id": "homer-iliad"}` row from `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (6,009 → 6,008 rows) — no tie-break needed, since no source actually attributes that direction (uniformly wrong, same reasoning DEV-067 used for its removals). The correct `Agastrophus son_of Paeëon` row (→ normalizes to `Paeëon parent_of Agastrophus` via Track F) is untouched. **(3) Deferred, not fixed**: `Creon ⇄ Menoeceus` filed as new checklist item **J3e** (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`), needing an entity split (`Menoeceus (elder)` / `Menoeceus (younger)`) in its own batch, not this one. **(4) Also noted, not yet investigated**: J3c's `Astyoche ⇄ Tros ⇄ Ilus ⇄ Laomedon` cycle (DEV-068) no longer appears in `cycle_check --db` as of DEV-076's pass — flagged in the checklist as needing re-verification of *why* it disappeared (a `relation_aliases` side effect vs. something else) before treating it as actually fixed; not root-caused here. |
| **Reason** | Mirrors this project's own already-established split: a genuine reversed edge (both citing sources agree on one direction; the seed row disagrees with its own cited passage) gets fixed immediately and mechanically, same as DEV-067; a namesake collision (both edges individually correct, entity boundaries wrong) needs the same "source-verify, then split, don't rush" discipline DEV-068 used, since guessing a split from partial evidence risks making the seed data worse for a product whose value proposition is source-attributed accuracy. Fixing only the clean case now (rather than both, or neither) keeps this as a single, well-scoped backlog slice per Track I's "never batch two backlogs into one unaudited reseed" discipline — the Menoeceus split is entity-editing work of a different, more careful kind, better done as its own pass. |

| **Original Plan** | DEV-068 diagnosed the cycle against `apollodorus_bibliotheca_frazer1921.txt` `[1.7.1]`–`[1.8.1]`: "Aeolus" (Hellen's son) conflated with his descendant "Aetolus" (Endymion's actual son), and "Calydon" (the DB edge's target) conflated with "Calyce" (Aeolus's actual daughter) — flagged for Stage P3, not fixed in P2. The checklist's J3a bullet: split Aeolus from Aetolus and Calydon from Calyce, repoint the offending edges, citing that same passage range. |
| **What Changed** | **Re-verified against the full passage span (not just DEV-068's summary) and found DEV-068's own diagnosis was half-right**: `Calyce` and `Calydon` were **already two separate entities** in the confirmed set (`Aeolus parent_of Calyce` already existed correctly, per `[1.7.3]`) — no `Calydon`/`Calyce` split was actually needed; DEV-068's phrasing was describing the *effect* (a wrong edge landing on `Calydon`), not a literal merged entity. The **real** gap: `Aetolus` **did not exist as an entity at all** (`Endymion had... a son Aetolus` per `[1.7.6]` was extracted as `Endymion parent_of Aeolus` — a fuzzy-name confusion with the unrelated, much-earlier `Aeolus`), and — found only by reading the full `[1.7.4]`–`[1.7.8]` span exhaustively, not by trusting DEV-068's summary — a **second, separate conflation in the same cycle DEV-068 didn't name**: `Protogenia` also refers to two different people (Deucalion's daughter, mother of Aethlius, `[1.7.2]`; and Calydon's daughter by Aeolia, mother of Oxylus, `[1.7.7]`), both merged into one entity. **Added 2 entities** to `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` (matching the file's own disambiguation convention already used elsewhere, e.g. `Creon (king of Thebes)` / `Creon (son of Hercules)`): `Aetolus` (plain name, no existing collision) and `Protogenia (daughter of Calydon)` (the original `Protogenia` name kept for Deucalion's daughter, since far more existing correct edges already reference it that way). **Repointed 7 rows** in `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (all `apollodorus-bibliotheca`, `1.7.4-1.7.8`, verified by exact full-row match before editing, one match each): `Endymion parent_of Aeolus`→`Aetolus`; `Iphianassa parent_of Aeolus`→`Aetolus` (contested); `Aeolus parent_of Pleuron`→`Aetolus parent_of Pleuron`; `Aeolus parent_of Calydon`→`Aetolus parent_of Calydon`; `Calydon parent_of Protogenia`→`...Protogenia (daughter of Calydon)`; `Aetolia parent_of Protogenia`→`...Protogenia (daughter of Calydon)`; `Protogenia parent_of Oxylus`→`Protogenia (daughter of Calydon) parent_of Oxylus`. |
| **Reason** | Reading the entire cited passage span line-by-line (rather than trusting the prior diagnosis's summary) is exactly the discipline this project already applies to source verification (DEV-067/DEV-077) — it caught a second real conflation (`Protogenia`) that a narrower re-read would have missed and left in place, still forming a cycle even after the `Aetolus` fix alone (since `Calydon parent_of Protogenia` → `Protogenia parent_of Aethlius` would still falsely link Calydon's daughter to Aethlius's mother). Reusing the file's existing `Name (descriptor)` disambiguation convention (already used for `Creon`, `Ilus`, `Aias`, etc.) rather than inventing a new pattern keeps the entity set internally consistent — directly useful precedent for the still-open `Creon`/`Menoeceus` split (J3e). |

Total candidate-level cycle count 106 → 103 (3 fewer, not just 1) — the other 2 are incidental: `Aetolus` (via the renamed `Iphianassa`/`Pleuron` edges) still appears in a much larger, wholly unrelated pre-existing tangle (`Agenor → Demonice → ... → Zeus → ... → Iphianassa → Aetolus → Pleuron/Calydon → Agenor`), confirmed present in the `2026-07-23-I1-baseline.md` snapshot under the old (wrong) `Aeolus` name — not newly introduced by this fix, just correctly relabeled. All 250 `ingestion/.venv` tests still pass (no test touches these specific candidate rows). **Not yet regenerated/reseeded/re-evaluated** — batched with DEV-077's `Agastrophus` fix for a single future Track I pass, per the user's explicit cost-minimization request; `cycle_check --db` will continue to report the old cycle until that pass runs. |
