### DEV-079 — Track J3b: source-verified and split `Cecrops` and `Pandion` to close the `Cecrops ⇄ Pandion ⇄ Erechtheus ⇄ Cecrops` cycle — found **3** distinct Pandions, not the hypothesized 2; batched with DEV-077/DEV-078 for a future Track I pass

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3, Track J3b (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`) — the second of DEV-068's three flagged conflation cycles; its own checklist bullet explicitly required source-verification first ("two Cecrops / two Pandions hypothesis... not yet source-verified"). |
| **Original Plan** | DEV-068: "`Cecrops ⇄ Pandion ⇄ Erechtheus ⇄ Cecrops`... especially fits the same conflation pattern on priors (Athenian myth has two Cecrops and two Pandions in most tellings — Cecrops I/Erechtheus's line vs. Cecrops II, per Apollodorus 3.14–3.15), but this is not yet source-verified." The checklist's J3b: source-verify, split analogously if confirmed. |
| **What Changed** | Read `apollodorus_bibliotheca_frazer1921.txt` `[3.14.1]`–`[3.15.8]` in full (not just the cycle's own 3 edges) to trace the entire Athenian succession: Cecrops I (autochthonous first king) → Cranaus → Amphictyon → Erichthonius → **Pandion I** (Erichthonius's son, father of Erechtheus/Butes/Procne/Philomela, `[3.14.6]`–`[3.14.8]`) → Erechtheus → **Cecrops II** ("the eldest of the sons of Erechtheus," `[3.15.5]`) → **Pandion II** (Cecrops II's son, father of Aegeus/Nisus/Pallas/Lycus, `[3.15.5]`–`[3.15.6]`). The hypothesized "two Cecrops" was confirmed exactly. The hypothesized "two Pandions" turned out to be **three**: pulling every candidate row referencing `Pandion` (not just the cycle's own edges) surfaced `Pandion married_to Callidice` / `Hephaestine parent_of Pandion` citing `[2.1.5]` — traced to a wholly unrelated passage (the 50 sons of Egyptus marrying the 50 daughters of Danaus) naming a **third, unrelated Pandion** who has nothing to do with Athens. Missing this would have left a real person's data silently merged into whichever Athenian Pandion kept the bare name. **Added 3 entities**: `Cecrops (son of Erechtheus)` (Cecrops II, `subtype: king` matching the existing `Cecrops` row's convention), `Pandion (son of Cecrops)` (Pandion II), `Pandion (son of Egyptus)` (the unrelated Danaid-myth figure) — kept the bare `Cecrops`/`Pandion` names for Cecrops I / Pandion I, the more central, earlier, more-referenced figures in the Athenian line (mirroring J3a's choice to keep the bare name for the more-established figure). **Repointed 12 rows** (all `apollodorus-bibliotheca`, verified by exact full-row match before editing): 3 rows to `Cecrops (son of Erechtheus)`, 7 rows to `Pandion (son of Cecrops)` (one of these, `Cecrops parent_of Pandion` `[3.15.3-3.15.5]`, needed **both** endpoints changed), 2 rows to `Pandion (son of Egyptus)`. Two remaining ambiguous rows (`Cecrops founder_of Athens`, `Pandion ruler_of Athens`, both citing Ovid `Metamorphoses` `15.391-15.430`) were checked against that passage and found to be a rhetorical list of fallen civilizations (Pythagoras's speech, Book 15 end) using "Cecrops'"/"Pandion's" as generic epithets for Athens rather than asserting a specific reign — left attached to the bare (Cecrops I / Pandion I) names as the more defensible default, not touched. |
| **Reason** | Pulling every row referencing the ambiguous name (not just the 3 edges forming the reported cycle) is what caught the third, unrelated Pandion — the same "verify against the live tree, don't trust the summary" discipline DEV-078 already demonstrated finding the second `Protogenia`. Keeping the bare names for the earlier/more-central figures (rather than qualifying all variants equally) matches this file's existing convention and DEV-078's own precedent, and minimizes touched rows (the Cecrops-I/Pandion-I lines have far more existing correct references than their namesakes). |
| **Impact** | Changed: `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` (1,971 → 1,974 entities), `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (6,008 rows, unchanged count). `python -m audit.cycle_check` (candidates mode): cycle count 103 → 101 (the targeted `Cecrops ⇄ Pandion ⇄ Erechtheus` cycle confirmed gone via grep; the other 1 fewer is incidental, not separately traced). All 250 `ingestion/.venv` tests still pass. **Not yet regenerated/reseeded/re-evaluated** — batched with DEV-077 (`Agastrophus`) and DEV-078 (`Aeolus`/`Protogenia`) for a single future Track I pass, continuing the user's cost-minimization approach. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-080 — Track J3c: root-caused why the `Astyoche ⇄ Tros ⇄ Ilus ⇄ Laomedon` cycle vanished from `--db` after Track F — a masking side-effect (a different `Ilus` mislabel), not an actual fix; fixed both the mask and the real underlying `Astyoche` conflation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3, Track J3c (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`) — the checklist itself flagged this cycle's disappearance as needing re-verification ("don't just take the absence at face value") rather than an already-closed item. |
| **Original Plan** | DEV-076 observed the live cycle count go 3→4 after Track F's landing, with the old `Astyoche ⇄ Tros ⇄ Ilus ⇄ Laomedon` cycle (DEV-068) gone and 2 new ones (DEV-077/078's `Agastrophus`, `Creon`/`Menoeceus`) in its place, explicitly not root-caused at the time. The checklist's J3c: trace it, identify reversed edge vs. conflation. |
| **What Changed** | **Empirically compared** `resolve_canonical_edges`'s output for `Ilus`'s resolved parent with and without Track F's `relation_alias_map` applied (a direct script against the real `seedgen` functions, not abstract reasoning) — found it flips from `Tros` (pre-Track-F) to `Dardanus` (post-Track-F). Traced why: a bare `Ilus son_of Dardanus` row (`homer-iliad`, `11.368-11.410`) — the **exact same passage** already checked for DEV-077's `Agastrophus` fix ("the barrow that men's hands reared for **Ilus, son of Dardanus, an elder of the people in days of old**") — was, pre-Track-F, a literal `son_of` edge invisible to `canonical_edge.py`'s contested-group logic (which only ever grouped literal `parent_of`/`married_to`/`killed_by`). Track F's normalization turned it into `Dardanus parent_of Ilus`, newly entering the contested-parent group for bare `Ilus` alongside the real `Tros parent_of Ilus` claim — and won the alphabetical tie-break (`dardanus` < `tros`) among `homer-iliad`-citing claims. **This "fixed" nothing** — it masked the real cycle by introducing a *different* wrong resolution (this Homer line's "Ilus, son of Dardanus... an elder... in days of old" is explicitly a distinct, ancient figure, not the Trojan-line Ilus/son-of-Tros the cycle is about) — confirmed by checking `--candidates` mode (which applies no normalization or contested-collapse at all): the original cycle is still there, unchanged, as cycle #17 in the current report. **Fixed both**: (1) repointed the `Ilus son_of Dardanus` row to the **already-existing**, correctly-disambiguated `Ilus (son of Dardanus)` entity (found already in the confirmed set, with its own correct `Dardanus parent_of Ilus (son of Dardanus)` edge from Apollodorus `[3.11.2]`+ — the split had already been done for *this* namesake at some earlier stage, just missed for this one Homer row) — this restores `Tros` as `Ilus`'s correct resolved parent once reseeded, which would **resurrect** the original cycle unless the real bug is also fixed; so (2) **source-verified and split `Astyoche`**, the actual root cause: `[3.12.2]` names her explicitly "**Astyoche, daughter of Simoeis**" as Erichthonius's wife and Tros's mother; a separate, unmarked continuation near `[3.12.3]` and `[E.6.15c]` (a finer epitome sub-marker than the candidate row's cited `E.6.15-E.6.22` range, itself unrelated content — a passage_ref imprecision, not investigated further here) name a **different** Astyoche as Laomedon's daughter, Priam's sister. Added `Astyoche (wife of Erichthonius)` and `Astyoche (daughter of Laomedon)`; repointed the 4 rows that specifically form the cycle. **Noted, not fixed**: `Astyoche` also names at least two more distinct people in this corpus (daughter of Phylas, mother of Tlepolemus by Heracles, `[2.7.6]`/`[E.3.13]`; daughter of Niobe and Amphion, `[3.5.6]`) — left on the bare `Astyoche` name, an unresolved residual ambiguity flagged for a future pass, since neither participates in this cycle. Also separately noticed (not fixed, out of scope for this cycle): 3 `Aeneas parent_of/ancestor_of Ilus` rows (`ovid-metamorphoses`, `14.566-14.608`/`15.431-15.452`/`15.760-15.806`) whose cited passages, read in full, never mention "Ilus" at all — they discuss **Iulus**/Ascanius (Aeneas's actual son); likely a `Iulus`/`Ilus` extraction confusion, not a namesake collision, and not yet corrected. |
| **Reason** | Testing the actual resolution empirically (writing a script against the real `seedgen` functions with and without the alias map) rather than reasoning abstractly about whether normalization "could" affect this chain is what found the precise mechanism — abstract reasoning alone would not have surfaced that an *unrelated* Homeric aside about a minor, ancient namesake was the actual cause. Fixing only the mask (the `Ilus`/`Dardanus` mislabel) without also fixing the real `Astyoche` conflation would have made the audit *worse*, not better — reintroducing the exact cycle DEV-068 flagged, now for a reason nobody understood; fixing both together is the only way this batch is a genuine improvement rather than a shell game. Leaving the further `Astyoche` residual (Phylas's/Niobe's daughters) and the `Iulus` mislabel unfixed keeps this batch scoped to what's actually needed to close the reported cycle, consistent with this session's discipline of not solving every adjacent issue discovered mid-investigation in the same pass. |
| **Impact** | Changed: `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` (1,974 → 1,976 entities), `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (6,008 rows, unchanged count — 5 in-place field edits). `python -m audit.cycle_check` (candidates mode): 101 → 100 cycles; the `Astyoche ⇄ Tros ⇄ Ilus ⇄ Laomedon` signature confirmed absent. All 250 `ingestion/.venv` tests still pass. **Not yet regenerated/reseeded/re-evaluated** — batched with DEV-077/078/079 for a single future Track I pass. Once that reseed runs, `--db` cycle count is expected to reflect a *genuine* fix here rather than the DEV-076 masking artifact — worth explicitly re-confirming post-reseed that the count moves for the right reason. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-081 — Track J3e: source-verified and split `Creon`/`Menoeceus` (3 distinct Creons, 2 distinct Menoeceuses); discovered — but did not fix — a new, unrelated `Helle`/`Hellen` conflation while verifying the fix in the simulated post-Track-F graph

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3, Track J3e (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`) — the `Creon ⇄ Menoeceus` lead DEV-077 filed while triaging DEV-076's two new cycles, deferred at the time per the DEV-068 precedent (source-verify before splitting). |
| **Original Plan** | DEV-077's diagnosis: Apollodorus `[3.5.8]` names Menoeceus the elder (Creon's father); `[3.6.7]` names Menoeceus the younger (Creon's son, the Seven-Against-Thebes self-sacrifice) — both edges individually correct, one entity wrongly shared by two people. Filed as J3e, not fixed in that pass. |
| **What Changed** | Pulled every row referencing `Creon` or `Menoeceus` (the J3b/J3c lesson — check all references, not just the cycle's own edges) and found the ambiguity ran deeper than DEV-077's diagnosis: **`Creon` names three different people** — Creon of Thebes (Menoeceus-elder's son, Menoeceus-younger's/Haemon's/Megara's father), Creon of Corinth (`[1.9.28]`, Glauce's father, killed by Medea's poisoned robe), and Creon son of Hercules (already correctly split out as `Creon (son of Hercules)` in an earlier pass). **Two of these three already had partial disambiguation**: `Creon (king of Thebes)` already existed as an entity with one correctly-attached edge (`parent_of Megaera`, `[2.7.8-2.8.1]`) — the same "some rows got the memo, some didn't" partial-split pattern DEV-080 found for `Ilus`. Added the missing `Creon (king of Corinth)` and both `Menoeceus (father of Creon)` / `Menoeceus (son of Creon)` (no prior disambiguation existed for either Menoeceus). Repointed 8 rows: 2 to `Creon (king of Corinth)`, 2 more to `Creon (king of Thebes)` (joining the one already there) plus the 2 rows whose *both* endpoints needed changing (`Creon child_of Menoeceus` → `Creon (king of Thebes)` / `Menoeceus (father of Creon)`; `Creon parent_of Menoeceus` → `Creon (king of Thebes)` / `Menoeceus (son of Creon)`), and 2 to `Menoeceus (father of Creon)` (the contested `Epicasta`/`Jocasta` parentage claims from `[3.5.6]`-`[3.5.7]`, confirming the classical detail that Jocasta is Creon's sister — same father, Menoeceus the elder). Bare `Creon` and bare `Menoeceus` end up with zero remaining edges after this — left in the entity set rather than deleted (deleting orphaned entities is a separate decision with its own risk to `variant_claims`/`myth_participants` references, out of scope here). A minor, separately-noted-but-not-fixed spelling issue: one repointed row cites Creon of Thebes's daughter as `Megaera` (a Fury in standard mythology) where Homer's actual text (`Odyssey` 11.225-270) says `Megara` (Heracles's wife) — a likely typo, left alone. **Verifying the fix required a different method than J3a/J3b**: this cycle only ever existed via the `child_of`→`parent_of` normalization (a raw `--candidates` check can't see it, same as DEV-080's finding), so verification used the same simulated-resolved-graph script as DEV-080 rather than `cycle_check --candidates` directly — confirmed zero cycles involving `Menoeceus` or `Creon` in the simulated post-Track-F graph. **Found something new while checking**: the simulated graph's only remaining cycle is `Aeolus → Athamas → Hellen → Aeolus`, unrelated to this fix. Traced it: `Athamas parent_of Hellen` (`[1.9.1]`) actually reads "Athamas... begat a son Phrixus and a daughter **Helle**" (the girl who fell into the strait later named the Hellespont after her) — an extraction spelling conflation between `Helle` (Athamas's daughter) and `Hellen` (the male ancestor-figure, Deucalion's son, already a distinct, heavily-referenced entity). **Not fixed** — a new, unrelated lead, noted here for a future Track J batch, not investigated further. |
| **Reason** | Checking every reference rather than just the reported cycle's edges is now this session's standing practice (DEV-079/DEV-080 both found extra scope this way); doing it again here found a genuinely more tangled situation (3 Creons, one already half-split) that the original DEV-077 diagnosis had no way to know about from 2 edges alone. Using the simulated-resolved-graph verification (rather than plain `cycle_check --candidates`) is necessary specifically because this cycle's existence depends on Track F's `child_of` normalization, which raw candidates-mode checking cannot see — the same lesson DEV-080 established. Not chasing the new `Helle`/`Hellen` lead keeps this batch scoped to Creon/Menoeceus, consistent with the whole session's discipline of noting-not-solving every adjacent discovery in the same pass. |
| **Impact** | Changed: `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` (1,976 → 1,979 entities), `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (6,008 rows, unchanged count — 8 in-place field edits). Simulated post-Track-F resolved graph: 1 `parent_of` cycle remains (`Aeolus ⇄ Athamas ⇄ Hellen`, new, unrelated, not yet filed as its own checklist item), down from having included the `Creon`/`Menoeceus` cycle before this fix. All 250 `ingestion/.venv` tests still pass. **Not yet regenerated/reseeded/re-evaluated** — batched with DEV-077/078/079/080 for a single future Track I pass. This closes DEV-077's originally-filed J3e lead; the new `Helle`/`Hellen` finding is a fresh, not-yet-filed lead for a later batch. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-082 — Track J3f: `Hellen` conflated **three** people, not a simple typo — split off `Helen` (of Troy, 52 rows) and `Helle` (Athamas's daughter, 3 rows) from `Hellen` (Deucalion's son, 5 rows); closes the last known live cycle and 10 other tangled ones as a side effect

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3, Track J3f (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`) — the fresh lead DEV-081 filed while verifying the `Creon`/`Menoeceus` fix (the last remaining cycle in the simulated post-Track-F resolved graph, `Aeolus ⇄ Athamas ⇄ Hellen`). |
| **Original Plan** | DEV-081's filing: `Athamas parent_of Hellen` (`[1.9.1]`) should read `Athamas parent_of Helle` — a single mislabeled row, "not yet source-verified beyond this one row (check whether other `Helle`/`Hellen` rows are similarly confused before splitting)." |
| **What Changed** | Pulled every one of the **60** rows referencing bare `Hellen` (the by-now-standard practice) and found the conflation was far larger than one row: `Hellen` mixes **three** different people — **Hellen** the male, Deucalion's son and eponym of the Greeks (`[1.7.1-1.7.3]`, 5 rows: parented by Deucalion/Pyrrha, father of Aeolus/Dorus/Xuthus — all internally consistent, left untouched); **Helle**, Athamas's daughter by Nephele who fell into the strait later named the Hellespont after her (`[1.8.6-1.9.1]`, 3 rows: `Athamas parent_of`, `Nephele parent_of`, `killed_by Hellespont`); and **Helen of Troy** (everywhere else — **52 rows** across `homer-iliad`, `homer-odyssey`, `ovid-metamorphoses`, and multiple `apollodorus-bibliotheca` passages — married to Menelaus then Paris/Alexander then Deiphobus, briefly abducted by Theseus, daughter of Zeus and Leda/Nemesis, mother of Hermione/Nicostratus, sibling of Castor). Spot-verified the largest bucket directly against the source (`[3.10.8]`: "the kings of Greece repaired to Sparta to win the hand of **Helen**"; `[3.11.1]`: "Menelaus had by **Helen** a daughter Hermione... a son Nicostratus") — the corpus text itself spells her name correctly; only the extraction added the spurious extra "n". Added `Helen` and `Helle` as new entities; **bulk-edited all 55 non-`Hellen`-male rows programmatically** (partitioned by the two distinctive passage-ref buckets, `1.7.1-1.7.3` untouched vs. `1.8.6-1.9.1`→`Helle` vs. everything else→`Helen`), asserting the exact expected bucket sizes (5/3/52) before writing, rather than editing 55 rows by hand as individual exact-match assertions (impractical at this scale, unlike the smaller per-row edits in DEV-078/079/080/081). |
| **Reason** | The bucket-partition-by-passage-ref approach (rather than individually enumerating and hand-verifying each of 55 rows) was the only tractable way to handle a conflation at this scale, and is still safe: the two special-case passage refs (`1.7.1-1.7.3`, `1.8.6-1.9.1`) are exhaustively verified against source text, and everything else is spot-checked and mythologically unambiguous (Menelaus/Paris/Troy is definitionally Helen of Troy — there is no other plausible reading for those passages). Checking all 60 references (not just the one row DEV-081 flagged) is what caught that this was a 3-way, 60-row conflation rather than a 1-row typo — exactly the pattern DEV-079/080/081 each independently re-discovered, now clearly the standing lesson for this whole class of bug: a namesake collision is never safely assessed from the cycle's own 2-3 edges alone. |
| **Impact** | Changed: `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` (1,979 → 1,981 entities), `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (6,008 rows, unchanged count — 55 in-place field edits). Simulated post-Track-F resolved graph: **0** `parent_of` cycles remain (was 1, the `Aeolus ⇄ Athamas ⇄ Hellen` cycle this fix targeted). `python -m audit.cycle_check` (candidates mode, no normalization applied): **100 → 89** cycles — 11 fewer, not just 1, because `Hellen`'s conflated Helen-of-Troy edges (particularly `Hellen parent_of Hermione`/`married_to Menelaus`) had been silently threading through several of the large, already-observed tangled chains this session kept encountering (e.g. the `Agenor → Demonice → ... → Leda → Hellen → Xuthus → ...` mega-cycle seen since the very first `--candidates` baseline) — disentangling the real `Hellen` from `Helen` broke those false links too. All 250 `ingestion/.venv` tests still pass. **Not yet regenerated/reseeded/re-evaluated** — batched with DEV-077/078/079/080/081 for a single future Track I pass. This is the last of the currently-known live-cycle leads; the simulated post-Track-F graph is now clean. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

|---|---|

Unlike the first Track I pass (DEV-076), this one needed only a **single** reseed — `relation_aliases` (V17) was already live from the prior pass, so no chicken-and-egg ordering issue. |

A1/A4 unchanged in kind (A1 candidates count ticked up 45→48, since the 12 new disambiguated entity names introduce a few new fuzzy-similarity leads for a future Track J1 pass — not a regression, an expected side effect of adding more entities). **I6**: `python -m runner --runs 3 --label p3-j3-batch` against the freshly-reseeded server, then `python -m runner.compare` vs `results/2026-07-23T12-31-46Z__ed2ddcc__p2`. |

### DEV-084 — Track J1: triaged all 48 live fuzzy-duplicate pairs (A1) — 8 merged, 40 rejected with recorded reasoning; discovered `Epicaste` was a 3rd multi-way name conflation (Jocasta + 2 unrelated women) needing a split, not a simple merge

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3, Track J1 (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`) — the 29-pair (now 48, after the Track J3 entity splits added a few incidental leads) fuzzy-duplicate backlog, triaged for the first time since DEV-044's original unreviewed scan. |
| **Original Plan** | J1a–J1c: for each pair, decide merge (DEV-043 pattern: pick the canonical name, rewrite the loser's occurrences at the candidate layer, add an `entity_aliases` row) or reject (record the reason in the file); propagate merges through `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` + `variant_claims_candidates.json`; run the batch through Track I. |
| **What Changed** | Pulled all 48 live pairs from `duplicate_entities.py` with type/relationship/claim-count context (mirroring the old flagged-file's format) and triaged each individually against the actual corpus text or relationship-context, rather than the fuzzy score alone. **8 merged**: `Ilithyia`→`Eileithyia` (the childbirth goddess, cross-source parentage variants of one figure — DEV-044's own flagged high-confidence case), `Alcmene`→`Alcmena` (Heracles's mother, consistent across 6 sources), `Atropus`→`Atropos` (one of the Fates, alternate genealogy traditions for the same figure), `Euneos`→`Euneus` (Jason/Hypsipyle's son — the *same* `homer-iliad` source spells him both ways in different books), `Cebrenus`→`Cebren` (the river-god father of Oenone/Hesperia across sources), `Perimela`→`Perimele` (a zero-data stub merged into the documented Ovid figure, no data lost), `Lampetia`→`Lampetie` (Homer's Odyssey and Ovid's Metamorphoses both name the identical sister pair "Phaethusa and Lampetie/Lampetia," daughters of the Sun), and `Epicaste`→(split then merge, see below). **Found a third multi-way conflation while triaging** (matching DEV-079/080/081/082's now-standard finding): `Epicaste` alone covered **three** different women — Jocasta/Oedipus's mother (Homer's spelling for the same figure `Epicasta` already carried, via `[3.5.6]`/Odyssey `[271]` "the mother of Oedipodes, fair Epicaste"), Calydon's daughter (`[1.7.4]`, Agenor's wife), and Augeas's daughter (`[2.7.8]`, mother of Thettalus by Heracles). Split off `Epicaste (daughter of Calydon)` and `Epicaste (daughter of Augeas)` as new entities; merged only the Jocasta portion into the already-used `Epicasta`. **40 rejected**, each with a specific recorded reason — patterns found: (a) already-resolved by this session's own J3 splits (`Aeolus`/`Aetolus`, `Helen`/`Hellen`, `Helle`/`Hellen`); (b) type mismatches signaling genuinely different figures (`Ares`/`Dares`, `Medus`/`Medusa`, `Nice`/`Nike`, `Idas`/`Midas`, etc.); (c) **false positives from shared descriptor suffixes** — `Antiope (daughter of Thespius)`/`Erato (daughter of Thespius)` and `Laomedon (son of Hercules)`/`Palaemon (son of Hercules)` fuzzy-match on the parenthetical, not the actual names; (d) **catalogue-list coincidences** — `Eione`/`Ione` (both Nereids in the same enumeration, Apollodorus `1.2.1-1.2.7`/Hesiod `233-269`) and `Plexaura`/`Plexaure` (an Oceanid vs. a Nereid — different catalogues entirely) are adjacent, similarly-named entries in intentional lists of ~50 distinctly-named sisters, not duplication bugs; (e) one **downgrade from a tentative merge**: `Coeranos`/`Coeranus` looked promising (both "killed_by" claims) until closer inspection showed `Coeranus` itself already covers two different Iliad casualties (killed by both Odysseus `[5.655]` and Hector `[17.582]`) — merging would compound an existing ambiguity rather than resolve one, so rejected and flagged as a new, deeper, not-yet-investigated lead. Wrote all 48 decisions with full reasoning to `entities_fuzzy_duplicates_flagged_for_review.json` (superseding DEV-044's 29-entry, all-`unreviewed` snapshot). Added all 8 merges to both `V14__create_entity_aliases.sql` (already covered by every reseed since it's in `reseed-local.sh`'s V17-aware drop/clear lists) and `ingestion/extraction/known_aliases.json` (the sibling extraction-time resolver source), mirroring the exact DEV-043 dual-file convention — except `Epicaste`, which deliberately gets **no** blanket alias (unlike the other 7): `Epicaste` is genuinely ambiguous in the source material itself (three different women), so a global alias would incorrectly resolve two of those meanings to the wrong entity; per-row disambiguation (what the split does) is the only correct fix, matching how `Menoeceus`/`Astyoche`/`Creon` were handled in Track J3. |
| **Reason** | Verifying every pair against actual corpus/relationship context (rather than trusting the fuzzy score or type-only heuristics) is what caught the `Epicaste` conflation and the `Coeranos`/`Coeranus` internal-ambiguity risk — both would have been mis-triaged by a shallower pass (a naive merge would have wrongly folded Calydon's and Augeas's daughters into Jocasta; a naive `Coeranos`/`Coeranus` merge would have compounded rather than resolved a duplicate). Rejecting the two shared-descriptor-suffix false positives and the two catalogue-coincidence pairs follows directly from checking *why* they scored high rather than assuming a high score implies a real duplicate — exactly the caution this project has applied to every entity-conflation decision since DEV-068. Withholding a blanket alias for `Epicaste` (unlike the other 7 genuine 1:1 merges) follows the same principle DEV-078/079/080/081 already established for `Menoeceus`/`Astyoche`/`Creon`: an alias is only safe when the loser name unambiguously means one thing everywhere it appears. |
| **Impact** | Changed: `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` (1,981 → 1,975 entities: +2 new `Epicaste` splits, −8 merged-away losers), `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (6,008 rows, unchanged count — 22 in-place field edits across the 8 merges/split), `core-api/.../V14__create_entity_aliases.sql` (+7 alias rows), `ingestion/extraction/known_aliases.json` (+7 entries, mirroring V14), `entities_fuzzy_duplicates_flagged_for_review.json` (rewritten with all 48 decisions + reasoning, superseding the DEV-044 snapshot). All 250 `ingestion/.venv` tests still pass. Live-verified: `python -m audit.duplicate_entities` now reports **40** pairs (down from 48, exactly the 8 merges); `python -m audit --candidates` shows A2's regenerate-estimate rising to 2,501 seeded rows (from 2,378 pre-batch) — expected, since merging previously-fragmented duplicate entities lets more rows collapse via exact-duplicate dedup once both sides share one canonical name. **Not yet regenerated/reseeded/re-evaluated** — this is its own Track J1 backlog batch (J1c), not yet run through Track I; a fresh reseed + eval decision is a separate step. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-085 — Track J2: triaged all 203 ambiguous-direction relationship pairs (`relationships_flagged_for_review.json`) — 202 resolved (895 rows promoted), 1 rejected; surfaced 2 more conflations and a 3-row main-file mislabeling

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3, Track J2 (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`) — the 203-pair ambiguous-direction backlog flagged alongside J1/J3 as part of the same review sweep. |
| **Original Plan** | J2a–J2c: for each of the 203 pairs where extraction candidates disagreed on `from`/`to` direction, resolve to one direction and promote the winning row(s) into `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json`, recording the reasoning per entry. |
| **What Changed** | Applied a 3-tier methodology developed for this batch: (1) if one direction had strictly more corroborating candidate rows, promote it; (2) else if `SPINE_PRIORITY` (`seedgen/canonical_edge.py`) ranked the two directions' sources differently, use that; (3) else manually verify the correct direction against corpus text. Of 203 pairs, 202 resolved this way and 895 rows were promoted; 1 rejected outright (`Eumelus`/`Pheres` — a generation-skip error in the original extraction, no valid claim either direction). Triage surfaced three cross-cutting issues beyond simple direction-picking: **entry 16** (`Zeus`/`Hellen`) was a second instance of the `Helen`/`Hellen`/`Helle` conflation DEV-082 had already split for other rows — required the same 3-way passage-based split applied here too. **Entry 119** (`Pandion`/`Cecrophus` context) needed the already-split `Pandion (son of Cecrops)` entity (from DEV-079) rather than the bare `Pandion`. **Entry 173** required splitting off a 4th, previously-unknown `Creon` — `Creon (father of Lycomedes)` — distinct from the 3 Creons DEV-081 had already split. **Entries 114 and 200** both turned out to be the same `Phorcus`→`Phocus` mislabeling (Aeacus and Psamathe's son, misspelled with the sea-god Phorcys/Phorcus's name); a blanket scan for every "Phorcus" occurrence among the 203 flagged entries found all of them were this same bug, so fixed as one pass rather than per-entry; the same scan also caught **3 pre-existing rows already in `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` before this batch** (`Psamathe parent_of Phorcus` ×2, `Phorcus killed_by Telamon` ×1) with the identical typo, fixed alongside. Wrote the full 203-entry decision record (direction chosen, tier used, reasoning) to `relationships_flagged_for_review.json`, superseding the original unreviewed 203-entry array. |
| **Reason** | A pure majority-vote or pure spine-priority rule would have mis-resolved the ~48 pairs that needed tier 3 (manual verification) — e.g. the `Zeus`/`Hellen` and `Creon`/Lycomedes cases were not simple direction flips but symptoms of an entity conflation that direction-picking alone would have silently baked into the promoted rows. Fixing the `Phorcus`/`Phocus` mislabeling as one blanket pass (rather than special-casing entries 114/200 individually) follows the same principle DEV-084 used for `Epicaste`: once a root cause is confirmed, apply it everywhere it appears rather than patching only the instances that happened to get flagged. |
| **Impact** | Changed: `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (6,008 → 6,903 rows, +895 promoted, plus 3 in-place `Phorcus`→`Phocus` field fixes on pre-existing rows), `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` (1,975 → 1,977: added `Phocus` and `Creon (father of Lycomedes)`), `relationships_flagged_for_review.json` (rewritten with all 203 decisions + reasoning). All 250 `ingestion/.venv` tests and all `:core-api:test` tests still pass. `python -m audit --candidates` afterward showed A3 (`parent_of` cycles) jump from 89 to 127 — investigated separately, see DEV-086. **Not yet regenerated/reseeded/re-evaluated** — bundled with J1 for a future Track I pass. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-086 — Post-J2 investigation: A3 cycle count jump (89→127) traced to 8 pre-existing majority/minority reversed-direction pairs, previously hidden inside larger cycle chains; fixed by reversing the minority rows

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3 — a direct follow-on from Track J2 (DEV-085), investigating an unexplained side effect rather than a planned checklist item. |
| **Original Plan** | None — J2's own checklist called only for resolving the 203 flagged pairs; this was discovered while sanity-checking the post-J2 audit output. |
| **What Changed** | After promoting J2's 895 rows, `python -m audit --candidates` reported 127 `parent_of` cycles, up from 89 pre-batch — a 38-cycle jump, unexpectedly large for a batch whose entire purpose was eliminating exactly this class of reversed-edge cycle. Confirmed none of the 8 "near-certain" (2-node) cycles among the 127 — `Achilles⇄Peleus`, `Priam⇄Hector`, `Telamon⇄Ajax`, `Penelope⇄Telemachus`, `Clymene⇄Oceanus`, `Cronus⇄Hestia`, `Zeus⇄Cronus`, `Tantalus⇄Niobe` — came from the 895 J2-promoted rows; all 8 were pre-existing pairs already in `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json`. Pulling every row for each pair showed each was a large majority/minority split already present in the data (e.g. `Peleus parent_of Achilles` ×78 vs. `Achilles parent_of Peleus` ×15; `Cronus parent_of Zeus` ×36 vs. `Zeus parent_of Cronus` ×12), not a 1-vs-1 ambiguity — all majority directions match standard mythology. Working theory: `cycle_check.py` reports one cycle per strongly-connected component via a single DFS back-edge (documented as non-exhaustive), so these 8 pairs' minority-direction rows were likely already inside some larger, already-counted tangled SCC pre-J2, reported as one long chain rather than as their own 2-cycle; adding 895 new edges changed adjacency-list iteration order enough to make DFS surface these specific back-edges directly instead. Reversed all 45 minority-direction rows (across the 8 pairs) to match the majority direction, keeping each row's original `source_id`/`passage_ref` (the citation is still real, just was extracted backwards). Post-fix, `python -m audit --candidates` A3 dropped from 127 to 99, and zero 2-node cycles remain among them — consistent with the theory. |
| **Reason** | Leaving a 38-cycle jump unexplained after a batch specifically aimed at reducing cycles would break this project's verify-before-trusting discipline (DEV-076 through DEV-084 all root-caused similar surprises before moving on). Reversing rather than deleting the minority rows preserves genuine textual citations (a translator's phrasing was extracted backwards, not fabricated) — the same non-destructive-fix principle used throughout J2/J3. |
| **Impact** | Changed: `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (6,903 rows, unchanged count — 45 in-place `from_name`/`to_name` swaps). All 250 `ingestion/.venv` tests and all `:core-api:test` tests still pass. `python -m audit --candidates` A3: 127 → 99 cycles, 0 near-certain 2-cycles remaining (down from 8). The 99 remaining cycles are longer chains (see e.g. the `Eurydice→Danae→Perses→Aeetes→...→Clymenus→Eurydice` 15-node chain), a separate backlog not addressed here. **Not yet regenerated/reseeded/re-evaluated** — bundled with J1/J2 for a future Track I pass. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-087 — Loose-lead cleanup: `Coeranos`/`Coeranus` untangled (3-way split), `Astyoche`'s 2 remaining meanings split, `Aeneas`/`Iulus`↔`Ilus` mislabel fixed, `Megaera`/`Megara` typo fixed

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3 — four small leads noted in passing during J1c (`Coeranos`/`Coeranus`), J3c (`Astyoche`'s remaining ambiguity, `Aeneas`/`Iulus`), and J3e (`Megaera`/`Megara`), none previously promoted to their own checklist items. |
| **Original Plan** | None — these were parenthetical notes ("flagged for a future pass," "a new lead," "left alone") rather than planned work; tackled together as one small batch since each is a self-contained, source-verifiable fix. |
| **What Changed** | **`Coeranos`/`Coeranus`** (J1 had rejected merging these because `Coeranus` alone already named 2 different Iliad casualties): pulled every row for both spellings and found **3** distinct figures, not 2 — `Coeranus` the Lycian warrior in Sarpedon's host (killed by Odysseus, `homer-iliad` `5.655-5.702`; independently corroborated by `ovid-metamorphoses` `13.238-13.279`, Ulysses' own boast listing the identical casualty roll — Coeranus, Alastor, Chromius, Alcander, Prytanis, Halius, Noemon — confirming `Coeranos` is just the Latin spelling of the same man, previously reversed as `Odysseus killed_by Coeranos`), `Coeranus` the charioteer of Meriones (killed by Hector, `5.582-5.625`... `17.582-17.625`), and `Coeranus` father of the seer Polyidus (`apollodorus-bibliotheca` `3.3.1` — "Polyidus, **son of** Coeranus," a reversed edge: the candidate had `Polyidus parent_of Coeranus`). Split into `Coeranus (Lycian warrior)`, `Coeranus (charioteer of Meriones)`, `Coeranus (father of Polyidus)`; merged `Coeranos` into the first via a new `entity_aliases`/`known_aliases.json` row (safe here — unlike `Epicaste`, `Coeranos` has exactly one usage and it unambiguously means the Lycian warrior). **`Astyoche`** (J3c had split off 2 of 5 meanings — wife of Erichthonius, daughter of Laomedon — and flagged "at least 2 more distinct people" without naming them): found and split the remaining 3 — `Astyoche (daughter of Phylas)` (Tlepolemus's mother by Heracles, `2.7.4-2.7.6`/`2.7.8-2.8.1`/`E.3.13-E.3.19`, corroborated by `homer-iliad` `2.631-2.680`'s "Astyocheia"), `Astyoche (daughter of Niobe)` (`3.5.6-3.5.7`, one of Niobe and Amphion's seven daughters), and `Astyoche (daughter of Actor)` (mother of Ascalaphus and Ialmenus by Ares, `homer-iliad` `2.484-2.535`). Zero bare `Astyoche` rows remain. **`Aeneas`/`Ilus`** (3 rows citing Ovid passages that never mention "Ilus"): all 3 passages (`14.566-14.608`, `15.431-15.452`, `15.760-15.806`) actually reference **Iulus** (Ascanius's Julian-line name) in passing — apotheosis of Aeneas, Pythagoras's prophecy of Rome, Venus lamenting Caesar's murder — not "Ilus" (an unrelated Trojan, Tros's grandson). Since `Ascanius` already exists as the canonical entity (and already has a direct `Aeneas parent_of Ascanius` citation from `13.600-13.639`), renamed `Ilus`→`Ascanius` in all 3 rather than creating a new `Iulus` entity. **`Megaera`/`Megara`** (DEV-081 had spotted this in passing, left unfixed): confirmed the real Fury `Megaera` is a separate, correctly-named figure (`Sky parent_of Megaera`, `1.1.1-1.1.7`/`1.1.4` — born of Uranus's severed-genital blood, alongside Alecto and Tisiphone) from Heracles's wife, whom **every** citing passage (`apollodorus-bibliotheca` `2.4.11-2.4.12`, `2.5.12-2.6.1`, `2.7.8-2.8.1` ×4; `homer-odyssey` `11.225-11.270` ×2) spells "**Megara**," daughter of Creon — a straight extraction typo, not a spelling variant. Added `Megara` as a new entity and renamed all 8 wife-of-Heracles rows to it, leaving the 1 genuine Fury row alone. |
| **Reason** | Each of these was flagged and explicitly deferred in an earlier pass rather than guessed at — resolving them now follows the same verify-against-corpus-text discipline as J1/J3, just applied to leads that had accumulated as footnotes rather than their own checklist rows. The `Coeranos`→`Coeranus (Lycian warrior)` alias (unlike `Epicaste`) is safe specifically because there is only one row using that spelling and its content corroborates, rather than conflicts with, the disambiguated target. |
| **Impact** | Changed: `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` (1,977 → 1,982: +3 `Coeranus` splits −2 old `Coeranus`/`Coeranos` = net +1, +3 `Astyoche` splits, +1 `Megara`), `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (6,903 rows, unchanged count — 24 in-place field edits: 4 Coeranus, 9 Astyoche, 3 Aeneas/Ascanius, 8 Megara/Megaera), `V14__create_entity_aliases.sql` (+1 `Coeranos` row), `known_aliases.json` (+1 mirrored entry). All 250 `ingestion/.venv` tests and all `:core-api:test` tests still pass. `python -m audit --candidates`: A1 40 → 39 pairs (the `Coeranos`/`Coeranus` pair confirmed gone), A3 99 → 96 cycles (3 fewer — some of these entities' bad edges were threading through still-tangled chains). **Not yet regenerated/reseeded/re-evaluated** — bundled with J1/J2/J3g for a future Track I pass. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-23 |

### DEV-088 — Track J4a: ADR-020's co-mention-count discriminator replaced with a four-part rule after simulation against live data; scope widened to cover the 612 dropped rival parents that are recorded nowhere — of which the widened scope reaches 145 (documentation-only; implementation still pending)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3, Track J4a (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`) — the joint-parentage fix decided as ADR-020 on 2026-07-23 with DEV-088 reserved for its implementation. This entry records a **pre-implementation amendment to the decision itself**; no code, data, migration or seed has been touched. |
| **Original Plan** | ADR-020 as accepted 2026-07-23: allow >1 canonical `parent_of` edge per child for genuine joint parentage, discriminated from a contest by a **bare co-mention count** in `resolve_canonical_edges()` — within one `(source_id, passage_ref)`, exactly 2 distinct co-named parents ⇒ couple (keep both), 3+ ⇒ mutually-exclusive alternatives (collapse), cross-source disagreement ⇒ contest (collapse, disagreement in `variant_claims`). Stated blast radius ~665 children; stated that the A3 `cycle_check` DAG invariant would be unaffected; scope limited to joint parentage. |
| **What Changed** | Before implementing, replayed the real pipeline (relation aliases → `relationships_gen._filter_and_dedup` → `resolve_canonical_edges`) over the live `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` + `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` to measure the proposed rule rather than assume it. **The problem is worse than recorded and the proposed discriminator does not hold.** Baseline measured: 2,492 canonical edges, 1,145 children with a parent, **0 children with two** — the loss is total. The bare count rule, simulated: gives children up to **6** parents (`antiphus`: Hecuba, Heracles, Laothoe, Myrmidon, Pisidice, Priam — it keeps every 2-pair from every passage, unanchored to the canonical resolution); injects false parents the resolver never selected (`Athena parent_of Zeus` from Iliad `5.864`, an extraction error; `Pallas`/`Styx` as Zeus's parents from Theogony `346-403`); introduces **6 new `parent_of` cycles**; still drops a true parent on couple-plus-rival groups (`Hellen`: Deucalion, Pyrrha + rival Zeus — counts 3, collapses to Deucalion alone; same for `Endymion`); and **mis-couples `Io`, ADR-020's own worked example** — `Piren` is absent from the confirmed entity set, so `_filter_and_dedup` reduces Io to *exactly two* rival fathers before the resolver ever counts them, and Io is one of the three ADR-004 floor conflicts. Replaced the discriminator with a **four-part rule, all four required**, applied over **co-mention pairs** — two distinct parents of the same child whose rows share one `(source_id, passage_ref)`, taking *every* unordered pair where a passage co-names three or more (the superseded rule's "3+ ⇒ alternatives" clause does **not** carry over): (1) **contested-aware** — rows carrying the extractor's `is_contested=true` flag are excluded from couple candidacy (177 flagged `parent_of` rows; the field already exists on every candidate and was unused by the resolver — it is what separates Hellen's Deucalion+Pyrrha from the rival Zeus), evaluated **per row, not per parent**; (2) **winner-anchored** — a pair is kept only if it contains `_pick_winner`'s canonical winner (that function is unmodified: first spine source that backs any value, alphabetically within it; no spine source ⇒ most distinct corroborating sources, then alphabetical), capping every child at 2 parents and blocking the false-parent injection; (3) **corroboration-ranked** tie-break (most distinct sources → spine rank → alphabetical; this is what yields Heracles → Alcmena+**Zeus** rather than Alcmena+Amphictyon); (4) **deny-listed** — a hand-maintained not-a-couple list, seeded with Io, for the residue rules 1–3 cannot catch. **Rules 1 and 2 interact by design:** `_pick_winner` does not consult `is_contested`, so a winner named only in flagged rows is stripped from every pair by rule 1 and can never couple — the child collapses to that lone winner even where other unflagged parents were co-named. That, not an absence of unflagged candidates, is what produces `Helen → Leda`; it must be pinned by a test. Measured under the adopted rule: **472 children** regain a co-parent, max 2 parents each, **1** new cycle. Also corrected three stated facts: the "~665" blast radius was counted over *raw* candidates before the entity filter seedgen applies (real figures 472 adopted / 487 naive); "the A3 DAG invariant is unaffected" is **false** (the restored — and mythologically correct — `Enarete parent_of Salmoneus` edge closes a chain through a pre-existing reversed hop, so A3 must re-run with budget for a reversed-edge fix pass); and pairs must be formed **pre-dedup**, since `_filter_and_dedup` keeps only the **first** row per `(from, relation, to, source_id)` and discards later passages with their `passage_ref`, so a co-mention survives only where the co-naming passage is the first retained for *each* parent — it is lost for **34 children** (Agamemnon, Ajax, Antiope, Auge, …). **Scope widened** to the second half of the same loss, recorded as `docs/DATA-GAPS.md` GAP-001 **Root cause 3**: the collapse discards **1,084** distinct parent values; ADR-020 recovers 472; **612** rival parents stay collapsed and are recorded **nowhere**, and V12 holds `parentage` rows for exactly **two** subjects (Aphrodite, Io — the hand-curated floor). ADR-007 §6's promise that "the contradiction lives in `variant_claims`" therefore does not currently hold for parentage. J4a's landing scope now also covers a per-row dropped-parent audit check (A2r contract, alongside `drop_accounting.py`) and a same-source qualifying condition in `detect_conflicts` for `parentage`, with the ADR-004 human promotion gate unchanged. |
| **Correction (same day, second measurement pass)** | Two claims in the paragraph above were themselves wrong and are corrected here rather than left standing in an unlanded entry. **(a) The "442" figure is superseded by 472.** It came from a simulation whose co-mention semantics were never written down; ADR-020 as amended did not define what a co-mention pair *is*, so the rule was not reproducible. Under the now-explicit definition (recorded in ADR-020 *Decision*), a replay that matches the baseline exactly — 2,492 canonical edges / 1,145 children with a parent / 0 with two / 641 contested / 1,084 dropped / 608 same-source — and reproduces all ten worked outcomes gives **472**; four other readings give 460 / 465 / 467 / 480 and none gives 442. Every figure in ADR-020 remains a *simulation*: the implementer re-measures against the real `canonical_edge.py` change. **(b) The Root-cause-3 diagnosis was wrong for most of the population.** "608 of 641 contested children are contested inside a single source, so no candidate is ever emitted" conflates two different predicates: 608 counts children where *some one source* names ≥2 parents, whereas `detect_conflicts` (`conflict_detector.py:82`) counts distinct `source_id`s across the **whole** `(subject, claim_type)` group and requires no distinct *values* at all. Measured: **270 of those 608 clear the gate today**. Of the **612** rivals still collapsed after the carve-out, only **145** are in single-source groups the detector cannot see; the other **467** are in multi-source groups where candidates **are already emitted** and stall at the ADR-004 human review gate — a review-throughput problem no detector change touches. So the widened scope reaches ~24% of the residue, and **J4a landing clean will not make parentage conflicts user-visible**; DATA-GAPS records the promotion half as unscoped option (a′) and ADR-007 §6's non-compliance banner now says the gap survives the landing in reduced form. **(c) Smaller corrections in the same pass**, all verified against the candidate data: three worked-example rows misstated their own evidence — `Helen` has a fourth, *unflagged* candidate (`Tyndareus`) so "all three flagged" was wrong (the outcome holds via the rules-1×2 corollary instead), Apollodorus flags **both** `Hera` and `Zeus` for Hephaestus rather than only Zeus, and Heracles has four candidates (incl. `Antiochus`) with Alcmena+Zeus co-named in **6** sources rather than "2 sources beat 1"; `Cronus` has **four** candidate rows, not two, the extra pair being the same couple under the `Heaven` duplicate, which only loses on spine rank. **A3 cycle counts now state their layer** — the "1 pre-existing / +1 new" figures are **post-resolver `parent_of`** and were correct, but sat unlabelled next to 62 (pre-collapse candidates), 96 (DEV-087's `audit --candidates`) and the exit criteria's 0 (currently-seeded DB), none of which are comparable; the landing gate is harmonised to **clean-or-explicitly-waived** across ADR-020, DATA-GAPS and the TODO (it had read plain "audit clean" in the TODO, which is unreachable). **J4c** no longer instructs confirming `Ouranos`/`Chaos` — both stay unreachable after J4a, so its success condition is restated as reach-the-co-parent-generation + timeout + zero stable regressions, with the keyword list explicitly not to be edited. **GAP-001's status** changed from "Partially resolved" to "Open — decided but not landed", since nothing has shipped. Finally, the Root-cause-3 illustration "who was Perseus's father?" was **false** — there are zero `parent_of` candidates into `Perseus` (the only rows are `Nestor`/`Anaxibia` → *"Perseus son of Nestor"*, a different figure), so nothing was dropped for him; it is replaced with `Hellen`/`Zeus` and the Perseus case is boxed as a **fourth, untracked extraction-coverage gap**. |
| **Reason** | ADR-020's decision (couples branch, contests collapse; offline-only, no DDL, no runtime code) is sound and unchanged — only its discriminator and its stated facts failed verification. Measuring the rule against the actual data before writing code follows the same verify-before-trusting discipline as DEV-084/085/086, and caught two defects that would have been expensive after seeding: false parents entering the graph, and a corrupted `Io` floor conflict. `is_contested` was preferred over the count because it is the *source's own* signal about mutual exclusivity, is already densely present in the data, and needs no new curation (unlike the `entities.sex` alternative ADR-020 deferred). Widening scope to Root cause 3 follows directly from the original problem statement — a family tree that keeps co-parents but still silently discards rival parents is only half-fixed, and the product's defining feature cannot fire on its most common conflict dimension. |
| **Impact** | **Documentation only — no code, data, migration, seed, or eval run.** Changed: `docs/adr/adr-020-joint-parentage-multi-edge.md` (status → amended; Decision replaced with the four-part rule + pre-dedup constraint; worked-outcomes table expanded to 10 verified cases; Context/Consequences figures corrected; cycle claim corrected; the bare count added to *Alternatives considered* with its measured failures; *Scope / follow-ups* extended with Root cause 3 and the `Sky`/`Heaven`/`Uranus` blocker; Traceability extended with the affected code touch-points), `docs/DATA-GAPS.md` (GAP-001 status block, Root cause 1 decision block rewritten, **new Root cause 3**, landing note, Recommendation section), `docs/adr/adr-007-conflict-detection-and-surfacing.md` (§6 amendment banner restated + a known-non-compliance banner), `docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md` (J4a rewritten, J4c gains the duplicate-entity caveat). Implementation remains **pending** and still lands through one Track I pass (seedgen → reseed → audit clean-or-waived → 3-run eval → compare). Two known remainders recorded rather than fixed: Q9's literal `Ouranos` keyword still fails until `Sky`/`Heaven`/`Uranus` are merged (three separate confirmed entities today; `Heaven` even carries `Earth` as its own parent) — J1-shaped work, not J4a; and J4b (Chaos cosmogony) is unaffected and still open. Also noted for the implementer: `ingestion/tests/test_canonical_edge.py::test_gyes_shape_same_source_multi_value_ties_break_alphabetically` asserts the *old* single-winner behaviour for exactly the Sky/Earth case this fix reverses, and `canonical_edge.py`'s module docstring cites Gyes as its motivating contested example when Gyes is in fact a couple — both must be rewritten, not deleted. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-26 |

### DEV-089 — Track I (third pass): J1 (8 fuzzy-dup merges) + J2 (203 flagged relationships, 895 rows) + J3f/J3g/J3h (Hellen/Helle/Helen split, 8 reversed-direction pairs, loose-lead cleanup) landed together; first live use of the A3 waiver mechanism

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3, Stage P3, Track I — a standing per-batch loop, run here for the third time. Lands five backlogs (J1a/b, J2a/b, J3f, J3g, J3h) that had each independently reached "candidate-JSON edits complete, batched, not yet reseeded" between 2026-07-23 and 2026-07-23 (DEV-084 through DEV-087) and were explicitly deferred to one future Track I pass rather than triggering a reseed each. |
| **Original Plan** | Run the standing loop (`seedgen --strict` → `reseed-local.sh` → `python -m audit` → `runner --runs 3` → `compare.py` → commit-or-revert) once over the combined J1+J2+J3f+J3g+J3h candidate-JSON edits, per the cost-minimization convention already used for the J3a-e batch (DEV-083). |
| **What Changed** | **I1** — baseline audit on the current tree: `--candidates` matched the documented post-J3h state exactly (A1 39 pairs, A3 96 cycles), confirming no drift since DEV-087. `--db` (pre-reseed) showed A3 **0** cycles (the J3a-e landing holding), A1 41 pairs, A4 116 relations. **I2** — no-op: the J1/J2/J3f/J3g/J3h candidate-JSON edits were already committed in three prior sessions (`ffcfda4` J1, `c0db135` J2, `9c18a23` J3h — J3f/J3g's edits are recorded inside the J3a-e/J2 diffs per their own entries). **I3** — `python -m seedgen --strict` regenerated V10 (1,982 entities) / V11 (2,492 of 6,903 relationship candidates) / V12 (44 of 7,429 variant-claim candidates, `--strict` floor check passed) with no errors, only pre-existing `near-duplicate claim_type` warnings (achilles/cadmus/odysseus/zeus `notable claim` vs `notable_claim`, unrelated to this batch). **I4** — `scripts/reseed-local.sh --local-only`: had to first kill a stale `core-api` process (pid 94052, uptime ~2d20h, same build classpath the script itself starts) that was squatting on port 8080 from an earlier, unrelated session — the script refuses to run while anything holds that port. Reseed completed in one pass (no chicken-and-egg this time; `relation_aliases`/V17 already live from the first Track I pass). Post-reseed counts: entities 1,982, relationships 2,492, variant_claims 44, **narrative_chunks 3,524 unchanged** (embeddings preserved). **I5** — `python -m audit --db`: A1 **39** pairs (matches candidates-mode exactly — the J1 merges took effect, the remainder is the expected permanent long-tail A1 always reports for genuinely-distinct-but-fuzzy-similar names, since A1 only suppresses documented aliases, never "reject, distinct" triage outcomes), A4 **116** relations (unchanged — Track F territory, untouched by this batch), A5 clean (35 `entity_aliases`, 22 `myth_participants`, 2,492 relationships, 0 findings). **A3 reported 1 finding, not 0**: `Eurymachus → Polybus → Eurymachus` (`homer-odyssey` `15.493-15.544` vs `2.177-2.223`) — this cycle is untouched by any J1/J2/J3f/J3g/J3h edit; it was masked pre-reseed by DFS iteration order (the same phenomenon DEV-081/DEV-086 already document for `Creon⇄Menoeceus` and the J2-promotion 2-cycles) and surfaced only because this batch's edges shifted that order. It is also, by name, the exact cycle ADR-020 and `docs/DATA-GAPS.md` GAP-001 already cite as the measured **post-resolver baseline of 1**, which DEV-088/J4a's landing is expected to take to 2. Rather than fix it unbudgeted inside this batch, created **`ingestion/audit/audit-waivers.json`** (the A3r waiver mechanism existed in `__main__.py` since Track A but had never been exercised — no such file existed before this pass) with a written reason citing ADR-020/DATA-GAPS by name and deferring the actual fix to J4a-8 (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`, the reversed-edge fix pass already budgeted there). Re-running `--db` after adding the waiver: A3 **WAIVED**, all five checks clean-or-waived. **I6** — `python -m runner --runs 3 --label p3-j1j2j3fgh-batch`: pessimistic aggregate **11/16 (69%)**, below the 75% target with a DATA floor breach — but `python -m runner.compare` against the last accepted run (`p3-j3-batch`, `d934a8e`) reports **"No stable regressions"**: both runs score identically 11/16, `diff.md` shows zero category-rate changes, zero route changes, zero conflict-count changes. None of the 16 gold questions touch any entity this batch renamed, split, or repointed, so the identical score is expected, not a null result — the pre-existing DATA floor breach (Q9's already-documented content gap, GAP-001) is unchanged, not newly introduced. **I7** — **deliberately not run**, per this project's standing "never commit without being asked" convention and matching the precedent of both prior Track I passes. |
| **Reason** | This is the standing Track I gate applied to already-fully-triaged backlogs sitting idle since 2026-07-23 specifically to avoid the cost of five separate reseed/eval cycles — the same batching call made for J3a-e. The A3 waiver (rather than an in-batch fix) follows the "root cause first, code fix only if still needed" + "never batch two backlogs into one unaudited reseed" rules together: fixing `Eurymachus⇄Polybus` here would silently absorb J4a-scoped work (it's explicitly budgeted as J4a-8) into a batch that has nothing to do with joint parentage, and the ADR-020/DATA-GAPS citations already establish it's understood, not novel. |
| **Impact** | **Code/data landing, no application code changed.** `core-api/src/main/resources/db/migration/V10__seed_entities.sql` / `V11__seed_relationships.sql` / `V12__seed_variant_claims.sql` regenerated; local Postgres (`blame-zeus-postgres-1`) reseeded from them. New file `ingestion/audit/audit-waivers.json` (1 entry, A3/Eurymachus-Polybus). New results dir `evaluation/results/2026-07-26T14-06-17Z__80bad75__p3-j1j2j3fgh-batch/` (`raw_responses.json`, `scores.json`, `report.md`, `diff.md`). `docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p3.md`: J1c and J2c checked off; J3a/b/c/e/f/g/h's "batched, not yet reseeded" notes superseded (landed in this pass, not the second J3a-e pass they originally cited); DoD checklist's A1/A3 counts updated. Operational note for future reseeds: kill any stray `core-api` process on :8080 first — `reseed-local.sh` refuses to start otherwise and gives no hint that an old background process, not a currently-relevant one, is the blocker. **I7 (commit) initially withheld, later committed as `dc22459`** ("Land Phase 3 P3 Track I third pass: J1/J2/J3f/J3g/J3h merged") — candidates were already committed in prior sessions; the regenerated V10-V12 migrations, the new waiver file, the results dir, and these doc updates were folded into that commit. |
