## Stage P3b — Track D follow-up: triage of A7's six findings (2026-07-27)

### DEV-100 — Worked A7's first sweep: 3 entities were not entities at all (an epithet, an ancient author, a metrical contraction), 1 gained its missing relationship, and 2 are true positives with genuinely nothing to extract — waived with written reasons rather than fabricated edges

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 3b, Track D follow-up (`docs/TODO2.md`) — the data batch DEV-099 opened when A7 went live. |
| **Original Plan** | DEV-099 recorded the six findings and diagnosed three of them in passing (`Argeiphontes` an epithet of Hermes, `Acusilaus` an author, `Diomed` a spelling variant), explicitly deferring the triage: "None of these were triaged here — that is a data batch, and this entry delivers the detector, not the fixes." No fix shape was decided for any of the six, and the other three (`Charybdis`, `Demodocus`, `Thisbe`) were guessed at as "likely genuine extraction misses". |
| **What Changed** | Each of the six was checked against the corpus before anything was edited, and they resolved into **three different fix shapes**, not one. **Three were not entities at all and were removed** from `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json`: `Argeiphontes` — the *Odyssey* reads "let us send forth **Hermes, the messenger, Argeiphontes**" and "the messenger Argeiphontes"; independently, the extraction's **own `variant_claims` candidates already say so**, carrying two rows of `subject_name='Hermes', claim_type='epithet', claim_value='Argeiphontes'` (`homer-iliad 24.77-24.119`, `homer-odyssey 10.274-10.320`) — so the entity was a duplicate of `Hermes` under an epithet, recorded now as an **alias**. `Diomed` — Brookes More's metrical contraction, appearing 10x in Ovid and **zero times in any other source**; *Metamorphoses* 13's debate over Achilles' arms assigns it the Iliadic Diomedes' own deeds ("his sleeping Rhesus, his unwarlike Dolon, Helenus taken, and Pallas gained by theft — all done by night and all with **Diomed**"), so it is the same person and likewise becomes an alias. `Acusilaus` — **an ancient mythographer Apollodorus cites**, not a mythological figure: "so says Acusilaus", "Hesiod and Acusilaus say that she was a daughter of Piren", "but Acusilaus says that he was earth-born". Removed outright with **no** alias, since he is not any entity's other name. (Corroboration that he is a cited authority rather than a figure: he already appears inside a *promoted* `V12` claim_value — Io's "daughter of Piren, according to Hesiod and Acusilaus" — as attribution text, not as an entity reference, so the removal breaks nothing.) **One gained the relationship it was missing**: `Thisbe` is the Ovidian heroine (11 mentions in *Metamorphoses* 4; the single *Iliad* hit is the homonymous Boeotian town in the catalogue of ships, which this project does not model), and `Pyramus` was **also** sitting at zero rows — the passage states reciprocity outright ("they grew fond, and **loved each other**"), so two rows were added at `ovid-metamorphoses 4.55-4.80`, `Pyramus loves Thisbe` and `Thisbe loves Pyramus`, using the `loves` relation Ovid rows already use (`Apollo loves Daphne`, `Polyphemus loves Galatea`). Both directions are justified here specifically *because* the text states mutual love, unlike the one-sided `Apollo loves Daphne` pattern. **Two were true positives with nothing to fix, and were waived rather than papered over**: `Charybdis` (17 mentions) is a sea hazard in a strait in every one of them — no parentage, marriage, or death is stated anywhere in the six sources (unlike `Scylla`, whose parents Apollodorus names at `E.7.20` and who has 17 rows); Odysseus passes but neither fights nor kills her, and there is no `encountered` relation in the vocabulary. `Demodocus` (11 mentions, all *Odyssey* 8) is the blind Phaeacian minstrel at Alcinous's court — "summon hither the divine minstrel, Demodocus" — with no kinship, marriage or death stated, and "court bard" has no honest match in the existing vocabulary (`servant_of` overstates a minstrel the king summons as an honoured performer). Both got `audit-waivers.json` entries with the full reasoning. Aliases landed in a **new additive migration**, `V14_1__add_epithet_and_spelling_aliases.sql` (the V9_2 precedent — V14 is hand-written and already applied, so it is not edited), mirrored into `ingestion/extraction/known_aliases.json` (37→39) as V14's own comment requires. Regenerated `V10`/`V11` (`seedgen --strict`; V10 1994→1991, V11 3293→3295); reseeded; `audit` clean-or-waived. |
| **Reason** | The user asked to fix the six findings. Checking each against the corpus first — rather than acting on DEV-099's own passing diagnoses — is what separated the three shapes: two of the six turned out to need *removal*, which is the opposite of the "add the missing rows" reading a coverage gap invites, and two needed **nothing at all**. Waiving those two is the honest outcome: A7 asks "does the corpus talk about an entity nothing references?", and for `Charybdis`/`Demodocus` the answer is yes *and there is still nothing to extract*. Inventing an `encountered` or `court_bard_of` relation to drive the count to zero would let an audit check dictate the data model, and fabricating a parentage is the DEV-047/DEV-095 line this project does not cross. |
| **Impact** | **Data + alias-migration change; no Kotlin, no schema, no gold-question change.** Files: `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` (−3), `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (+2), `known_aliases.json` (+2), new `core-api/src/main/resources/db/migration/V14_1__add_epithet_and_spelling_aliases.sql`, regenerated `V10`/`V11`, `ingestion/audit/audit-waivers.json` (+2), `scripts/reseed-local.sh` (see below). Live DB: `entities` 1991, `relationships` 3295, `entity_aliases` **37→39**, `variant_claims` 44 and `narrative_chunks` 3524 unchanged (embeddings preserved). Verified in the seeded DB, not just the JSON: both aliases resolve (`Argeiphontes`→`Hermes`, `Diomed`→`Diomedes`), all three removed names return zero rows from `entities`, and both `loves` rows are present at `4.55-4.80`. **Audit: A7 6 findings → 2, both waived; A5 still PASS** (its alias-shadowing check would have fired had the entities been left in place alongside the new aliases — removing them first is what keeps it green). A1 41, A2 residual 0, A3 1 waived, A4 119, A6 722 — all unchanged. **A latent reseed bug was found and fixed while landing this**: `scripts/reseed-local.sh` drops `entity_aliases` and clears a **hardcoded** version list from `flyway_schema_history` (`'10'..'17'`). A new `V14_1` that inserts into that dropped table would have been silently skipped on every subsequent reseed — V14 recreating the table empty of the new aliases, Flyway considering V14_1 already applied — so the aliases would have vanished at the next reseed with no error. `'14.1'` added to the list (Flyway's version key for `V14_1__` is `14.1`, not `14_1`), with a comment warning that any future migration writing into a dropped/truncated seed table needs the same treatment. `./gradlew :core-api:test` green. **Eval:** `evaluation/results/2026-07-27T21-21-29Z__3a3f894__p3b-a7-findings-triage/` — 14/16 (88%) → **13/16 (81%)**, above the 75% target, **zero stable PASS→FAIL regressions** per `compare.py`, all category floors met. The headline number understates the run: **four questions went flaky (Q3, Q8, Q13, Q15) and none of it is this change** — established by measurement, not assertion. **Q13 and Q15 returned identical conflict sets to the baseline whenever the request completed** (Q13 13 conflicts, Q15 22, in both runs), and their only failures were **client-side `transport error: timed out`** against the runner's `timeout_seconds: 60` (`evaluation/eval-config.json`): 6/6 of those requests succeeded in the baseline, 3/6 here. The cause was an **LLM-API latency episode during the run**, not the data — during the investigation a *light* SQL question ("Who is the father of Zeus?") measured **24.5 s** end-to-end and one Q15 attempt did not return within **7.5 minutes**, while the server itself was idle and healthy throughout (`/api/v1/sources` in 13 ms, 0% CPU, so nothing was hung server-side). Once the episode passed, **Q15 completed in 9.1 s with all 22 conflicts** and Q13 re-queried live returned the full correct answer on the SQL route with all 13 — i.e. both are comfortably inside the 60 s budget at normal latency, and the timeout is not structurally too tight. Q8's run-3 failure is the already-known malformed-recursive-CTE class from DEV-098, and Q3 answered correctly in all three runs (keyword-phrasing noise on the Odysseus/Polyphemus question, the same flake DEV-094 saw). Per the standing "never act on a single-run delta" rule, none of this is chased here. **Harness observation worth carrying forward, not fixed here:** a slow-API episode does not merely add latency, it silently converts passing CONFLICT questions into *false failures* and drops the reported score by 7 points — so an eval run should be treated as invalid, not as evidence, when transport timeouts appear in it. There is currently no signal in the report that distinguishes the two. Recorded in `docs/TODO2.md`. Results dir not yet committed, per this project's standing "never commit without being asked" convention. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-27 |

## Stage P4 — Prerequisite: DEV-038 (2026-07-28)

### DEV-101 — Fixed `write_output`'s blind overwrite of `variant_claims_candidates.json` with a merge-on-write that preserves review decisions; verified against the live file that all **71** existing promotions now survive a re-extraction

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2 Stage **P4**, first of the two prerequisites (`docs/TODO2.md` → Stage P4 → *Prerequisites*, "Do these **before** the first promotion batch"). Opening item of P4; P3b was closed the same day (see below). |
| **Original Plan** | DEV-038 recorded the hazard and left it: "`write_output` blind-overwrites `variant_claims_candidates.json` on every extraction run, so any re-run after a review pass destroys the `trust_tier=1` promotions it made… a known unaddressed risk, mitigated only by operator discipline." `docs/TODO2.md` set the decision explicitly: "Fix (merge-on-write preserving promoted rows, or a hard refuse-to-overwrite guard) or accept it with a written waiver and a documented backup step — but decide explicitly, do not inherit it silently." |
| **What Changed** | Chose **merge-on-write**, implemented as `_write_claims_preserving_review()` in `ingestion/extraction/run_extraction.py`, replacing the bare `_write_json` call for this one file. The hazard was quantified before writing any code: the live `variant_claims_candidates.json` holds **7,429 rows, of which 71 carry `trust_tier=1`** — hand-reviewed promotions under the ADR-004 gate, and the source of the 44 claims `seedgen` renders into `V12`. A single re-extraction destroyed all 71, with no warning, no error and no backup. Design decisions: (1) **The merge is one-directional — the extraction owns *which claims exist*, review owns their `trust_tier`.** A promoted row whose claim the extraction no longer produces is deliberately **not** resurrected, because reinstating a claim no source supports is a worse failure than losing a review decision; those drops are reported by subject/claim/source, never silent. (2) Identity is the 5-tuple `(subject_name, claim_type, claim_value, source_id, passage_ref)` — everything that identifies the claim — with `trust_tier` excluded on purpose as the mutable verdict layered on top. (3) **Any** tier differing from the `DEFAULT_TRUST_TIER = 3` staging value is treated as a review decision, not just `1`, so a future "rejected" tier is covered without another change here. (4) A corrupt or partial file raises `SystemExit` rather than being overwritten — such a file may still hold promotions, so clobbering it is the exact loss this guard exists to prevent. (5) The other two files `write_output` writes are left on the plain path, and the docstring records why: `entities_candidates.json` and `relationships_candidates.json` are **not** the curated artifacts `seedgen` reads (`entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json`, `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json`), so a re-run cannot clobber review there — the hazard is specific to variant_claims, where extraction output and promotion target are the *same file*. That asymmetry is not noted in DEV-038 and is what makes the fix narrow. |
| **Reason** | The user asked for the DEV-038 fix as P4's opening item, after closing P3b's bookkeeping. Merge-on-write was chosen over the alternative "hard refuse-to-overwrite guard" because a guard makes the promotion-heavy P4 loop *fail* on every re-extraction and pushes the operator back into manual file-shuffling — i.e. it converts a silent data-loss risk into a workflow blocker, rather than removing it. The waiver-plus-backup-step option was rejected for the same reason DEV-093 gave it a home in the first place: operator discipline is what already failed to be a control here. |
| **Impact** | **Tooling/data-safety change; no schema, no migration, no seeded-data change, no gold-question change, and nothing reseeded** — this code path only runs during offline extraction, which was not re-run. Files: `ingestion/extraction/run_extraction.py` (one call site + one new function), `ingestion/tests/test_run_extraction.py` (+7 tests). **Verified against the real file, not just fixtures**: a dry run that replays the live 7,429-row file as if freshly extracted (every row staged back to `trust_tier=3`) reports `carried over 71 reviewed promotion(s)` and leaves **71** promotions standing, where the old code left **0**. Tests: 13 in `test_run_extraction.py` (6 pre-existing, 7 new), covering preservation, new rows staying at tier 3, the deliberate non-resurrection of a dropped promotion, the carry-over count message, the drop WARNING naming the lost claim, the corrupt-file `SystemExit`, and a fresh directory with no existing file. **Verified non-vacuous** by forcing the merge lookup to `None` and confirming three tests fail, then restoring. Full ingestion suite green — **294 tests** (`tests/` + `audit/tests/`). **Not addressed here:** DEV-049, P4's second prerequisite, remains open — ADR-010's REFUSAL Q16/Q17 are by construction the zero-retrieval case and still surface as `serviceError`, so they cannot be authored yet. Work stopped after this item at the user's request; nothing committed. **⚠️ Two corrections, recorded at P4's close (Track J6, 2026-07-28), not rewriting the above:** (1) *"nothing committed"* is **wrong** — this work was committed as **`2e4ce40`** ("Fix DEV-038: preserve review decisions across re-extraction"); the sentence recorded the state at the moment of writing and was never updated. (2) The *"Not addressed here"* claim about DEV-049 was **investigated and did not reproduce** — see **#DEV-102**: the `@SystemMessage` reaches the model independently of `DefaultContentInjector`, 6/6 negative controls parsed cleanly with `serviceError: false`, and it closed as a verification note with no code change. The real obstacle to Q16/Q17 was different (neither draft question actually retrieves zero chunks), and both were replaced with live-verified alternatives in P4 Track F1 (#DEV-110). |
| **Date** | 2026-07-28 |

### DEV-102 — Track A (DEV-049 zero-retrieval refusal): reproduction against the live tree found no defect; re-scoped from a code fix to a verification note plus a scorer phrase-list extension, per A1f's own "if it does not reproduce, stop" rule

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2 Stage **P4**, Track A (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p4.md`), the second of the two prerequisites gating ADR-010's REFUSAL Q16/Q17. |
| **Original Plan** | Track A prescribed A1f (reproduce, live, before any code) then A2f–A4f: a custom `ContentInjector` in `RagConfig.kt` that injects an explicit "no passages were retrieved" sentinel instead of letting `DefaultContentInjector.inject()` short-circuit to the bare question on empty `contents`, plus a unit test on the injector alone. DEV-049 (Stage 6) had recorded this short-circuit as the cause of a `serviceError` on zero-retrieval questions, and DEV-101's carry-over repeated the claim that Q16/Q17 "are by construction the zero-retrieval case." A1f explicitly said to stop and re-scope to a verification note if the defect did not reproduce. |
| **What Changed** | **A1f reproduced the setup, not the bug.** Against the live stack (`core-api` already running on `:8080`, seeded DB, `LLM_CHAT_MODEL=claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`), `POST /api/v1/query` with DEV-049's own negative control ("What is the chemical formula for the caffeine molecule?") returned `serviceError: false`, a well-formed parsed JSON answer, and `citations: []` on every one of **6** repeated runs — `debug: true` confirmed `retrievedChunks: []` each time, so this is a genuine zero-retrieval case, and it never failed structured-output parsing. Draft Q16 ("What did Achilles look like physically?") and Q17 ("What were Zeus's exact words at the Trojan council?") were also run: **neither retrieves zero chunks against the live corpus at `min-score=0.5`** — Q16 retrieved one Iliad passage (19.369–19.386) and answered with a real citation about his armor/"glorious limbs"; Q17 retrieved two Iliad passages (8.1–8.26, 20.19–20.29) and answered with real quoted council speeches — so neither is currently the zero-retrieval case ADR-010's fixed question text assumes. Root cause of the non-reproduction, established by reading the actual `langchain4j-core-1.0.0` sources (`DefaultContentInjector.java`): `inject()` does return `chatMessage` unchanged when `contents.isEmpty()`, exactly as DEV-049 described — but the `@SystemMessage` carrying the JSON/refusal-shape instructions is attached by `AiServices` independently of the `ContentInjector`, which only ever augments the `UserMessage`. DEV-049 was diagnosed in Stage 6, before ADR-008 switched `RagAgent`'s chat model to `AnthropicChatModel`/Claude Haiku 4.5 (`git log` shows no change to `RagConfig.kt`/`RagAgent.kt`/`LangChain4jConfig.kt` since DEV-049 was written); the leading hypothesis is that the original failure was specific to whichever chat model/structured-output path Stage 6 used, and Claude Haiku 4.5 reliably follows the system message's JSON instruction even when the user message carries no retrieved content — a hypothesis recorded here, not proven, since Stage 6's exact conditions were not reproduced. Per A1f, **A2f/A3f/A4f (the `ContentInjector` fix and its unit test) were not built** — there is nothing to fix. **A5f was still run**, since it is a live-verification step independent of whether A2f's fix exists: none of the 6 caffeine-question answers matched any entry in `evaluation/runner/scoring.py::SOURCE_SILENCE_PHRASES` (e.g. "do not contain information about the chemical formula for caffeine... falls outside the scope of the available sources"). Per DEV-050's sanctioned extension point, added `"do not contain"` (the phrase common to 6/6 runs) and `"outside the scope"` (2/6) to the tuple, plus a regression test (`test_refusal_pass_live_verified_wording_p4_track_a5f`) pinned to the exact live wording. **A6f** confirmed on the one genuine zero-retrieval case: `serviceError: false`, `citations: []`, `routeDecision: RAG`, answer acknowledges the corpus is silent — stable across all 6 runs. **A7f**: `./gradlew :core-api:test` green, 189 tests (no Kotlin file was touched, so this is a baseline confirmation, not a regression check against new code). |
| **Reason** | A1f's rule — reproduce before writing any code, and re-scope rather than fix an unreproduced defect — is what this stage's operating principle ("root cause first — code fix only if still needed") requires. Building `RagConfig.kt`'s replacement `ContentInjector` anyway would have been an unnecessary change to every RAG request's augmentation path (A3f's own caution: "an unnecessary prompt edit is a change to every RAG answer, not just refusals") to fix a bug that no longer exists in the live tree, most likely because ADR-008's model swap already resolved it as a side effect. |
| **Impact** | **No Kotlin/production code change** — `RagConfig.kt`, `RagAgent.kt` untouched. `evaluation/runner/scoring.py`: `SOURCE_SILENCE_PHRASES` +2 entries (`"do not contain"`, `"outside the scope"`); `evaluation/runner/tests/test_scoring.py`: +1 test. `evaluation` test suite green — 24 tests in `test_scoring.py` (23 pre-existing + 1 new). **Definition-of-done impact: DEV-049 can be ticked** — the `serviceError` failure mode it named does not occur on the live tree, live-verified rather than assumed. **Carry-over for Track E, which must act on this:** ADR-010's Q16/Q17 text is fixed verbatim (`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md:1041-1042`) and both were authored on the assumption that they are zero-retrieval questions; **live evidence here shows neither retrieves zero chunks at the current corpus/`min-score=0.5`**, so authoring them as-is with `refusal_criteria.must_not_assert_answer: true` would fail them against their own live answers (both currently return substantive, correctly-cited content, not a refusal). Track E (E1/E2) must either confirm this is stable and pick different REFUSAL candidate questions that do genuinely zero-retrieve at the live corpus, or reconfirm Q16/Q17 retrieve nothing under whatever conditions E1/E2 actually runs them — this checklist's own A1f flagged Q17 as "doubtful" but the live run also overturned the "credible for Q16" half of that prediction. Not resolved here — Track A's scope was DEV-049's `serviceError` mechanism, not ADR-010's question selection. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-28 |

### DEV-103 — Track B: built the A8/A9/A10 audit emitters `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §5` and `TODO2.md:389` assumed already existed ("the audit package emits the ranking"); it did not

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2 Stage **P4**, Track B (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p4.md`) — audit emitters gating Track F (no ranking, no tranche selection) and Track G (V18 seed rows). |
| **Original Plan** | `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §5` step 1 and `TODO2.md:389` both assert "the audit package emits the ranking" and instruct P4's batches to rank the unreviewed groups by subject prominence. `grep -rn "prominence\|degree\|rank" ingestion/audit/*.py` returned nothing before this track — no such code existed. Track B's checklist (B1-B9) specified three new checks: A8 (subject prominence ranking), A9 (normalized claim_type distribution), A10 (per-group inventory), each conforming to `audit/contract.py`'s `NAME`+`run()` shape with no separate registration step. |
| **What Changed** | Three new modules, each pure-core-plus-loaders-plus-`run()`-adapter in the existing `relation_taxonomy.py`/`dropped_parents.py` mould: **`prominence.py` (A8)** — `rank_subjects(degree, mentions, group_counts, promoted_group_counts) -> list[SubjectRank]`, composite = degree + mention_count (both reported alongside it, no hidden weighting), subject names resolved through `known_aliases.json` + the live `entity_aliases` table (reusing `duplicate_entities.load_entity_aliases_from_db`, not re-deriving it, so `Sky`/`Ouranos` DEV-092 merges correctly). **`claim_type_distribution.py` (A9)** — groups every raw `claim_type` surface form by `extraction.claim_type_normalizer.normalize()` into canonical buckets (the 7-member `notable*` family, 648 rows, now shows as one canonical with its breakdown intact), plus a narrow mechanical duplicate-detector (`find_unmapped_duplicates`) that only flags raw forms differing purely by whitespace/underscore/case (`"notable claim"` vs `notable_claim`) with no existing alias row — deliberately **not** attempting the full semantic `notable*` collapse, left to Track G's human review (G1), the same restraint `relation_taxonomy.py` already documents for relation labels. **`group_inventory.py` (A10)** — one row per `(resolved subject, canonical claim_type)` group with candidate/source/value/promoted counts and the subject's A8 rank; a small committed baseline file (`group_inventory_baseline.json`, same convention as `audit-waivers.json`) records the group total and `zero_promoted` count on its first-ever run and rolls `lastZeroPromoted` forward each run after, since the P7(a)/(c) invariants ("total drifted", "zero_promoted increased") are inherently relative-to-last-run and nothing else in this package persists cross-run state. All three follow B9's inversion rule precisely: `run()` never returns a `Finding` for the ranking/distribution/inventory data itself (that goes into `summary` + each module's own `--output` JSON) — the *only* findings are A9's mechanical duplicates and A10's (a)/(b)/(c) anomalies, never one-per-row (unlike A2/A4/A6, which is exactly why those three need standing waiver policies to ever reach a clean exit, per `README.md`'s new inversion note). 35 new tests (13 A8, 10 A9, 12 A10) in the existing fixture/fake-cursor style, no live DB required; full `ingestion` suite green at **329** (294 + 35). |
| **Reason** | The user asked to implement Track B next, following Track A (DEV-102). Building three separate small modules rather than one combined "reporting" module matches every existing check's one-module-per-concern granularity and lets each be independently `--only`-run, waived, and tested. The mechanical-fold-only duplicate detector in A9 (rather than flagging every un-aliased surface form, or attempting to guess the full `notable*` semantic collapse) was chosen specifically to avoid the DEV-068/`relation_taxonomy.py` mistake of an audit check guessing at cross-concept collapses — a wrong guess here writes a wrong `claim_type_aliases` row that silently merges two distinct concepts. |
| **Impact** | **New tooling only — no data, schema, migration, prompt, or gold-question change; nothing reseeded, no eval needed** (matches every prior audit-check-only entry, e.g. DEV-099). New files: `ingestion/audit/prominence.py`, `ingestion/audit/claim_type_distribution.py`, `ingestion/audit/group_inventory.py`, their three test files, `ingestion/audit/group_inventory_baseline.json` (committed state, first-run values below). `ingestion/audit/README.md`: check count 7→10, new A8/A9/A10 sections, and B9's inversion note (`exit_code` ignores `severity`, so "reporting" checks must return zero findings on the normal path, not a `"warning"`-severity finding). **Live results (measured today, `2e4ce40`+DEV-102 tree, matching the Contracts section's own snapshot)**: **A8** ranks 2,088 subjects from candidates alone (top by composite: Zeus 842, Odysseus 718, Achilles 586) or 1,663 against the live DB's narrower relationship set. **A9**: 18 raw `claim_type` surface forms collapse to **17 canonical** (only `birth`→`parentage` is currently aliased), with **2** unmapped-duplicate findings — `'notable claim'` (14 rows) → proposed alias of `'notable_claim'` (268), `'notable act'` (9) → proposed alias of `'notable_act'` (56); the other 3 `notable*` members (`notable`, `notable_deed`, `notable_event`) are correctly left unflagged, matching the "no semantic guessing" design. **A10**: **835** canonical groups (not the raw-839 figure — see the module's own reconciliation note, verified by direct query that the entire 4-group reduction is exactly the `Aphrodite`/`Athena`/`Dionysus`/`Adonis` birth+parentage merges, zero from subject-alias merging), **3** with a promoted row (`Aphrodite/parentage`+`birth` merged, `Achilles/death`, `Io/parentage` — one fewer than the raw scheme's "4 groups" since Aphrodite's two raw groups collapse to one canonical group here), **832** zero-promoted; baseline file created and committed with these as its frozen starting figures. `python -m audit` end-to-end: all ten checks discovered and run, exit code reflects pre-existing unwaived findings from A1/A2/A3/A4/A6/A9 (none of that pre-existing state was touched here — F0c's standing-waiver decision, not yet made, is what will clean the gate). Ad-hoc report files generated while testing (`reports/2026-07-28*`) were deleted, not committed, per the DEV-094 convention — this package's `reports/` only commits labeled Track-I-style passes. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-28 |

### DEV-104 — Track C: re-keyed `02_verify_conflicts.ipynb`'s promotion workflow from position to the DEV-101 claim identity, after proving live (not hypothetically) that positional promotion is already unsafe

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2 Stage **P4**, Track C (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p4.md`) — the notebook re-key gating Track F (promoting by a stale index is silent data corruption, not an inconvenience). |
| **Original Plan** | Track C's checklist (C1-C6): prove the positional-promotion hazard live before touching anything (C1); re-key `review_group`/promotion to the same 5-tuple `_CLAIM_IDENTITY` DEV-101's merge-on-write uses, imported not redefined (C2); migrate the notebook's three committed historical `approved_indices += [...]` cells to the keyed scheme, first asserting they resolve to the same 71 rows under both schemes — stop and triage on any mismatch (C3); treat an unmatched key as an error, not a silent skip (C4); add a per-batch, append-only promotion log so a batch can be identified and reverted as a unit (C5); surface Track B's A8 ranking / A10 inventory in the group-listing cell, reading the JSON artifact from disk rather than importing the audit package (C6, gated on B7's artifact, which Track B had just delivered). |
| **What Changed** | **C1's hazard, proven, not assumed**: replayed `run_extraction.py`'s real `_write_claims_preserving_review` merge against a *shuffled* copy of the live 7,429-row file (same claims, different order — what a re-extraction with a different segment-processing order produces) and checked what the three historical cells' 73 committed indices now resolve to. **73 of 73 changed** — e.g. index `743` used to be `('Aphrodite', 'parentage', 'child of Zeus', ...)`, after the reorder it resolves to `('Agamemnon', 'death', 'killed by Aegisthus', ...)` — with no error, no warning. DEV-101's own key-based merge worked correctly throughout the same test (all 71 promoted rows' *keys* survived), confirming the hazard is specific to a notebook re-deriving positions across sessions, not a DEV-101 regression. **C3 found the drift was not hypothetical**: resolving those same 73 historical indices against **today's** live file (no simulation at all) already disagrees with what's actually promoted — only **71 of 73** currently land on a `trust_tier=1` row, and one promoted row (`Aphrodite`/`birth`) is covered by none of the three cells. Per C3's own instruction ("a mismatch means the file already drifted — stop and triage, do not proceed"), the three stale index cells were **not** used as the migration source; instead, the notebook's new single keyed cell lists the **71 keys the live file actually has at `trust_tier=1` right now** (verified: `Achilles/death` 27, `Aphrodite/parentage` 33, `Aphrodite/birth` 1, `Io/parentage` 10 — matching the Contracts section exactly), which is the real, current review decision DEV-101 has been correctly preserving by key. **C2**: `review_group()` now imports `_claim_key`/`_CLAIM_IDENTITY` from `extraction.run_extraction` (never redefined) and its paste-ready output line changed from `approved_indices += [i, i, ...]` to `approved_keys += [(subject, claim_type, claim_value, source_id, passage_ref), ...]`. **C4**: the promotion-write cell now builds a `key_to_index` map and calls `raise SystemExit(...)` naming every unmatched key if any `approved_keys` entry doesn't resolve — verified live against both the real 71-key migration (zero unmatched) and a deliberately bogus key (correctly caught). **C5**: the write cell appends `{batchLabel, date, keys[], groupCount}` to a new committed `ingestion/audit/promotion_log.json` on every run that contains at least one *newly* promoted key — migrating the already-promoted 71 logs nothing, since nothing changed, which was verified rather than assumed. **C6**: the group-listing cell now reads `ingestion/audit/group_inventory_findings.json` (A10's artifact, generated via `python -m audit.group_inventory --db`) from disk — never importing `audit.*` — and sorts every `(subject, claim_type)` group by its subject's A8 rank instead of alphabetically, falling back to the old alphabetical dump with an instructive message if the artifact is missing. Verified live: top of the ranked listing is `Odysseus` (rank 1, matching A8's own live top-ranked subject). None of the notebook cells were executed end-to-end via `nbconvert --execute` (B6's hand-add cell is non-idempotent and would have duplicated the Io rows in the live file); instead every changed cell's logic was verified against a **scratch copy** of the live candidates file, never the real one, mirroring DEV-101's own dry-run-first discipline. |
| **Reason** | The user asked to implement Track C next, following Track B (DEV-103), now that B7's artifact existed to unblock C6. Proving the hazard with the actual merge function against actually-shuffled real data (rather than reasoning about it) is what this checklist's operating principle demands throughout ("root cause first" / "evidence first, same as Track A"), and it paid off immediately: the drift found in C3 was real, already present in the live file, not a constructed worst case — meaning the old notebook's positional record was already silently wrong for 2 of its 3 historical cells before this track touched anything. |
| **Impact** | **Tooling-only change to the review workflow; no data, schema, migration, or gold-question change; nothing reseeded, no eval needed** — the live `variant_claims_candidates.json` file's `trust_tier` values are untouched (the migration cell, when actually run against the real file, promotes zero *new* rows since all 71 keys already resolve to `trust_tier=1`, verified). Files: `ingestion/notebooks/02_verify_conflicts.ipynb` (group-listing cell rewritten for C6; `review_group` rewritten for C2; `approved_indices: list[int]` → `approved_keys: list[tuple]`; three stale positional historical cells replaced by one markdown cell documenting the C1/C3 findings plus one keyed cell with the live 71 keys; final write cell rewritten for C2/C4/C5), new `ingestion/audit/group_inventory_findings.json` (A10's committed artifact, generated live: 835 groups, 3 promoted, 832 zero-promoted — matching DEV-103's own figures exactly, confirming A8/A10 are stable across the two tracks). `promotion_log.json` was **not** created by this entry — it's created lazily by the write cell's own logic on the first run that contains a genuinely new promotion, which did not happen here (the migration is a no-op against the live file by design). Full `ingestion` test suite unaffected (still 329 — no `.py` test files changed by this track, only the notebook), notebook validated with `nbformat.validate` (20 cells, well-formed) after every edit. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-28 |

### DEV-105 — Track D: transport/latency signal now surfaces in `report.md`/`scores.json`, and `compare.py` refuses a transport-dirty run outright; retroactively verified against the real DEV-100 incident

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2 Stage **P4**, Track D (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p4.md`) — the transport/latency signal that makes Track F's gate trustworthy (F1g/F0d depend on it). |
| **Original Plan** | Track D's checklist (D1-D5): record per-request elapsed seconds in `raw_responses.json` (D1); propagate `_runnerNote` into `scores.json` per question/run so `compare.py` can act on it (D2); `report.md` gains a top-line transport-error banner above the score table, a per-question latency column, and the slowest-request figure (D3); `compare.py` refuses to treat a transport-dirty run as an accepted baseline or candidate without an explicit override flag (D4); TDD covering both the dirty and clean cases (D5). `TODO2.md:455-464`'s open item — before this track, `report.py` never read `_runnerNote` at all (verified by the Contracts section's own grep), so a DEV-100-style API slow episode was indistinguishable from a real quality regression. |
| **What Changed** | **D1**: `runner/__main__.py`'s `query_once` now times the whole call (including retries) with `time.monotonic()` and attaches `raw["_elapsedSeconds"]` via a new `_with_elapsed` wrapper around every return path (success, 4xx, and the exhausted-retries synthetic error) — a timer around the existing logic, no control-flow change. **D2**: `report.py`'s `_per_run_entry` now carries `runner_note`/`elapsed_seconds` straight from the raw response into every `scores.json` per-run entry. **D3**: `report.md` gained (a) a top-line banner — `"⚠️ **N request(s) failed on transport; this run is invalid as evidence (DEV-100).**"` plus one line per failed request — placed immediately after the title and **before** the "Overall (pessimistic...)" summary line, so the banner is unavoidable rather than requiring a reader to scroll to the aggregate first; (b) a `latency` column in the per-question table showing each question's **worst-observed** elapsed time across all runs (computed independently of `worst_run_index`, since the slow outlier and the score-worst run aren't necessarily the same run); (c) a "Slowest request: Q<id>, run <n> — <seconds>s" summary line. **D4**: `compare.py` gained `transport_error_notes(scores) -> list[str]` (reads D2's `runner_note` field) and `main()` now refuses — exit code 2, an explanatory `stderr` message, **no `diff.md` written** — when either the baseline or candidate `scores.json` carries any transport error, unless `--allow-transport-errors` is passed. **D5**: 10 new tests across `test_report.py` (+2: clean run → no banner + latency column populated; dirty run → banner present above the aggregate, correct `scores.json` fields, unaffected sibling question), `test_compare.py` (+5: empty/populated `transport_error_notes`, refusal on a dirty baseline, refusal on a dirty candidate, override flag bypasses the refusal), and a new `test_main.py` (+3, since no test file previously existed for `query_once`'s retry/transport logic at all: elapsed time recorded on success, on a transport-error-with-retry path — asserting both attempts' sleep time is included and the retry actually fired twice — and on a 4xx no-retry path). **Retroactively verified against the real incident, not only synthetic fixtures**: reconstructed what the P3b close's `scores.json` would carry under D2 by pairing its own `raw_responses.json`'s three real `_runnerNote` entries onto the matching per-run positions, then ran the reconstructed dict through `transport_error_notes` — it correctly names all three (`Q13 run 2`, `Q15 run 0`, `Q15 run 1`), exactly matching the ground truth DEV-100 already established by hand. Full `evaluation` suite green — **74 tests** (64 + 10 new; 1 pre-existing skip unrelated to this track). |
| **Reason** | The user asked to implement Track D next, the last of the four tracks that "fan out from minute one." Placing the banner **above** the aggregate summary (not just present somewhere in the file) directly targets what DEV-100 actually cost: an operator reading the headline "13/16, 81%" number before noticing three requests never completed. Reporting worst-observed latency **per question across all runs**, rather than only the worst-scoring run's latency, was chosen because the two are independent signals — a transport error can (and did, in the real incident) land in a run that isn't the score-worst run, so tying the column to `worst_run_index` would have hidden exactly the outlier this column exists to surface. `compare.py` refusing outright (no `diff.md` at all) rather than writing a diff with a warning was chosen because a written-but-caveated diff is still an artifact an operator can accidentally treat as evidence; refusing to produce one at all removes that failure mode, matching this stage's "invalid, not evidence" framing precisely. |
| **Impact** | **Tooling-only change to the evaluation harness; no data, schema, migration, gold-question, or seeded-data change — no reseed, no live eval run needed to validate this** (verified entirely against injected fake transports and the already-committed P3b artifact). Files: `evaluation/runner/__main__.py` (`_with_elapsed` + `time` import), `evaluation/runner/report.py` (`_transport_error_entries`, `_max_latency_by_question`, `_slowest_request`, `_latency_cell`, `_per_run_entry` +2 fields, `_render_md` banner + latency column + slowest-request line), `evaluation/runner/compare.py` (`transport_error_notes`, `--allow-transport-errors` flag, `main()` refusal), `evaluation/runner/tests/test_report.py` (+2), `evaluation/runner/tests/test_compare.py` (+5), new `evaluation/runner/tests/test_main.py` (+3). **Backward compatible**: every pre-existing test in `test_report.py`/`test_compare.py` still passes unmodified — old fixtures with no `_elapsedSeconds`/`_runnerNote`/`runner_note` render a clean report (`—` latency cells, no banner) and compare normally (no refusal), so this is additive, not a breaking schema change to `scores.json`. **Not addressed here**: retrofitting the already-committed `2026-07-27T21-21-29Z__3a3f894__p3b-a7-findings-triage/scores.json` on disk with the new `runner_note`/`elapsed_seconds` fields — that file is a historical artifact from before D2 existed and is left as originally committed; the retroactive check above was done in memory only, to verify D4's logic against real data, not to mutate a committed results directory. F0d (not this entry's scope) is what actually re-runs and re-baselines against this run. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-28 |

### DEV-107 — Track G: V18 collapses the `notable*` claim_type family into one canonical, `notable_claim`, after real-content review found no claim/deed split in the data

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2 Stage **P4**, Track G (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p4.md`) — a Track F prerequisite, gated on Track B's B5/B6 normalized distribution (delivered by DEV-103). |
| **Original Plan** | Track G's checklist (G1-G4): decide the canonical form for the 7-member `notable*` family (`notable_claim` 268, `notable` 218, `notable_deed` 75, `notable_act` 56, `"notable claim"` 14, `"notable act"` 9, `notable_event` 8 — 648 rows total), **review-gated per ADR-019 Track D** — explicitly posing the open question of whether it is one canonical type or genuinely two ("a *claim about* a figure vs. a *deed done by* one"); write `V18__add_claim_type_aliases_notable.sql` in the V8_2/V9_2 style (G2); confirm no Kotlin/code change is needed since `seedgen`/`ConflictLookup` already normalize (G3); verify the effect through Track F1's reseed, not a standalone one (G4, deferred). |
| **What Changed** | **G1's decision, made from real data, not guessed**: pulled and read random samples (9 per label across two passes, seeded for reproducibility) of actual `subject_name`/`claim_value` pairs from all seven surface forms. The candidate "claim vs. deed" split does not exist in the data — every one of the seven labels freely mixes active deeds (`notable_event`: "Seized and bound the Cercopes"), passive events (`notable_event`: "fled when Achilles came to lift his kine"), and asserted claims (`"notable claim"`: "Claims credit for Troy's impending fall through his wisdom and strategy") within the *same* label, not cleanly separated across labels. Decision: **one canonical type**. Canonical name **`notable_claim`** — the plurality surface form (268/648 rows), chosen by the same majority-frequency rule `claim_type_distribution.py`'s (A9, DEV-103) `find_unmapped_duplicates` already applies to its own narrower mechanical-duplicate proposals, extended here to the full semantic family for one consistent decision rule rather than a separate aesthetic judgment call. **G2**: `V18__add_claim_type_aliases_notable.sql` inserts the other six surface forms (`notable`, `notable_deed`, `notable_act`, `"notable claim"`, `"notable act"`, `notable_event`) as aliases of `notable_claim`, lower-cased/untrimmed-as-stored (matching every candidate row's actual casing — `normalize()` lowercases the *input* at lookup time, not the stored alias key, so this matches V8_2/V9_2's own convention exactly). `afterMigrate__grant_app_user.sql` confirmed schema-wide (`GRANT SELECT ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA public`) — verified by reading the file, not assumed, so no follow-up grant is needed. **Syntax/semantics-verified via a rolled-back transaction** against the live DB (the V17 precedent, since V18 has not been applied anywhere yet): all 6 rows insert cleanly and read back correctly, then rolled back — the live DB and `flyway_schema_history` are untouched, so the file remains freely editable until Track F1's real reseed applies it. **Independently confirmed via `./gradlew :core-api:test`**: the Testcontainers integration suite boots a *fresh* Postgres and runs every migration from `V1` through `V18` for real on every run — 189 tests green, meaning V18 is now proven Flyway-valid against a real fresh apply, not just a manual rollback. **G3 verified, not just asserted**: `grep -n normalize seedgen/variant_claims_gen.py` confirms line 39 already calls `normalize(alias_map, c["claim_type"])` at promotion time regardless of when `claim_type_aliases` gained the new rows — no Kotlin change, no Python change, exactly as G3 predicted. **G4 deferred**, per its own instruction — the regenerated `V12` zero-collapsed-surface-form check happens inside Track F1's reseed loop, not standalone. |
| **Reason** | The user asked to implement Track G next. Reading real sample content before deciding the canonical shape (rather than picking a plausible-sounding split from the label names alone) is this checklist's own operating principle applied to a genuine editorial judgment call — `relation_taxonomy.py`'s own docstring warns against exactly this kind of unverified semantic collapse, and the same caution applies in reverse: refusing to *invent* a split that isn't in the data is as important as refusing to *merge* concepts that are. Choosing the canonical name by the same majority-frequency rule A9 already uses (rather than by which spelling "reads better") keeps the decision mechanical and auditable rather than a second, unrelated matter of taste. |
| **Impact** | **New migration only — no seeded-data change yet** (the live DB and `flyway_schema_history` are untouched; V18 applies for the first time at Track F1's reseed, per the V17 precedent). Files: new `core-api/src/main/resources/db/migration/V18__add_claim_type_aliases_notable.sql`. No Kotlin, no Python production code changed (G3's finding). `./gradlew :core-api:test` green — 189 tests, including a fresh Testcontainers apply of V1-V18. **Once F1 reseeds**, this is expected to move the "≥4 canonical claim_types" exit-gate figure from candidate-space canonical count 17 (DEV-103's snapshot) down by six (the six now-aliased forms merge into `notable_claim`), and separately *up* toward the ≥4 target once `notable_claim` itself gets its first promoted row — those are two different effects on the same count, worth distinguishing when F1 reports it. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-28 |

### DEV-108 — Track H: 12 more GAP-002 bucket-1 names landed, 3 translation-spelling aliases (one caught only via the full audit loop after a self-introduced duplicate), 3 more bucket-2 collisions confirmed, and the residual ~330 names explicitly bucketed/deferred

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2 Stage **P4**, Track H (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p4.md`) — the GAP-002 unknown-name long-tail triage, independent of Track F but merging serially through it. |
| **Original Plan** | Track H's checklist (H1-H6): re-run A2/A7 for the current unknown-name count (H1); bucket every name per `DATA-GAPS.md`'s four buckets (H2); work bucket 1 only, in small source-verified batches, never bulk-add (H3); work DEV-098's open generalization — does the same near-miss corruption recur elsewhere (H4); watch for new A3 cycles from graph growth (H5); explicit written deferral for whatever isn't worked, never a silent close (H6). |
| **What Changed** | **H1**: re-ran A2/A7 live — 359 distinct unknown names (down from the docs' stale 362 figure, drifted from intervening edits since DEV-100); A7 unchanged at 2 waived findings. **H2**: built a ranked full list (359 entries) and a heuristic noise classifier (lowercase-leading / possessive-phrase / "sons of X" patterns) to separate likely-noise from likely-name candidates before any manual review, rather than eyeballing the raw list. **H3**: for the top candidates by reference count, pulled every actual candidate relationship row (not just the name) and checked it against known mythology and, where flagged, the raw corpus text — the same discipline DEV-096 applied to `Arges`/`Steropes`, extended here as the default method rather than an exception. Result: **12 entities added** (`Alcinous`, `Arete`, `Pegasus`, `Amphitrite`, `Chiron`, `Chrysaor`, `Argus Panoptes`, `Nessus`, `Rhadamanthys`, `Epaphus`, `Tisiphone`, `Hippolyte` — types assigned by matching existing precedent: `hero` for Zeus's mortal-mother sons per Heracles/Perseus, `monster` for centaurs/giants per Geryon/Medusa/Cerberus with `subtype` preserving the specific kind, `other_god` for minor deities per Triton/Eos/Selene), unlocking 75 previously-dropped relationship rows. **3 more names resolved as translation-spelling aliases of already-confirmed entities, not new gaps** — `Aesculapius`→`Asclepius`, `Phorcus`→`Phorcys` (checked deliberately, before adding, once the pattern was recognized), and `Helios`→`Helius`, which was **not** caught before adding: it went into the entities file as bucket 1, and only surfaced as a duplicate when this batch's own fix-loop verification ran the **full** `python -m audit` (not just the checks that seemed relevant to entity additions) — A1's transliteration pass fuzzy-matched the newly-added `Helios` against the already-confirmed `Helius` at 83.3, same sun god, same parentage (Hyperion), same children (Circe, Aeetes), across the same sources. Reverted `Helios` from the entities file and re-added it as an alias instead (`Helius` kept canonical, being the one already confirmed). **3 more bucket-2 (namesake collision) confirmations**, found the same way DEV-096 found `Arges`/`Steropes` — checking a candidate's own rows for internal identity conflicts rather than trusting a single high reference count: `Oenomaus` (the king of Pisa conflated with an unrelated Trojan warrior killed at `homer-iliad` 5.703), `Hippolytus` (at least three distinct figures — a Giant, a son of Helios, and Theseus's son), `Ascalaphus` (two distinct figures split cleanly by parentage — Acheron/Orphne's Underworld gardener vs. Ares/Astyoche's Argonaut). Two more, `Coronis` and `Eurynome`, were investigated and **left undecided** — each mostly consistent with one well-known figure but with one or two rows that don't fit, not resolved either way rather than guessed. **H4**: A7 still shows exactly the same 2 waived findings after this batch — no new corruption of the `Arges`/`Ares` shape found; the generalization stays formally open (no negative can ever fully close it) but this pass adds no new positive evidence for it either. **H5**: A3 cycle count unchanged at 92 (candidates) / 1 waived (db) after the additions — no new cycle surfaced. **Verified through the real fix loop, three passes**: `seedgen --strict` → `reseed-local.sh` → `python -m audit`, run three times because the `Helios` fix required a second pass, and `scripts/reseed-local.sh`'s `CLEAR_HISTORY_SQL` needed `'18'` and `'19'` added for a third — the exact "Detected resolved migration not applied to database" Flyway out-of-order failure DEV-100 already hit once for `'14.1'`, recurring here because V19 (this track's own migration) also writes into the `entity_aliases` table the script drops every reseed. Final state, live-verified: A1 41 pairs (baseline, zero touching the new entities), A5 clean (42 `entity_aliases`, 0 findings), A9/A10 consistent with DEV-107's snapshot, `./gradlew :core-api:test` green (189 tests, independent Testcontainers apply of V1-V19). **H6**: two concrete leads flagged for a future batch rather than worked here — Hesiod's *Theogony* 211-232 personified abstractions (`Famine`, `Doom`, `Woe`, `Toil`, `Sorrows`, `Battles`, and 14 more, which read as noise at a glance but are genuine named figures in the source text) and the Hecatoncheires cluster (`Cottus`, `Gyes`, `Briareus`, collective `Hecatoncheires` — siblings of the already-resolved `Arges`/`Brontes`/`Steropes` trio). Everything else — ~330 names — bucketed explicitly rather than left as one undifferentiated pile: bucket 3 noise (`<UNKNOWN>` + ~98 heuristically-flagged descriptive phrases) now further split out a **place-name sub-class** (`Asia`, `Sparta`, `Olympus`, `Troy`/`Ilios`, ... — the `entities.type` enum has no "place" value at all, a schema-scope fact worth naming, not a triage miss); a **collective/group-noun sub-class** (`Titans`, `Giants`, `Amazons`, `Erinyes`, ...) whose individual members may be separately addable but whose plural form isn't itself one entity; and ~230 remaining individual-name candidates, several flagged by name for future high-value batches (`Tiresias`, `Narcissus`, `Calchas`, `Alecto`, `Enceladus`, `Talos`). |
| **Reason** | The user asked to implement Track H next. Checking every high-reference candidate's actual rows against known mythology *before* adding it — rather than adding by reference count alone and fixing problems found later — is what caught `Oenomaus`/`Hippolytus`/`Ascalaphus` as collisions before they entered the graph. Still, `Helios` slipped through that same discipline (its own candidate rows looked perfectly self-consistent — the conflict was against an entity *outside* the batch, not within it), which is exactly why H3/H5's instruction to run the real fix loop — not just eyeball the diff — matters: the full `python -m audit` run is what caught it, not the manual review. Recording it in the DEV entry as a caught-and-fixed mistake, not silently folding it into a clean-looking final diff, matches this project's own standing practice of showing the process, not just the outcome (DEV-096's own `Arges`/`Steropes` reversal is the precedent). Leaving `Coronis`/`Eurynome` undecided rather than guessing either way follows the same "don't guess, defer" discipline Track G applied to the `notable*` family. |
| **Impact** | **Data + migration change; no schema, no gold-question change.** Files: `ingestion/extraction/output/entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` (+12), `ingestion/extraction/known_aliases.json` (+3: `Aesculapius`, `Phorcus`, `Helios`), new `core-api/src/main/resources/db/migration/V19__add_entity_aliases_translation_spellings.sql`, regenerated `V10`/`V11` (2003 entities, 3365 relationships), `scripts/reseed-local.sh` (`CLEAR_HISTORY_SQL` +`'18'`+`'19'`, the same latent-bug class DEV-100 already fixed once for `'14.1'` — any future migration writing into a table this script drops/truncates needs the same treatment, and ordering requires clearing every version from the first cleared one through the highest one, not just the ones touching a dropped table). Live DB (local, reseeded with explicit user confirmation before stopping the running server): entities **1991→2003**, relationships **3295→3365**, `entity_aliases` **39→42**, `variant_claims` 44 and `narrative_chunks` 3524 unchanged (embeddings preserved). `docs/DATA-GAPS.md` GAP-002 updated with this pass's detail, the bucket-2 growth, and the newly-named place-name schema gap; `docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p4.md` Track H boxes ticked. **No eval run** — H's own checklist scope is the audit/data-quality fix loop (seedgen → reseed → audit), not the score-driven eval loop, which is Track F's; a future F batch's eval run will be the first to measure this pass's effect on gold-question scores. `python -m audit`/`./gradlew :core-api:test` both green as detailed above. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-28 |

### DEV-109 — Track F0: tranche-priority rule + frozen top-20 (F0a), bounded GAP-001 promotion tranche (F0b), a standing-waiver policy that discovered the gate was blocked by more than the two named checks (F0c), and a clean 3-run baseline that beat the P3b close (F0d)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2 Stage **P4**, Track F0 (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p4.md`) — the four decisions (+one clean re-run) that gate Track F1, the first real promotion batch. |
| **Original Plan** | F0a: resolve the `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §5` ("beyond parentage/death") vs. `TODO2.md` ("parentage is the largest unworked dimension") conflict as one mechanical, repeatable tranche-selection rule, with the top-20 prominence list frozen from A8's first clean run. F0b: decide GAP-001 Root cause 3's promotion half — a bounded first tranche, a sampling rule, or an explicit P5b deferral, all in writing. F0c: decide whether A1's and A6's "permanent-by-design" residue gets a standing category-level waiver, since without one the gate is red on every run. F0d: re-run the P3b close cleanly (no data change first) as the F1 baseline, checking for zero transport errors, a DATA≥4/5 floor-holding reproduction, and Q13/Q15's known 13/22 conflict counts. |
| **What Changed** | **F0a**: wrote the tranche rule as a 4-tier mechanical procedure over A8's ranking + A10's inventory — Tier 1 (subject in the frozen top-20 AND claim_type not yet promoted anywhere — advances both exit-gate numbers at once), Tier 2 (claim_type not yet promoted, subject outside top-20 — advances the type-count gate), Tier 3 (subject in top-20, claim_type already has coverage elsewhere — typically parentage/death, which is exactly where GAP-001's backlog and F0b's tranche live), Tier 4 (everything else), filled in that order until the tranche is full; within any group, distinct `(claim_value, source_id)` pairs are reviewed before a repeat of an already-promoted pair, per the *Contracts* section's dedup-waste finding (27 of 71 promotions produced no table row). This resolves the §5-vs-TODO2 conflict by not picking a side: Tier 1/2 satisfies §5's "new types" instinct, Tier 3 satisfies TODO2's "parentage is the biggest backlog" observation, applied to whichever gate is actually still short. **Top-20 frozen from a fresh A8 run** (post-Track H, since H changed the graph): `Odysseus, Zeus, Achilles, Heracles, Hector, Agamemnon, Apollo, Telemachus, Diomedes, Patroclus, Athena, Menelaus, Penelope, Poseidon, Hermes, Aphrodite, Priam, Hera, Nestor, Ajax` — with the extraction sentinel `<UNKNOWN>` (which A8 ranks at #19 by raw mention count, an artifact worth a future J-item to exclude from `prominence.py` itself) skipped rather than counted, pulling `Nestor`/`Ajax` up from #20/#21. Only 2 of the 20 (`Achilles`, `Aphrodite`) currently have any promoted coverage. **F0b**: decided a **bounded first tranche** — from A6's live per-row record (739 dropped rows today, up from 697 as Track H's growth added more candidate rows), the 49 rows whose *child* is in the frozen top-20 become F1's explicit working set; the remaining ~690 are deferred with a written reason (not silently, not permanently — GAP-001's own docs already reject a permanent waiver for this class). **F0c**: while measuring what actually blocks a clean exit today (not the checklist's original snapshot), found the gate is blocked by **six** checks, not the two F0c names — A1 (82 findings), A2 (347), A3 (93), A4 (9), A6 (739), A10 (1) — only A5/A7(already waived)/A8/A9 are clean. Investigated each rather than blanket-waiving: **A1** (fuzzy-dup residue) matches F0c's "permanent by design" framing and DATA-GAPS.md's own established precedent of treating it as long-tail — standing waiver, all 82 current findings, explicitly scoped to *today's* pairs only (a future new pair, like this stage's own `Helios`/`Helius`, is a genuine new finding, not auto-covered). **A6** waived exactly per F0b's tranche decision — the 49 top-20-child rows stay unwaived as F1's queue, the rest waived with F0b's reasoning. **A2** (GAP-002's unknown-name backlog) and **A4** (V17's abandoned relation_aliases seed-row backlog) were *not* named by F0c's text but block the gate today all the same — waived with explicit non-permanent framing pointing at their real owning tracks (Track H for A2, whoever resumes V17's seed rows for A4), so P4's gate isn't blocked by a different stage's or track's unfinished business. **A10**'s one finding (`groups_total` drifted 835→798) is Track G's V18 collapse working exactly as designed, not a regression — waived, and the baseline file re-anchored to 798/795 so the same finding doesn't recur every future run. **A3 was deliberately left OUT of the standing-waiver policy** — its own docstring calls a cycle "near-certain" a reversed-edge bug, unlike A1/A6's residue framing, and sampling its 92 candidate-layer cycles found a median length of **37 edges** (one cycle spans 15 edges through completely unrelated genealogies), meaning these are overwhelmingly large-SCC artifacts of candidate-space name collisions — the same root cause as GAP-002's bucket-2 namesake collisions, just manifesting as graph cycles instead of lookup ambiguity — not simple one-line direction fixes, and not something F0's decision-only scope should bulk-waive. Only one 2-cycle (`Zeus↔Athena`, both directions independently attested) looks like a real candidate for individual investigation. **The gate does not fully exit 0 after F0c** — A3's 92 findings remain red, by deliberate choice, flagged as its own future track rather than smoothed over. Verified live: `python -m audit` now shows A1/A2/A4/A10 `WAIVED`, A6 `FINDINGS` (49 unwaived, exactly the frozen tranche), A3 unchanged `FINDINGS` (92). **F0d**: ran `python -m runner --runs 3 --label p4-f0d-baseline` against the current tree — **not literally eval-identical to `3a3f894`/`2e4ce40` as the checklist assumed**, since Tracks A-H happened since that assumption was written and Track H specifically changed the seeded graph (entities 1991→2003, relationships 3295→3365); recorded as a genuine baseline for the *current* tree rather than pretending otherwise. Result: **15/16 = 94%**, the project's best result to date, beating the P3b close (13/16=81%) outright — zero `_runnerNote` entries (`grep -c` confirms 0, and Track D's banner correctly rendered nothing), DATA 5/5 (100%, exceeds the ≥4/5 bar), CONFLICT 4/4 (100%), all floors met, **zero flaky questions**, and Q13/Q15 returned their DEV-100-established 13 and 22 conflicts identically across all 3 runs. Only Q11 (MIXED) lost its author point in the worst run — a single known point loss, no floor breach, not investigated further here (out of F0's scope). Results dir: `evaluation/results/2026-07-28T17-01-35Z__e4a369a__p4-f0d-baseline/`. |
| **Reason** | The user asked to implement Track F0 next. F0a's tier rule was written to be genuinely mechanical (not a per-batch judgement call, as the checklist requires) by keying entirely off two already-built, already-verified Track B artifacts (A8, A10) rather than inventing a new scoring signal. F0c's expanded scope (six checks, not two) came from actually running the audit and reading the real finding counts rather than trusting the checklist's original framing, which was written before Tracks B/G/H existed and could no longer see what the current tree actually blocks on — the same "record any delta rather than code against a stale figure" discipline this whole checklist asks for elsewhere, applied to F0c's own scope. Refusing to blanket-waive A3 after sampling its cycles (rather than waiving all six blocking checks uniformly for gate-cleanliness) follows directly from the "root cause first, never guess" principle: a check whose own docstring calls its findings near-certain bugs is not the same shape as A1/A6's accepted, reviewed residue, and treating them the same would have been expedient, not honest. |
| **Impact** | **Data + waiver-policy change; no schema, no gold-question change, no new migration.** Files: `ingestion/audit/audit-waivers.json` grew from 3 to **1050** entries (5 shared reasons across the newly-waived categories, mechanically generated from live findings, not hand-typed — 624K on disk, a real consequence of a fine-grained per-finding waiver format applied to a bulk decision, noted rather than hidden), `ingestion/audit/group_inventory_baseline.json` re-anchored (835/832→798/795), `ingestion/audit/prominence_ranking.json`/`group_inventory_findings.json` refreshed. New results dir `evaluation/results/2026-07-28T17-01-35Z__e4a369a__p4-f0d-baseline/` (not yet committed — F1g's job to reference, per the checklist). No entity/relationship/variant_claims data changed by F0 itself (F0b/F0c only *decided* which of the already-existing findings to act on later vs. waive now). `python -m audit` exit code is still non-zero (A3's 92 findings, deliberately unwaived) — **F1e's "clean or waived" gate is not yet fully green**, and this is recorded as an explicit, known state rather than papered over; F1 either works a batch that doesn't touch A3-flagged subjects, or A3 gets its own dedicated pass first. `./gradlew :core-api:test` (189) and both Python suites (329 ingestion + 74 evaluation) green throughout — none of F0's changes touch application or extraction code. |

### DEV-110 — Track F1: batch 1 promotes 95 rows across 26 groups, meets the ≥4-canonical-claim-types gate outright, catches 5 extraction errors and one eval-scorer gap live, lands 4 ADR-010 gold questions with zero stable regressions

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2 Stage **P4**, Track F1 (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p4.md`) — the first concrete batch of the serial integration loop, gated on F0's decisions and Tracks B/C/G. |
| **Original Plan** | F1a-j: pick a ~25-50 group tranche via F0a's rule, log it before review starts; review and promote per row in the notebook's keyed workflow, no bulk promotion; new claim_type surface variants get alias rows, not code; regenerate V12 and inspect the diff; reseed and audit to clean-or-waived; author the ADR-010 backlog (E1/E2/E3/E5/E6, E4 only if its death conflict exists) into the same commit; run a 3-run eval and compare against F0d's baseline; decide commit or revert; report the five exit-gate numbers in both candidate and table space; note flaky CONFLICT questions separately. |
| **What Changed** | **F1a**: applied F0a's Tier-1 rule mechanically — every `marriage` and `epithet` group for the 20 frozen top-20 subjects (48 Tier-1 groups existed; selected all of `marriage`+`epithet` specifically since both are canonical types with zero promoted coverage, satisfying the type-count gate in one move) — 26 groups, `Telemachus`/`Patroclus`/`Ajax` turned out to have none. **F1b**: reviewed every one of the resulting 26 groups' distinct claim values against real corpus snippets (not promoted by `claim_value` string alone) before deciding — 95 rows promoted, and the verification caught **5 real extraction errors that were NOT promoted**: `Achilles married_to Peleus` (the cited passage is about the wedding gifts given to Peleus, Achilles' father, not a claim about Achilles at all), `Aphrodite married_to Arges` (cited passage is Athena's birth from Zeus's head), `Poseidon married_to Medus` (the passage names Medusa, not "Medus"), `Menelaus married_to Hellen` (spelling collision with the unrelated Hellen, son of Deucalion — correctly-spelled `Helen` rows from the same passages were promoted instead), `Zeus married_to Herse` (the cited passage never mentions Herse at all). Breadth over depth applied throughout: near-duplicate rephrasings of one fact were not all promoted (e.g. a dozen ways of saying "wife of Odysseus"), while genuinely distinct claims (different named spouse, different epithet) were kept in full, producing real cross-tradition variety for `Zeus`, `Odysseus`, and `Priam`'s marriage groups especially. **F1c**: no new claim_type surface variants surfaced (both selected types were already canonical). **F1d**: `V12` diff inspected before applying — 96 lines changed (95 new additive `INSERT` rows + one trailing-comma adjustment), zero deletions or modifications to existing rows. **F1e**: `seedgen --strict` → `reseed-local.sh` → `python -m audit` — **zero new unwaived findings anywhere**; A10 correctly reported the 26-group promotion as a trend (`-26` since last run), never a finding, exactly as B7/G4 designed it to. **F1f**: authored the ADR-010 backlog, but not exactly as originally specified — Track A's DEV-102 finding (neither draft Q16 nor Q17 zero-retrieves at the live corpus) had to be resolved first. Live-tested replacement candidates and found simple physical-attribute questions reliably zero-retrieve while "exact quoted words" questions consistently retrieve ~5 chunks and refuse on content grounds instead (exactly the two paths DEV-102 predicted): **Q16** = *"What color were Penelope's eyes?"* (RAG, genuine zero-retrieval, stable across 3 manual runs), **Q17** = *"What were Hermes' exact words as a newborn infant?"* (RAG, retrieves 5 chunks, refuses on content grounds — the other path). **Q19** (E3) = *"Who did Zeus marry?"*, live-verified to route SQL and populate `conflicts[]` with all 11 of this batch's promoted Zeus/marriage rows — a clean, strong CONFLICT/enrichment-on-non-CONFLICT-route example. **Q21** (E5) = ADR-010's literal text *"Where is Achilles from?"*, but **`expected_route: SQL` not `RAG`** — live-verified stable across 4 runs that the router sends this to SQL (`entities.domain`, empty), which correctly and truthfully refuses; alternate phrasings ("What city was Achilles from?") route RAG and *do* answer (Phthia, well-attested in the Iliad), confirming the schema-boundary behavior is real, just via a different route than ADR-010 assumed. **E4** (Q20) correctly did **not** land — this batch promoted `marriage`/`epithet`, not `death`, so no new death conflict was created for it to test; deferred to F2 per the checklist's own instruction. **E6**: REFUSAL floor flipped off `null` to **0.5** (matching CONFLICT/DATA's existing floor, chosen deliberately rather than picked to guarantee a pass). CONFLICT's floor was *not* raised despite growing 4→5 questions — at exactly 5 questions, 0.5 and 0.6 require the identical `≥3/5` threshold, so raising now would be cosmetic, not substantive; revisit at 6+. **A second, live-caught eval-scorer gap**: F1g's first 3-run eval run scored `Q21` a stable 3/3 **fail** — not a pipeline defect, the system answered correctly every time ("The provided material does **not contain** sufficient information...", zero citations), but `SOURCE_SILENCE_PHRASES` only had `"do not contain"` (plural form), not the grammatically singular `"does not contain"` an SQL-route, single-entity-subject refusal naturally uses. Root-caused as an eval-bug (not a system defect) and fixed the same way DEV-102/DEV-108's `Helios` catch were — by running the real thing and reading what it actually says, not by guessing a phrase list would already cover a route (SQL refusals) no earlier pass had exercised. Added `"does not contain"` and `"does not provide"`, plus a regression test pinned to the exact live wording. **F1g (re-run after the fix)**: `python -m runner --runs 3 --label p4-f1-batch1-fixed` — **18/20 = 90%**, zero transport errors, all floors met (`REFUSAL` 3/3 = 100%, `CONFLICT` 5/5 = 100%, `DATA` 4/5 = 80%), `compare.py` vs. F0d's baseline (`2026-07-28T17-01-35Z__e4a369a__p4-f0d-baseline`) reports **zero stable regressions**, exit 0. The only deltas: `Q8` (`"List all monsters Perseus encountered"` — a subject/domain this batch never touched) flipped `stable-pass` → `flaky` (`SQL` in 2 of 3 runs, `RAG` in 1) — classified informational per the stable-only rule, not gate-blocking, and not investigated further here (**F1j**) since it is pre-existing router non-determinism unrelated to this batch's subjects, the same class of flake DEV-094 already documented for Q3. **A third live-caught gap, this time in a Kotlin test**: `./gradlew :core-api:test` failed one pre-existing test after this batch's reseed — `QueryControllerTest`'s `GET conflicts Aphrodite...` asserted `jsonPath("$[?(@.sourceAuthor == 'Hesiod')].sourceWork").value("Theogony")`, which broke with `Got a list of values ["Theogony","Theogony"] instead of the expected single value`. Root cause: `/api/v1/conflicts/{entityName}` fetches **all** claim_types for the subject (the browse endpoint, per `ConflictLookup`'s subject-only fetch), and this batch legitimately promoted a **second** Hesiod/Theogony-sourced claim for Aphrodite (an epithet, `hesiod-theogony 176-232`, alongside the pre-existing birth claim from the same passage) — genuinely correct data, surfacing exactly what the product is for. The test's single-scalar assertion only ever worked because exactly one match existed before; Spring's `jsonPath(...).value()` returns a list once 2+ filter matches exist, which a scalar-typed assertion cannot express. Fixed by asserting the actual invariant ("every Hesiod-authored entry cites Theogony, Hesiod's only seeded work") via a parsed-response check robust to any match count, not by reverting the promotion — the data was correct, the test's assumption was narrower than the schema it was testing. `./gradlew :core-api:test` green again, 189 tests. **F1h: decision is GREEN** — committed. |
| **Reason** | The user asked to implement Track F1 next. Checking every group's actual snippets before promoting (rather than trusting `claim_value` strings, which is what made the 71-row original seed batch possible to build quickly but never actually verified) is what caught all 5 extraction errors — none of them would have been caught by a schema check or an audit rule, only by reading the cited text. Re-verifying Q16/Q17 live before authoring them (rather than using ADR-010's literal text unchanged) is DEV-102's own carry-over instruction acted on, not skipped. Fixing the SQL-route refusal phrase gap via the scorer's sanctioned extension point (rather than reverting the batch or the new question) follows DEV-048/050's explicit rule: a keyword/phrase gap found via live evidence is an eval-bug fix, never silent tuning, and re-running to confirm the fix (rather than trusting the unit test alone) is what turned a plausible fix into a verified one. |
| **Impact** | **Data + gold-question + eval-config change; no schema.** Files: `ingestion/extraction/output/variant_claims_candidates.json` (+95 promoted), `core-api/src/main/resources/db/migration/V12__seed_variant_claims.sql` (regenerated, +95 rows, zero dedup loss — 95 candidate promotions → 95 table rows, unlike the original batch's 71→44), `ingestion/notebooks/02_verify_conflicts.ipynb` (+2 cells documenting this batch, matching the C1/C3 historical-migration style), `ingestion/audit/promotion_log.json` (new, 1 entry: `p4-f1-batch1`, 95 keys, 26 groups), `evaluation/gold-questions.json` (+4: ids 16/17/19/21; 20 total now, up from 16), `evaluation/eval-config.json` (`REFUSAL` floor `null`→`0.5`), `evaluation/runner/scoring.py` (`SOURCE_SILENCE_PHRASES` +4 across two live-verification passes: `"do not preserve"`/`"do not document"` for Q17, `"does not contain"`/`"does not provide"` for Q21), `evaluation/runner/tests/test_scoring.py` (+2 regression tests), `core-api/src/test/kotlin/com/blamezeus/coreapi/controller/QueryControllerTest.kt` (the Aphrodite/Theogony assertion made robust to any match count, not reverted). Live DB: `variant_claims` 44→**139**, canonical claim_types **2→4** (`parentage`, `death`, `marriage`, `epithet` — **exit gate met**), top-20-prominence subjects with coverage **3→17 of 20** (missing `Telemachus`/`Patroclus`/`Ajax`, which have no `marriage`/`epithet` candidates at all — a real gap for a future batch's `notable_claim` or other type, not an oversight). Gold set **16→20** questions (of the ≈25 target). Results dirs: `evaluation/results/2026-07-28T17-40-53Z__9b67321__p4-f1-batch1/` (the pre-fix run, kept as the historical record of the eval-bug, **not** the accepted baseline) and `evaluation/results/2026-07-28T17-49-45Z__9b67321__p4-f1-batch1-fixed/` (**the accepted result** — 18/20, zero stable regressions vs. F0d). `./gradlew :core-api:test` (189) and both Python suites (329 ingestion + 74 evaluation, +2 new) green. **Exit-gate scorecard after this batch**: canonical claim_types 4/4 ✅, top-20 subjects 17/20, gold set 20/~25, batches run 1/≥3, overall 90% (≥75% target, sustained across the fixed 3-run eval) — the loop continues with F2/F3. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-28 |
