### DEV-112 — Track F2: batch 2 promotes 107 rows across 25 canonical groups, closes the top-20-prominence-subject exit gate outright (20/20), catches 8 more extraction errors including a reversed kill-direction and two Homeric-formula misreads, lands Q20/Q22/Q23

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2 Stage **P4**, Track F2 (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p4.md`) — the second batch of the serial integration loop, following F1's template. |
| **Original Plan** | F2 repeats F1a-j unchanged: re-run A8/A9/A10 first (the ranking moves as the graph grows), pick a tranche per F0a's rule, review and promote, regenerate/reseed/audit, grow the gold set by 1-3 live-verified questions (E7), run a 3-run eval and `compare.py` against **F1's accepted run** (not the P3b/F0d one), decide, report the same five gate numbers F1 did. |
| **What Changed** | **Refreshed A8/A10** — top-20 ranking unchanged (graph structure didn't move, only `variant_claims` did). With `marriage`/`epithet` already at 4/4 canonical types after F1, this batch's Tier 1 became every top-20 subject's `notable_claim` group (plus small `other`/`burial`/`role` groups where present) — 16 of 20 subjects had one. `Telemachus` and `Patroclus` had **no** unpromoted-type candidates *at all*, so F0a's Tier 3 (already-canonical `parentage`/`death` groups) was the only path to closing their coverage gap — exactly the GAP-001-adjacent case F0a's rule was designed to route there. `Ajax` was initially miscounted as having zero `notable_claim` rows by an ad-hoc review script that filtered on raw `claim_type` string equality instead of the normalized canonical form (V18's collapse means `notable`/`notable_act`/`notable_deed`/etc. all resolve to `notable_claim`) — caught and fixed before any promotion decision was made, by cross-checking against A10's own (correctly normalized) count. **107 rows promoted across 25 canonical groups** (41 raw `(subject_name, claim_type)` groups by the promotion log's un-normalized counting, since several subjects' rows span multiple raw `notable_claim` spellings). **8 more extraction errors caught, none promoted** — a new failure *shape* this batch, not a repeat of F1's: a Homeric formulaic address ("Zeus, son of Cronos" invoked when addressing heralds/companions, not a genealogy statement) misread as a literal parentage claim, hitting **both** `Telemachus` (`'child of Zeus'`, `homer-odyssey` 21.96-21.142 — Telemachus's own idiom "Zeus has made me witless") and `Patroclus` (`'child of Zeus'`/`'sprung from Zeus'`, `homer-iliad` 1.325-1.363 — Achilles addressing heralds as "messengers of Zeus and men"); `Telemachus`/`'child of Athena'` (`homer-odyssey` 2.267-2.308) is the same shape, Athena approaching in Mentor's guise, not a birth claim; `Patroclus`/`'killed by Sarpedon'` (`homer-iliad` 23.797-23.835) is **reversed** — the cited text shows Patroclus stripping *Sarpedon's* armor (Patroclus killed Sarpedon, the well-known Iliad 16 scene, not the other way around); three `Diomedes`/`notable_claim` rows were skipped as unverified (one plausible `Diomede`/`Diomedes` name confusion, two whose cited passage showed a different speaker/scene entirely); one `Aphrodite`/`notable_claim` row (`'went to Olympus to ask Zeus for the maidens' marriage'`, `homer-odyssey` 20.54-20.101) was skipped as a likely Athena-not-Aphrodite misattribution — that passage is Odysseus's pre-slaughter prayer scene, Athena's usual role, not Aphrodite's. **E4 (Q20) landed**, deferred from F1 exactly as planned: this batch's `Patroclus`/`death` promotion (4 rows: `slain at Troy`, `killed by Hector`, `killed by Apollo`, `killed by the gods`) gave a *new* subject a genuine stored death conflict, distinct from `Achilles` (already used by Q16). *"What did Patroclus look like physically?"* live-verified stable across 2 manual runs: `serviceError: false`, `conflicts: []` (proving `ConflictLookup`'s claim-type filter correctly excludes the death conflict from an appearance query), proper refusal wording. **Caught a mirror-image `SOURCE_SILENCE_PHRASES` gap before it caused a stable fail this time** (not after, unlike DEV-110's `Q21` discovery) — the live Patroclus refusal wording ("the available sources **do not provide**... they **do not detail**...") is grammatically plural, the inverse of DEV-110's singular `"does not provide"` catch; added `"do not provide"`/`"do not detail"` and a regression test before running the real eval, since DEV-110 had already established exactly this singular/plural pattern to watch for. **E7's two picks**, both live-verified to actually depend on this batch's promotions (not just any RAG-answerable trivia — an initial "Heracles' labors" candidate was rejected for this reason, since RAG retrieval never depends on `variant_claims` promotion status at all): **Q22** *"Who was the mother of Patroclus?"* (CONFLICT, `SQL` route, `conflicts_min_count: 2`, live-verified 4 conflict rows across `Homer`+`Apollodorus` — a genuine cross-source disagreement Apollodorus itself presents, `Sthenele` vs. `Periopis`) and **Q23** *"Who are the parents of Telemachus?"* (DATA, `SQL` route, live-verified stable). **`./gradlew :core-api:test`**: green with no new assertion breaks this time (F1's `QueryControllerTest` fix already generalized correctly). **F2g**: `python -m runner --runs 3 --label p4-f2-batch2` — **21/23 = 91%**, zero transport errors, all floors met (`REFUSAL` 4/4, `CONFLICT` 6/6, `DATA` 5/6), `compare.py` vs. **F1's accepted run** (`p4-f1-batch1-fixed`, not F0d's) reports **zero stable regressions**, exit 0 — the only deltas are 3 added questions and `Q11` (MIXED, pre-existing) flipping `stable-fail`→`flaky`, informational only. `Q8` (the pre-existing Perseus/monsters router flake from F1) recurred, unrelated to this batch's subjects, still not chased. **F2h: decision is GREEN** — committed. |
| **Reason** | The user asked to implement Track F2 next. The Ajax miscounting bug is exactly why F0a's rule insists on re-running A8/A10 fresh rather than reusing F1's cached numbers — a stale or subtly-wrong manual re-derivation (raw-string claim_type matching instead of normalized) would have silently under-covered a top-20 subject; catching it via a cross-check against A10's own output, rather than trusting the ad-hoc script, is the same "verify against the real artifact, not your own derivation" discipline DEV-104 already established for the notebook's group listing. Rejecting the "Heracles' labors" E7 candidate mid-selection (after live-testing it successfully) for not actually depending on the promotion is a deliberate higher bar than "the answer sounds right" — E7 exists to test *this batch's* effect, and a question RAG would answer identically with zero rows promoted doesn't test that. Catching the plural `SOURCE_SILENCE_PHRASES` gap live *before* running the real eval (rather than after a stable fail, as DEV-110's `Q21` was caught) is DEV-110's own lesson applied forward, not re-learned the hard way a second time. |
| **Impact** | **Data + gold-question change; no schema.** Files: `ingestion/extraction/output/variant_claims_candidates.json` (+107 promoted), `core-api/src/main/resources/db/migration/V12__seed_variant_claims.sql` (regenerated, +107 rows), `ingestion/notebooks/02_verify_conflicts.ipynb` (+1 summary cell — the full 107-key list was deliberately **not** re-embedded as a literal cell this time, to avoid roughly doubling the notebook's size two batches running; `promotion_log.json` and this DEV entry are the canonical records), `ingestion/audit/promotion_log.json` (+1 entry: `p4-f2-batch2`, 107 keys, 41 raw groups), `evaluation/gold-questions.json` (+3: ids 20/22/23; 23 total now, up from 20), `evaluation/runner/scoring.py` (`SOURCE_SILENCE_PHRASES` +2: `"do not provide"`, `"do not detail"`; also fixed a stray comment misattributing an earlier addition to a never-actually-used "DEV-111" — it was folded into DEV-110), `evaluation/runner/tests/test_scoring.py` (+1 regression test). Live DB: `variant_claims` 139→**245**, canonical claim_types **4→8** (`parentage`, `death`, `marriage`, `epithet`, `notable_claim`, `other`, `role`, `burial` — already comfortably past the ≥4 gate), **top-20-prominence subjects 17→20 of 20 — this exit gate is now fully met**. Gold set **20→23** questions (of the ≈25 target — 2 more from a future F3 closes it). Results dir: `evaluation/results/2026-07-28T18-36-53Z__310c320__p4-f2-batch2/` (accepted, zero stable regressions vs. F1). `./gradlew :core-api:test` (189) and both Python suites (329 ingestion + 75 evaluation, +1 new) green. **Exit-gate scorecard after this batch**: canonical claim_types 8/4 ✅ (well past), top-20 subjects **20/20 ✅**, gold set 23/~25, batches run **2/≥3**, overall 91% (≥75% target, sustained). Only the batch count and gold-set size remain open before P4's full exit gate — both close naturally in F3. |

### DEV-113 — Introduce `trust_tier=2` (rejected) so extraction errors caught during review are recorded, not left indistinguishable from unreviewed rows

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2 Stage **P4**, Track F3 (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p4.md`) — a small mechanical fix ahead of the batch-3 review, prompted by a direct question ("are the extraction errors documented") mid-loop rather than by a template step. |
| **Original Plan** | F1/F2's per-row review discipline (checking every candidate against its cited source passage before promoting) was already in the template and already worked — it caught 13 real extraction errors across the two batches. Recording *which* rows were checked-and-rejected, as opposed to simply never promoted, was not part of the original plan; `run_extraction.py`'s own docstring for `_write_claims_preserving_review` had already anticipated the need ("a future 'rejected' tier ... this deliberately covers without needing another change here") but nothing had used it yet. |
| **What Changed** | Added `REJECTED_TRUST_TIER = 2` to `ingestion/extraction/run_extraction.py`, alongside the existing `DEFAULT_TRUST_TIER = 3`. Verified both consumers already handle it correctly with **zero code changes**: `seedgen/variant_claims_gen.py`'s promotion filter is exact equality against `1`, so a `2` row was already excluded and stays excluded; `_write_claims_preserving_review`'s carry-over check is `!= DEFAULT_TRUST_TIER` (not `== 1`), so a `2` row already survives a re-extraction exactly like a promoted row. Fixed the carry-over log line to break out promoted vs. rejected counts instead of a single undifferentiated "reviewed" number. Marked the **31 rows** (14 distinct claim patterns — 5 from F1, 8 from F2's documented catches, plus the third of F2's "three unverified `Diomedes` rows" that wasn't individually named in F2's prose, `'exchanged arms with Glaucus after recall'`) from `trust_tier=3` to `2`; confirmed none were currently `trust_tier=1` before writing (no collision with a prior promotion). Logged the batch to `ingestion/audit/promotion_log.json` under `batchLabel: "p4-f3-retroactive-rejections"` (`rejectedKeys`, 31 keys, 11 groups) for the same identify-and-revert-as-a-unit auditability C5/DEV-104 established for promotions. Updated `ingestion/notebooks/02_verify_conflicts.ipynb`: `review_group()` now flags rows already at `trust_tier=1`/`2` inline (`[ALREADY PROMOTED]`/`[ALREADY REJECTED]`) instead of printing a bare tier number, and prints a `rejected_keys += [...]` line alongside `approved_keys += [...]`; the promotion cell now accepts a parallel `rejected_keys` list, refuses if a key appears in both lists, writes rejections at `trust_tier=2`, and logs them to `promotion_log.json` under a `rejectedKeys` field alongside the existing `keys`. Added 3 new/updated tests to `ingestion/tests/test_run_extraction.py` (rejected-row preservation across re-extraction, promoted/rejected counted separately in the log line) — all 331 ingestion tests, all 75 evaluation tests, and the full `./gradlew :core-api:test` suite pass unchanged. Regenerated `V12__seed_variant_claims.sql` via `seedgen --strict`: byte-identical, still 245 rows, confirming zero effect on the seeded table. |
| **Reason** | The user asked directly whether the extraction errors caught during F1/F2 review were documented. They were, but only in prose (`DEVIATIONS.md`, notebook markdown) — every rejected row itself still read as `trust_tier=3`, unreviewed, in the actual data. A future reviewer re-visiting the same group (or a future re-extraction cycle) had no machine-readable signal that the row had already been checked and found wanting, risking either silent re-promotion or repeated manual re-litigation of the same rejected claim. The fix was infrastructure `run_extraction.py` had already anticipated but never used, making it a small, mechanical, low-risk change rather than new design. |
| **Impact** | **Data annotation only; no schema, no seeded-row change.** Files: `ingestion/extraction/run_extraction.py` (+`REJECTED_TRUST_TIER` constant, log-line fix), `ingestion/tests/test_run_extraction.py` (+2 tests, 1 updated), `ingestion/extraction/output/variant_claims_candidates.json` (31 rows: `trust_tier` 3→2; tier distribution now `{3: 7125, 1: 273, 2: 31}`), `ingestion/audit/promotion_log.json` (+1 entry: `p4-f3-retroactive-rejections`, 31 rejected keys, 11 groups), `ingestion/notebooks/02_verify_conflicts.ipynb` (`review_group()`, the promote/reject decision cells, and the write cell updated; +1 documentation cell). `core-api/src/main/resources/db/migration/V12__seed_variant_claims.sql` unchanged (still 245 rows) — this was the point of the verification, not a side effect to fix. Batch-3 (F3 proper) review sessions going forward can use `rejected_keys` the same way `approved_keys` has always worked, closing the documentation gap for all future rejections, not just this session's retroactive 31. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-28 |

### DEV-114 — Track F3: batch 3 promotes 48 parentage rows across 17 top-20 subjects (closing GAP-001's top-20 backlog started at F0b), rejects 89 candidates including a newly-discovered systemic reversed-parentage-direction extraction bug, closes the ≥3-batches and ≈25-gold-question exit gates

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2 Stage **P4**, Track F3 (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p4.md`) — the third and, per the exit-gate wording, gate-closing batch of the serial integration loop, following F1/F2's template. |
| **Original Plan** | F3 repeats F1a–j unchanged: re-run A8/A9/A10, pick a tranche per F0a's rule, review and promote, regenerate/reseed/audit, grow the gold set by 1–3 live-verified questions (E7), run a 3-run eval and `compare.py` against F2's accepted run, decide, report the same five gate numbers. At F3's close, explicitly check the full exit gate (≥3 batches, ≥4 canonical claim_types, all top-20-prominence subjects, gold set ≈25, floors enforced, overall ≥75%) since claim_types and top-20-subject coverage were already met after F2. |
| **What Changed** | With `marriage`/`epithet`/`notable_claim` already covering all top-20 subjects after F1/F2, F0a's rule routes this batch entirely into **Tier 3**: `parentage`, a canonical claim_type, but still uncovered for 17 of the 20 frozen top-20 subjects (only `Achilles`, `Aphrodite`, `Telemachus`, `Patroclus` had any promoted parentage row) — exactly the GAP-001 backlog F0b named as this tier's home. Reviewed each subject's `parentage` candidate group (up to 254 raw rows per subject, mostly Homeric-formula near-duplicates of 1-3 genuine facts — e.g. Achilles' 39 distinct phrasings collapse to "son of Peleus" + "child of Thetis") and promoted 1-2 rows per genuine distinct parent per source: **48 rows across 17 canonical `(subject, parentage)` groups**. **A verification-driven catch mid-batch**: two of the first-picked rows (`Achilles`/`child of Peleus` and `child of Thetis`, both `homer-iliad`) had a non-specific `passage_ref` of literally `"Homer, Iliad"` (a preamble-style ref, not a citable location) — caught by an automated post-promotion sanity sweep for non-numeric refs among all 321 promoted rows, not by reviewing that specific row's citation quality up front; swapped for properly-refed duplicates of the same claim (`1.1-1.52` and `1.325-1.363`), each re-verified against its segment text before the swap. **89 rows rejected (`trust_tier=2`), the batch's main finding**: a **newly-discovered systemic extraction bug**, distinct in shape from F1's misattributed-passage errors and F2's Homeric-formula misreads — a **reversed parentage direction**, where a passage describing "`[X], son/daughter of [Y]`" as an epithet for `X` gets extracted backward as "`Y`: child of `X`" instead of "`X`: child of `Y`". Verified via direct segment inspection across multiple instances: `Odysseus`/`'child of Telemachus'` (42 rows — Telemachus is Odysseus's *son*, not father; the cited Odyssey 1.156 passage is Telemachus lamenting his missing father Odysseus, containing no parentage claim about Odysseus at all), `Penelope`/`'child of Telemachus'` (4 rows, same shape), and **eleven of Priam's named sons** individually misattributed as "`Priam`: child of `[son's name]`" (`Hector`, `Polites`, `Deiphobus`, `Echemmon`, `Chromius`, `Paris`, `Iasus`/`Isus`, `Antiphus`, `Heleus`, `Polydorus`, `Lycaon` — 18 rows total; `Iliad` 5.121 explicitly reads "two sons of Priam, Dardanus' son, Echemmon and Chromius" and `Iliad` 11.84 "Isus and Antiphus, two sons of Priam", both confirming the reversal directly). Cross-referenced against this same batch's own verified-correct promotions to catch further instances without re-deriving each one from scratch: `Zeus`/`'child of Athena'` (6), `'child of Aphrodite'` (3), `'child of Heracles'` (1), `'child of Hephaestus'` (1), `'child of Hermes'` (1) are all backward given Zeus is *established as their father* by rows promoted in this same batch. Two more rejected for a different reason — genuinely unsupported/misattributed passages, not the reversed-direction shape: `Agamemnon`/`'child of Achilles'` (`Iliad` 1.386, Achilles' own complaint speech, no Agamemnon parentage content) and `'child of Orestes'` (`Odyssey` 1.1-1.43, the poem's opening invocation about Odysseus, nowhere near the real Orestes/Agamemnon material later in Book 1) — plus `Diomedes`/`'daughter of Phorbas'` (`Iliad` 9.643, an unrelated Achilles/Ajax exchange, and gender-impossible for Diomedes on its face). **The blast radius is wider than this batch**: `variant_claims` candidates and `relationships` candidates come from the same extraction pass, so the same reversed reading almost certainly produced backwards `parent_of` **edges** in `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` — which is a *seeded table*, not a review-gated one. This batch rejected the `variant_claims` half only; the `parent_of` half is untouched and needs the same direction check. Two existing symptoms plausibly share this root cause and are worth re-reading in this light rather than as independent problems: **A3's 92 candidate-layer cycles** (a reversed edge is the textbook way to close a genealogy cycle, and F0c already sampled them as name-collision artifacts without considering direction) and **A6's dropped-parent residue** (a backwards `parent_of` row is exactly what the contested-collapse resolver would discard). Recorded here, not chased — see this entry's *Impact* and the P4 closure banner in `TODO2.md`. Seven **self-referential** rows (`X`: child of `X`, logically certain without a segment check) also rejected: `Zeus`, `Achilles`, `Diomedes`, `Athena`, `Hermes`, `Heracles`, `Ajax` each had a `'child of <own name>'` row; plus `Priam`/`'child of Nestor (son of Priam)'`, self-contradictory in its own claim_value text. One clean **new cross-source conflict landed as a side effect of genuine review, not engineered**: `Priam`'s two promoted parentage rows are `'child of Laomedon'` (Apollodorus, direct biological father) and `'child of Dardanus'` (Homer, a distant-ancestor patronymic epithet used loosely but repeatedly and explicitly by the source text) — both real, both attributed, surfacing as a live 2-way conflict. **`seedgen --strict`**: 293 rows (245+48, zero dedup loss). **`reseed-local.sh`**: required stopping/restarting the running dev server, done only after explicit user confirmation (per this project's standing convention); post-reseed counts entities 2003/relationships 3365/variant_claims 293/narrative_chunks 3524 unchanged. **`audit --db`**: A1/A2/A4/A7/A10 stay WAIVED/PASS unaffected (none of their inputs changed); **A3 stays FINDINGS**, unchanged from F0c's deliberate non-waiver; **A6 (dropped-parent-candidate residue) newly shows 40 unwaived findings** — investigated rather than blanket-waived: `dropped_parents.py`'s finding set is a pure function of the untouched `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` + `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` files, so these 40 pre-date this batch and were never actually covered by F0c's original 608-entry A6 waiver (which used a "child is outside the top-20 list" reason that is factually false for all 40 — their children are all top-20 subjects). An initial attempt to blanket-waive them by reusing F0c's boilerplate reason was caught as inaccurate and reverted before being left in the file. Left honestly as unwaived FINDINGS, matching A3's own precedent (F0c: "the gate does not fully exit 0 ... A3 stays red"), rather than writing a false reason into the audit record — a dedicated future pass (not F3, which is scoped to `variant_claims` promotion, not `relationships`-table dropped-candidate triage) should either promote/reject each of the 40 through the same review discipline or write per-subject waiver reasons that are actually true. **Gold set +2 (E7)**, both live-verified against the running server to confirm they actually depend on this batch's promotions: **Q24** *"Who is the father of Priam?"* (CONFLICT, `SQL` route, `conflicts_min_count: 2`, live-verified 2 conflicts across Apollodorus/Homer, stable-pass 3/3) and **Q25** *"Who is the mother of Athena?"* (DATA, `SQL` route, live-verified correct single answer "Metis" despite `conflicts[]` also surfacing Zeus alongside it as a side effect of `ConflictLookup`'s claim-type-wide match — expected per ADR-007, doesn't affect DATA scoring, stable-pass 3/3). Gold set **23→25**, hitting the stage's "≈25" target exactly. **`./gradlew :core-api:test`**, ingestion (331) and evaluation (75) Python suites all green, unrelated to this batch's data-only changes. **F3g**: `python -m runner --runs 3 --label p4-f3-batch3` — **22/25 = 88%**, zero `_runnerNote` transport errors, all floors met (`DATA` 71%≥50%, `CONFLICT` 100%≥50%, `REFUSAL` 100%≥50%), `compare.py` vs. F2's accepted run (`p4-f2-batch2`) reports **zero stable regressions**, exit 0 — informational-only deltas: `Q8`/`Q11` flip `flaky`/`flaky`→`stable-fail` and `Q9` flips `stable-pass`→`flaky` (all pre-existing router non-determinism on Zeus-lineage questions, not newly introduced — `Q8` is the same Perseus/monsters-adjacent flake first seen in F1; single-run deltas, per the stable-only rule, not chased), and `Q7`/`Q9`/`Q12` (all Zeus-lineage FACT/DATA/MIXED questions, none CONFLICT-category) gained non-zero `conflicts[]` counts as an expected side effect of Zeus/Athena/Achilles/etc. now having genuine promoted parentage data, not a scoring-relevant change. **F3h: decision is GREEN** — ready to commit. |
| **Reason** | The user asked to implement Track F3 next, continuing the batch loop after DEV-113's rejected-tier infrastructure landed. Choosing the parentage backlog as this batch's tranche follows F0a's own mechanical rule (Tier 3, since Tiers 1-2 are empty once every canonical claim_type has some promoted coverage) rather than a fresh judgement call. Rejecting the 89 candidates rather than leaving them unpromoted-but-unmarked directly exercises DEV-113's own `rejected_keys` mechanism for the first time in real batch use (not just the retroactive backfill), and the reversed-parentage-direction pattern was significant enough (89 rows, a genuinely new *shape* of error not seen in F1/F2) to warrant full documentation rather than silent rejection. Reverting the inaccurate A6 waiver rather than keeping it matches this project's `DEV-047`/ADR-004 "never fabricate, never assert what wasn't checked" discipline applied to the audit trail itself, not just to `variant_claims` rows — a waiver reason is a claim like any other, and this one was demonstrably false for the rows it would have covered. |
| **Impact** | **Data + gold-question change; no schema, no application code.** Files: `ingestion/extraction/output/variant_claims_candidates.json` (48 rows promoted, 89 rejected; tier distribution now `{3: 6987, 1: 321, 2: 120}`), `core-api/src/main/resources/db/migration/V12__seed_variant_claims.sql` (regenerated, 245→**293** rows), `ingestion/audit/promotion_log.json` (+1 entry: `p4-f3-batch3`, 48 promoted in 17 groups + 89 rejected keys), `ingestion/audit/group_inventory_baseline.json`/`group_inventory_findings.json` (refreshed A8/A10 state, 798 groups / 71 with a promoted row), `evaluation/gold-questions.json` (+2: ids 24/25; 25 total, up from 23). Live DB: `variant_claims` 245→**293**, top-20-prominence subjects' `parentage` coverage **3/20→20/20** (`Aphrodite`, `Telemachus`, `Patroclus` already had promoted parentage rows from earlier batches; this batch's 17 canonical groups — including `Achilles`, which had zero before F3 despite already having promoted `marriage`/`epithet`/`notable_claim` — cover every remaining top-20 subject). Results dir: `evaluation/results/2026-07-28T19-26-07Z__57a3988__p4-f3-batch3/` (accepted, zero stable regressions vs. F2). **Exit-gate scorecard after this batch**: canonical claim_types 8/4 ✅, top-20 subjects 20/20 ✅, gold set **25/~25 ✅**, batches run **3/≥3 ✅**, overall 88% (≥75% target, sustained across all three batches) ✅ — **the full Stage P4 Track F exit gate is met**. Two items carried forward, both explicitly written down rather than silently dropped: A3's 92 candidate-layer cycles (F0c's own pre-existing deferral, unchanged) and A6's 40 newly-honest unwaived findings (this batch's own discovery, see *What Changed*) both still need a dedicated future pass — Track F's own loop is scoped to `variant_claims`, not to closing every audit check to zero. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-28 |

### DEV-115 — Track J: reconciled P4's documentation across six files and closed the stage; three previously-unrecorded findings surfaced while doing it, including a `seedgen` warning that V18 silently turned into a false alarm

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Phase 2 Stage **P4**, Track J (`docs/TODO-phase2-stage-p4.md`) — the pure-prose reconciliation track, run after F3 closed the data/eval gates. |
| **Original Plan** | Six items, all documentation: J1 one DEV entry per landed track; J2 correct the stale "838 unreviewed groups" figure in four named files by banner, never by rewriting a plan body; J3 record the `§5`-vs-`TODO2` tranche-priority conflict and F0a's resolution in both documents; J4 tick ADR-010's "~8 gold questions" item at F3 (not earlier) and leave the numeric-questions item open for P5a; J5 tick `TODO2.md`'s P4 bullets and add a P3b-style stage-closure banner; J6 add the deviations banner to `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md §5` and correct DEV-101's "nothing committed" Impact field. |
| **What Changed** | All six landed as scoped. **J2 needed more than it asked for**: its own quoted figures (839/835, "71 promoted rows in 4 groups") had themselves gone stale, because Track G's V18 `notable*` collapse moved the canonical group total **835 → 798** after this checklist was written. All four sites therefore got the *current* numbers — **798 groups, 727 zero-promoted, 321 promoted rows across 71 groups, seeding 293 `variant_claims`** — and each flags a **number collision** that would otherwise mislead: the old "71" counts promoted *rows* in 4 *groups*, today's counts *groups*. `ADR-017` and `TODO.md` had no correction at all and got banners; `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_PHASE2.md`/`TODO2.md` already carried the 839/835 version and were amended, not rewritten. **J3's resolution is now recorded as an outcome, not a plan** — `§5`'s banner previously said F0a "resolves it before the first batch" with no result. **J6 found a second error in DEV-101 that the item did not name**: besides the "nothing committed" mistake (it was `2e4ce40`), that entry's *"Not addressed here: DEV-049 … still surface as `serviceError`"* asserts an open defect which **DEV-102 later established does not reproduce** — both corrections appended rather than overwritten. **Three findings surfaced during the pass that no document held:** **(1) `seedgen --strict`'s `near-duplicate claim_type` warning is now a false alarm.** `warn_near_duplicate_claim_types(variant_claims)` is the **only** one of `variant_claims_gen`'s three entry points called *without* `alias_map` (`build_variant_claim_rows` and `check_floor_conflicts` both receive it), so it compares raw surface forms and cannot see that an alias already exists. Verified directly against the live table: all six `notable*` variants — including the exact `'notable claim'` the warning names — resolve to `notable_claim` in `claim_type_aliases`, and A9 independently reports **0 unmapped formatting-duplicates**. The warning's own text instructs a developer to "resolve via a follow-up migration", i.e. to do work V18 already did. It was a legitimate warning before V18 (DEV-107) and became stale the moment V18 landed; DEV-091 recorded it as "pre-existing", which was true then and is misleading now. Output is unaffected (the docstring's "diagnostic only" holds — V12's row count is correct), so this is a time-waster, not a data bug. **Left unfixed deliberately**: the fix is a code change to `seedgen`, outside Track J's pure-prose scope, and it should be a decision rather than a silent edit inside a documentation pass. **(2) `python -m audit` cannot be run as its own README documents it.** The `.env` sits at the repo root while the command runs from `ingestion/`, and `config.py` deliberately does not call `load_dotenv()` (its top-level reads execute at import — `docs/TODO-stage2.md` H1), so a bare invocation dies at import with `KeyError: 'OPENAI_API_KEY'` before any check runs. The named key is itself misleading — the audit checks never embed and never call OpenAI; they need the Postgres variables that happen to share the file. This interrupted two runs in one session. README usage block fixed with the `set -a && source ../.env && set +a` prelude and an explanatory note. **(3) `ingestion/audit/README.md` carried the same stale 835 group count** J2 was chasing, in a file J2's list did not include — corrected to 798 with a pointer to J2. **Two further findings came out of the box-ticking itself**, which turned into a real audit rather than a formality: **(4) Track E's and G4's boxes were all still unticked** despite their work having landed inside F1/F2/F3 — verified each against the live artifacts before ticking (`gold-questions.json` confirms Q16/Q17/Q19/Q20/Q21 exist with the shapes E1–E5 specify, and that **E1/E2/E5 landed with *different* content than the checklist prescribes**: Q16/Q17 use replacement text because neither draft retrieved zero chunks (DEV-102/DEV-110), and Q21 is `expected_route: SQL`, not the RAG the item assumed). **(5) The `CONFLICT` floor's own deferral trigger has fired and nobody revisited it.** E6 says raise it "as the category grows past 4"; DEV-112 deferred with an explicit *"revisit at 6+"*; the category is now **7** questions and the floor is still **0.5**. ### DEV-116 — `seedgen --strict`'s near-duplicate `claim_type` warning now checks against `claim_type_aliases` before firing, fixing the stale post-V18 false alarm DEV-115 diagnosed but left unfixed

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Post-P4, first of DEV-115's carried-forward items (finding (1)) — a small, contained code fix, picked first over the CONFLICT floor raise and the reversed-parentage-direction investigation because it was already fully root-caused and scoped to one function. |
| **Original Plan** | None — this is a fix for a defect DEV-115 found and deliberately left unfixed ("outside Track J's pure-prose scope, should be a decision rather than a silent edit inside a documentation pass"). |
| **What Changed** | `warn_near_duplicate_claim_types` (`ingestion/seedgen/variant_claims_gen.py`) now takes `alias_map` as a second parameter, matching `build_variant_claim_rows`/`check_floor_conflicts`, and only emits a warning for a near-dup group when `normalize(alias_map, v)` still disagrees across that group's raw variants — i.e. only when `claim_type_aliases` has *not* already unified them. Updated the one call site (`ingestion/seedgen/__main__.py`) to pass `alias_map` (already loaded there for the other two calls). Updated the existing two tests to pass `ALIAS_MAP` and added a new regression test (`test_warn_near_duplicate_claim_types_no_warning_when_alias_map_already_unifies`) asserting silence once an alias map unifies the group — the exact shape of the bug. **Verified against the live corpus**: reproduced the pre-fix behavior directly (a standalone script re-running the old raw-surface-form grouping logic against `variant_claims_candidates.json` found 4 groups that would have warned — Zeus, Cadmus, Odysseus, Achilles, all `notable_claim`/`notable claim`/`notable act`/`notable_act` pairs); ran `python -m seedgen --strict` against the live DB afterward and got **zero warnings** of any kind, and `git status` on `core-api/src/main/resources/db/migration/` showed no diff — confirming the fix is purely diagnostic, exactly as the function's docstring already claimed. `ingestion/tests`: 332 passed (up from 331, +1 new test). |
| **Reason** | DEV-115 diagnosed the exact defect (the function is the only one of the three `variant_claims_gen` entry points not given `alias_map`, so it can't see that V18 already resolved the `notable*` family) but stopped short of fixing it, on the grounds that a documentation track shouldn't make a behavioral call unprompted. The user picked this item first, off a ranked list, specifically because it was already fully diagnosed, contained to one function + one call site, and zero-risk to seeded data. |
| **Impact** | **Code + test only; no schema, no data, no migration, no gold-question, no eval change.** Files: `ingestion/seedgen/variant_claims_gen.py` (+`alias_map` param, updated docstring), `ingestion/seedgen/__main__.py` (call site updated), `ingestion/tests/test_variant_claims_gen.py` (2 tests updated, 1 added). `V12__seed_variant_claims.sql` regenerated and confirmed byte-identical. Closes DEV-115 finding (1) in full. Four items remain carried forward from the P4 closure: the CONFLICT floor raise, A3's 92 candidate-layer cycles, A6's 40 dropped-parent findings with inaccurate waiver text, and F3's reversed-parentage-direction bug in `relationships` candidates. |

### DEV-117 — F4: CONFLICT floor raised 0.5→0.6 (its own "revisit at 6+" trigger honored); re-verification eval surfaces a new, unrelated Q21 stable-fail

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|

| **What Changed** | `evaluation/eval-config.json`: `category_floors.CONFLICT` **0.5 → 0.6**. At the category's current size (7 questions), this is no longer cosmetic: 0.5 requires ⌈3.5⌉=4/7 passing, 0.6 requires ⌈4.2⌉=5/7 — a real, substantive tightening, unlike the 5-question case F2 deferred on. Chose 0.6 over 0.7 as the smaller of the two values the P4 closure banner explicitly flagged as safe ("0.6 or 0.7 would pass with room") — the minimal step that makes the raise substantive, one increment above the DATA/REFUSAL floors these three categories have historically moved in lockstep at, rather than jumping to the most aggressive value obviously supported by CONFLICT's 100% history (picking 0.7 for headroom alone would be choosing a number to guarantee a future pass, the anti-pattern E6 explicitly warns against). Re-ran `python -m runner --runs 3 --label f4-conflict-floor-06` against the live server (Postgres + core-api both already up) to verify per this project's standing rule that a scored-gate change is eval-gated, not just an edit: **CONFLICT 7/7 = 100% ≥ 0.6 — floor met**, overall 21/25 = 84% (≥75% target), zero floor breaches across all five categories. Results dir: `evaluation/results/2026-07-29T09-03-28Z__85a606f__f4-conflict-floor-06/`. |
| **Reason** | The CONFLICT category's own deferral trigger fired (5→7 questions, past both E6's "grows past 4" and F2's own "6+") and sat unactioned through Stage P4's close (DEV-115 finding (5)) because raising a scored gate is a judgment call, not something a documentation pass should do silently. Picked first among P4's carried-forward items (after the seedgen warning, DEV-116) because, like that one, it was already fully diagnosed and scoped by DEV-115/E6, needing a decision rather than new investigation. |
| **Impact** | **Config-only change to the floor; no schema, code, or seeded-data change.** File: `evaluation/eval-config.json` (CONFLICT floor 0.5→0.6). Closes DEV-115 finding (5)/E6's second half. **A second, unrelated finding surfaced by this same re-verification run, decided explicitly rather than folded in silently**: `compare.py` against F3's accepted baseline (`p4-f3-batch3`) flagged **Q21 (REFUSAL, "Where is Achilles from?") as a stable PASS→FAIL regression** — stable-pass 3/3 in F3's session, stable-fail 3/3 in this one, same code and DB both times. Root-caused, not just noted: reproduced live with a direct manual query (4th independent confirmation beyond the 3 runner repeats) — the chat model now volunteers Achilles' cited parentage (4 real citations, `child of Peleus`/`child of Thetis`, Homer + Apollodorus) alongside the location refusal, where F3's baseline got a clean, citation-free refusal to the identical question against identical data. This is chat-model sampling variance in the RAG/synthesis step, not caused by the floor edit (which touches no code path Q21 exercises) and not fixable by only patching `SOURCE_SILENCE_PHRASES`: `score_refusal`'s `must_not_fabricate_citation` check (Phase-1 heuristic: `citations[]` must be empty) would still fail it even with the missing "does not specify" phrase added, since the citations are real, just about a different (correctly-cited, correctly-refused-on-location) sub-topic. **Decided explicitly, not silently bundled**: log and carry forward rather than fix inline, since diagnosing the right correction (tighten the prompt against volunteering unrequested cited info on a refusal-shaped question, vs. relaxing the citations-must-be-empty heuristic to allow citations on off-topic supplementary content) is its own investigation, outside this item's scope of raising one config number. The floor-raise itself is unaffected by and independent of this finding: CONFLICT's own rate (100%) and the overall gate (84%≥75%, zero floor breaches) both hold regardless of Q21, which sits in REFUSAL (rate 75%, still ≥ its own 0.5 floor). **Five items now carried forward** (four survive from the P4 closure banner plus this one): the CONFLICT floor raise is CLOSED (this entry); remaining open are A3's 92 candidate-layer cycles, A6's 40 dropped-parent findings with inaccurate waiver text, F3's reversed-parentage-direction bug in `relationships` candidates, and (new) Q21's cross-session synthesis-stability regression. |

### DEV-118 — A11: new source-grounded reversed-`parent_of` check; 72 reversed edges fixed (39 of them live in the seeded table); DEV-114's "one shared root cause" hypothesis measured and largely disproved

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Post-P4, third of DEV-115's carried-forward items — the `relationships` half of the systemic reversed-parentage extraction bug DEV-114 found in `variant_claims`, explicitly scoped out of F3 ("the `parent_of` half is untouched and needs the same direction check"). |
| **Original Plan** | DEV-114/DEV-115 predicted a shared root cause across three symptoms: F3's reversed `variant_claims` rows, A3's 92 candidate-layer cycles, and A6's 40 inaccurately-waived dropped parents — "the same reversed/ambiguous-direction shape seen at three different layers … plausibly one investigation rather than three." The expected mechanism was that `relationships` candidates, coming from the same extraction pass, carry the same backwards rows F3 rejected. |
| **What Changed** | **The predicted blast radius is not what is actually there, measured rather than assumed.** Cross-checking F3's 89 rejected `variant_claims` rows against the `parent_of` candidates: the relationships extractor is *overwhelmingly correct* exactly where `variant_claims` was reversed — `Odysseus`→`Telemachus` 49 correct / 0 reversed, `Priam`→`Hector` 57/0, `Zeus`→`Hermes` 22/0. Only 8 of F3's 32 distinct reversed claims had any matching bad edge, and **all 8 had already been dropped by the contested-collapse resolver** before seeding (verified against the live DB: 0 rows). **What is really there is a different, larger population F3's review never saw**, found by a new detector rather than by extrapolating F3's list: **72 reversed pairs / 96 candidate rows**, almost entirely *minor* characters (Asius, Polydamas, Machaon, Eteoneus, Apisaon…) rather than the top-20 subjects F3's promotion review covered — which is precisely why a `variant_claims`-shaped review could never have surfaced them. **39 of the 72 were live in the seeded `relationships` table**, a table that is seeded rather than review-gated, i.e. serving user queries. New audit check **A11** (`ingestion/audit/parentage_direction.py`, 12 tests): for each `parent_of` edge it reads the source's own text and counts the patronymic formula both ways (`"<child>, son of <parent>"` vs `"<parent>, son of <child>"`), reporting only when the reversed reading is attested and the correct one never is, anywhere in that source. Requiring `correct == 0` is deliberately conservative — a name reused across generations attests both directions and is left to a human. **A11 is A3's complement, and the gap between them is the point**: A3 can only see a direction error that *closes a cycle*, which requires the correct edge to also exist, so of the 72 it had caught exactly **one** (`Eurymachus`/`Polybus`, the only mutual pair in a 4,466-edge graph). **A self-caught defect in the first cut of the check, worth recording because it was load-bearing**: matching `entities.name` literally missed every edge stated in a variant translation spelling — Murray's *Iliad* writes "Athene" where his *Odyssey* writes "Athena" — which scored the whole `Athena`/`Zeus` pair as "no evidence either way" while the Iliad states it twice. Resolving through the existing `entity_aliases` table (the same one `ConflictLookup` uses) found 2 further reversed edges, including **`Antiochus parent_of Heracles`, which no check had ever caught**; that fix is pinned by its own tests. Fix applied as a **swap, not a delete** — the kinship is real and cited, only the direction was wrong, so swapping recovers a true attested edge where dropping would lose one; verified from the git diff that all 96 changes are pure `from_name`/`to_name` swaps with `relation`/`source_id`/`passage_ref` untouched, and logged per-row to `ingestion/audit/direction_fix_log.json` for unit revertability (the DEV-104/113 discipline). |
| **Reason** | Picked as the highest-value of P4's carried-forward items because it was the only one where *wrong data was being served*: `relationships` is seeded, not review-gated, so unlike the `variant_claims` half F3 fixed behind a promotion gate, these edges reached users. |
| **Impact** | **Data + new audit check; no application code, no schema, no migration V-number added.** Files: `ingestion/audit/parentage_direction.py` (new, A11), `ingestion/audit/tests/test_parentage_direction.py` (new, 12 tests), `ingestion/extraction/output/relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (96 rows swapped across 72 pairs: iliad 45, apollodorus 28, odyssey 18, homeric-hymns 3, ovid 2), `ingestion/audit/direction_fix_log.json` (new, per-row record), `ingestion/audit/README.md` (A11 section + count 10→11), `core-api/.../V11__seed_relationships.sql` (regenerated, 3365→**3369** rows). **Post-reseed audit**: **A11 PASS (0 reversed)**; **A3's seeded cycles 1 → 0 — the live `parent_of` graph is now acyclic**; A1/A2/A4/A5/A7/A8/A9/A10 unchanged. **DEV-114's shared-root-cause hypothesis is now measured, and it mostly does not hold**: A3's *candidate*-layer cycles moved only **92 → 87**, so reversed direction explains **5 of 92 (~5%)** — the other 87 need the separate explanation F0c originally sampled (name collisions), and the P4 closure banner's "these three are the same shape of problem, plausibly one pass" should not be relied on. **A6 moved in both directions and is reported honestly rather than netted out**: total dropped-parent rows **739 → 732** (−7), but *unwaived* findings **49 → 76** (+27), because giving 72 entities a correct parent they previously lacked creates new contested-parentage groups whose subjects F0c's waiver text was never written for (e.g. `Admete`, now correctly a child of Eurystheus, contests with the Oceanid Admete — a genuine name collision the old wrong direction hid). That is new *visibility*, not new breakage, and it means A6's outstanding triage pass is now larger, not smaller. **Eval**: `python -m runner --runs 3 --label a11-parentage-direction-fix` — **20/25 = 80%** (≥75%), zero transport errors, all floors met (`CONFLICT` 7/7 = 100% against F4's new 0.6 floor, `DATA` 71%, `REFUSAL` 75%), `compare.py` vs. F4's accepted run reports **zero stable regressions, exit 0**; the single delta is `Q3` (*"How did Odysseus escape Polyphemus?"*) flipping stable-pass → flaky on one run's keyword miss — a pure RAG question over `narrative_chunks` that never touches the `relationships` table, and already documented as a known flake by DEV-094. Suites: ingestion **344**, evaluation **75**, `./gradlew :core-api:test` all green. **One incidental pre-existing defect found and left unfixed, recorded rather than glossed**: `tests/test_claim_extractor.py::test_extract_facts_passes_model_and_response_schema` hardcodes `assert kwargs["model"] == "claude-opus-4-8"` but reads the real `EXTRACTION_MODEL` env var, which the repo `.env` sets to `claude-sonnet-5` — so the ingestion suite is **344 passed with a bare `pytest`, 343 passed + 1 failed when `.env` is sourced first**. Both files are untouched by this work (last modified in Stage 4, `4216309`), so it predates it; what makes it newly reachable is DEV-115's own README fix, which now instructs every audit user to `set -a && source ../.env && set +a` — the same `.env`-vs-`ingestion/` interaction as DEV-115 finding (2), one layer down. The test should isolate the env var (monkeypatch) rather than assert a value the environment owns; not fixed here because it is unrelated to parentage direction and deserves its own decision. **Still carried forward**: A3's 87 candidate-layer cycles (now known *not* to be mainly a direction problem), A6's enlarged 76-finding unwaived backlog, Q21's synthesis instability (DEV-117), and this env-dependent test. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-29 |

### DEV-119 — A6 dropped-parent backlog worked to zero unwaived: 76 findings triaged per-row, 3 genuine variant traditions promoted, and F0c's factually-false blanket waiver replaced with seven true reasons

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Post-P4, fourth of DEV-115's carried-forward items — the A6 backlog DEV-114 found mis-waived and DEV-118 enlarged (unwaived 49 → 76). |
| **Original Plan** | DEV-114/DEV-115 left this as: the A6 findings "need either per-row review or waiver reasons that are actually true", after an attempt to blanket-waive them with F0c's boilerplate was caught as inaccurate and reverted. F0c's reason asserts "this row's child is outside the frozen top-20 list", which is false for every row whose child *is* top-20. |
| **What Changed** | **Reframed first, which is what made the rest tractable**: an A6 finding is not a defect to be prevented. ADR-007/`CLAUDE.md` are explicit that a contested relationship keeps **one** canonical edge in `relationships` and the contradiction lives in `variant_claims`, so the resolver dropping a rival parent is *correct behaviour* — A6 exists to prove the rival was not **lost**. Every waiver reason below is a statement about where the rival ended up. **All 76 unwaived findings triaged into seven buckets, each with its own true reason**: (1) **29** whose child is genuinely outside the frozen top-20 — F0c's original reason, but re-verified against the live A8 ranking rather than assumed, which is exactly the check DEV-114 found missing; (2) **15** whose dropped parent is *already* among that child's promoted `variant_claims` rows — matched diacritic- and alias-insensitively after round 1 wrongly called `Odysseus <- Laërtes` uncovered against a promoted `son of laertes`; (3) **7** already reviewed and rejected as extraction errors in P4, carrying `trust_tier=2` (DEV-113's tier), so the drop agrees with an existing human verdict; (4) **18** reviewed here against their cited passage and found to contain **no parentage claim at all** (F1's misattributed-passage shape — `Achilles <- Zeus` cites an Idomeneus battle scene, `Diomedes <- Xuthus` an Athamas/Ino passage, `Zeus <- Styx`/`Zeus <- Pallas` the Theogony's Oceanid roster); (5) **2 reversed-direction** (`Hermes <- Eudorus`, `Nestor <- Pisistratus` — both are the subject's *son*); (6) **2 of a NEW error shape not previously documented**; (7) **3 genuine variant traditions, promoted rather than waived-away**. **The new error shape (6) is worth its own note — in-narrative deception**: a character stating a *false* parentage while disguised, which the extractor recorded as fact. Aphrodite, posing as a mortal to Anchises, says *"Otreus of famous name is my father"* **immediately after** *"know that I am no goddess"*; Hermes, disguised as a Myrmidon before Priam, names Polyctor as his father. Distinct from F1's misattributed passages (the passage IS about the subject and DOES state a parentage), F2's Homeric formulae, and F3's reversed direction — the text says exactly what was extracted, and is lying. **(7), the actual data win**: `Agamemnon`/`Menelaus` **<- Plisthenes** and `Patroclus` **<- Polymele**, each verified against Apollodorus directly — *"Aerope became the wife of Plisthenes, who begat Agamemnon and Menelaus"* (3.2.1-3.2.2) and *"or, as Philocrates says, she was Polymele, daughter of Peleus"* (3.13.8-3.14.2, a third named mother alongside the already-promoted Sthenele and Periopis). Promoted to `trust_tier=1` on the keyed 5-tuple (DEV-104's rule, never positional), with the promotion script asserting no target was already at tier 1 or 2 before writing. **Also found, waived rather than fixed because it is an A1-class entity merge and not an A6 decision**: `Saturn` exists as a separate `other_god` **entity** rather than an alias of the `titan` `Cronus`, even though the parallel `Jove`/`Jupiter`→Zeus and `Juno`→Hera aliases *do* exist — so `Zeus <- Saturn` and `Hera <- Saturn` are true claims arriving under a Roman name, not second parents. Recorded as owed work. **A11's recall limit, found here rather than assumed**: (5)'s two reversed edges are invisible to DEV-118's A11 detector because Homer phrases them as "bare to Hermes" rather than the "X, son of Y" formula A11 keys on — the two checks are genuinely complementary, and neither subsumes the other. |
| **Reason** | The user selected this item. It was also the honest thing to own: DEV-118 enlarged this backlog (49 → 76 unwaived) as a side effect of correcting 72 edge directions, so leaving it would have meant shipping a fix that made an existing audit signal worse and walking away. |
| **Impact** | **Data + waiver-policy change; no schema, no application code, no new migration V-number.** Files: `ingestion/audit/audit-waivers.json` (+76 A6 entries across 7 distinct reasons, replacing zero-coverage — the pre-existing 608 F0c waivers are untouched), `ingestion/extraction/output/variant_claims_candidates.json` (3 rows `trust_tier` 3→1; distribution now `{3: 6985, 1: 324, 2: 120}`), `ingestion/audit/promotion_log.json` (+1 batch `a6-genuine-rivals`, keyed for unit revert), `core-api/.../V12__seed_variant_claims.sql` (regenerated, 293→**296** rows). **`python -m audit` now exits with A6 = WAIVED, down from FINDINGS** — every check is PASS or WAIVED except A3's 87 candidate-layer cycles, which DEV-118 established is a separate name-collision investigation. **Live behaviour confirmed, not just row counts**: *"Who was the father of Agamemnon?"* now returns a genuine attributed cross-source conflict — Homer's `son of Atreus` (Iliad 1.1-1.52, Odyssey 1.1-1.43) against Apollodorus's `child of Plisthenes` (3.2.1-3.2.2) — which is precisely the disagreement-surfacing this product exists for. **Eval**: `--runs 3 --label a6-triage-genuine-rivals` — **21/25 = 84%**, *up* from DEV-118's 80%, zero transport errors, all floors met (`CONFLICT` 7/7 = 100% against the 0.6 floor, `DATA` 71%, `REFUSAL` 75%), `compare.py` reports **zero stable regressions, exit 0**. Two informational deltas, both expected: `Q22` (*"Who was the mother of Patroclus?"*) conflicts **4 → 5**, which is Polymele arriving as the third attested mother; and `Q3` recovering `flaky` → `stable-pass`, confirming DEV-118's note that its flake was pre-existing noise rather than fallout from the direction fix. Suites: ingestion **344** green. **Still carried forward**: A3's 87 candidate-layer cycles, the `Saturn`→`Cronus` entity merge (new, above), Q21's synthesis instability (DEV-117), and DEV-118's env-dependent `test_claim_extractor` failure. **Addendum, 2026-07-29** — asked afterwards whether every finding from this session was written down, a verification pass (the DEV-115 discipline) found **one that was not**, now logged as **GAP-006**: `Ajax` is fragmented across **15 entities** for two people, proven co-referent by `Telamon parent_of` six of them and `Oileus`/`Oïleus parent_of` four, with the father duplicated as well. It matters beyond tidiness because it **defeats two of our own checks**: A1 flags none of these pairs (its fuzzy threshold cannot span `Great Ajax`↔`Telamonian Aias`), and A11 cannot see the reversed `Ajax the Lesser parent_of Oïleus` because the fragmented name never appears in the corpus in the patronymic formula A11 keys on. It also plausibly explains DEV-110's unexplained observation that `Ajax` had no promotable `marriage`/`epithet` candidates — its evidence is split 22-vs-15 across the cluster. Not fixed here: it is the same A1-class merge as GAP-004 and belongs with it, not inside an A6 triage. |

### DEV-120 — `test_claim_extractor` no longer asserts a value the environment owns; the ingestion suite is now green both bare and with `.env` sourced

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Post-P4, fifth of DEV-115's carried-forward items — the env-dependent test failure DEV-118 found incidentally and explicitly left unfixed ("should isolate the env var (monkeypatch) rather than assert a value the environment owns; not fixed here because it is unrelated to parentage direction and deserves its own decision"). |
| **Original Plan** | None — a pre-existing defect (both files last touched in Stage 4, `4216309`), not a deviation from any planned work. |
| **What Changed** | `test_extract_facts_passes_model_and_response_schema` took the `monkeypatch` fixture and now pins `ce.EXTRACTION_MODEL` to a sentinel (`"sentinel-extraction-model"`), asserting that value reaches the `model` kwarg. `extract_facts` reads the module global inside the function body, so the sentinel genuinely flows through — this is **stronger** than the alternative of asserting `kwargs["model"] == ce.EXTRACTION_MODEL`, which any wiring would satisfy, including none. The import-time `os.environ.setdefault("EXTRACTION_MODEL", ...)` fallback was changed from the real model name `claude-opus-4-8` to the neutral `test-extraction-model`, matching the `test-key` placeholder one line above, so nothing in the file reads as a claim about which model ADR-008 names; a comment records that both values exist only to let the module import. |
| **Reason** | The user picked this item off a ranked list of workable data problems. It is the cheapest of the carried-forward items and the only one that made the suite's result depend on shell state: the test hardcoded `assert kwargs["model"] == "claude-opus-4-8"` while `extract_facts` reads the real `EXTRACTION_MODEL`, which the repo `.env` sets to `claude-sonnet-5`. Newly reachable because DEV-115's README fix now instructs every audit user to `set -a && source ../.env && set +a` before running from `ingestion/`. |
| **Impact** | **Test-only; no production code, no data, no schema, no migration, no eval.** File: `ingestion/tests/test_claim_extractor.py` (1 test + the setdefault fallback). **Verified in both environments, which is the point of the fix**: bare `pytest` → **344 passed**; `set -a && source ../.env && set +a && pytest` → **344 passed** (was 343 passed + 1 failed). No `.env` change, and `EXTRACTION_MODEL` is left exactly as the environment sets it. **Still carried forward**: A3's 87 candidate-layer cycles, the `Saturn`→`Cronus` entity merge (GAP-004) and the `Ajax` 15-entity fragmentation (GAP-006) as one A1-class merge pass, GAP-002's 347-name residual triage, GAP-001 root cause 3's ~690 non-top-20 dropped rows, GAP-005's in-narrative deception shape, and Q21's synthesis instability (DEV-117). |
| **Date** | 2026-07-29 |

### DEV-121 — GAP-004 + GAP-006 closed as one entity-merge pass: `Ajax` de-fragmented from 15 entities to 2, `Saturn`→`Cronus`, plus three namesake/duplicate findings the merge itself surfaced

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Post-P4, sixth of DEV-115's carried-forward items. Both gaps were filed as "A1-class entity merge, belongs with the other one" (GAP-004 by DEV-119's A6 triage, GAP-006 by that entry's own verification addendum), so they were worked as a single pass rather than two. |
| **Original Plan** | `docs/DATA-GAPS.md` GAP-004 and GAP-006, both "Open — P5 (A1/J1 entity-merge backlog)". |
| **What Changed** | **Canonical-name choice follows this file's own precedent** (DEV-078/079/080/081/082): the bare name stays with the more central, more-referenced figure and the namesake takes a `Name (descriptor)` form. So `Ajax` = the Telamonian (49 of the 54 bare-name candidate rows are his) and the Locrian becomes **`Ajax (son of Oileus)`**, matching `Cecrops (son of Erechtheus)` / `Pandion (son of Cecrops)` / `Ilus (son of Dardanus)`. **Every one of the 73 cluster rows was assigned by reading its cited passage, not by its surface form** — which is what turned five bare-`Ajax` rows out to be the *Locrian*: `Oileus parent_of Ajax` + `Medon sibling_of Ajax` at Iliad 15.328 ("Medon … son of godlike Oïleus, and brother of Aias"), the two funeral-games rows at 23.473/23.753 ("swift Aias, son of Oïleus"), and Apollodorus E.6.6's shipwreck. **`Saturn` → `Cronus`** as an `entity_aliases` row, alongside V14's existing `Jove`/`Jupiter`/`Juno` for the same translator. **Three findings the merge itself surfaced, none of them predicted by either gap entry**: (1) **`Ops` is a namesake collision** — merging `Saturn married_to Ops` blindly would have produced `Cronus married_to Ops` where the confirmed `Ops` is the *male* Ops of the Odyssey, Eurycleia's father ("Eurycleia, daughter of Ops, son of Pisenor"); the Ovid row was repointed to `Rhea`, which is who Ovid's Roman Ops actually is. (2) **`Oileus` is a namesake collision too** — `Oïleus killed_by Agamemnon` (Iliad 11.84) is Bienor's charioteer, not the Locrian king who is alive for another twelve books; split off as `Oileus (charioteer of Bienor)`. (3) **A sixth Ajax-cluster duplicate that the first pass missed**: `Oïleus` (diaeresis) was *itself* a confirmed entity, not merely an unconfirmed surface form — the initial sweep grepped ASCII `oile` and the diaeresis defeated it, the same non-ASCII blind spot DEV-118 hit with `Athene`/`Athena`. It was caught by **A5** on the first post-merge audit ("'Oïleus' is both an entity_aliases.alias and an existing entities.name"), i.e. by running the *full* suite after a batch rather than only the checks that looked relevant — the DEV-108 `Helios` discipline catching a self-introduced duplicate a second time. **A1 scores the pair 83.3, under its 88 threshold**, so it would never have surfaced it. **Six reversed edges fixed as swaps, not deletes** (the DEV-118 rule: the kinship/kill is real and cited, only the direction was wrong), each verified against its own passage: `Ajax the Lesser parent_of Oïleus` (the one GAP-006 predicted A11 structurally cannot see, because the fragmented name never appears in the corpus in the patronymic formula A11 keys on), plus five `killed_by` rows where Ajax is the *killer* — Satnius (14.440), Archelochus (14.463), Caletor (15.419), Laodamas (15.516), and Apollodorus E.6.6 where Poseidon kills Ajax, not the reverse. **Two rows dropped as unsupported by their own cited passage** (the DEV-098 shape): `Ajax killed_by Hector` @ Ovid 13.82-13.127 (Ajax's own speech about Hector at the ships; no death stated) and `Ajax killed_by Odysseus` @ 13.280-13.312 (Ulysses' speech; the suicide is at 13.386ff, so the claim is real but not at this ref, and moving the ref would be inventing provenance). |
| **Reason** | The user picked this item off a ranked list. It was ranked first for effort-to-value: the co-reference was already *proved* by the data's own edges (`Telamon parent_of` six of the forms, `Oileus`/`Oïleus parent_of` four), so no new research was needed, and GAP-006 defeats two of our own audit checks at once (A1 by fuzzy distance, A11 by name-vs-corpus mismatch), which no amount of running them harder would fix. |
| **Impact** | **Data + one migration; no application code, no schema change.** Files: `ingestion/extraction/output/entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` (2003 → **1989**: −15 merged away, −1 for the missed `Oïleus`, +2 new), `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (6884 → **6882** rows; 41 endpoint renames, 6 direction swaps, 2 drops), `variant_claims_candidates.json` (2 `subject_name` renames, no tier changes), `ingestion/extraction/known_aliases.json` (+16), `core-api/.../V20__add_entity_aliases_ajax_and_saturn.sql` (**new**), `V10`/`V11` regenerated (relationships 3369 → **3364**), `ingestion/audit/audit-waivers.json` (−6 waivers this pass made stale, +1 reason-text correction), `scripts/reseed-local.sh` (`'20'` added to `CLEAR_HISTORY_SQL` — the Flyway ordering trap DEV-100 and DEV-108 each hit once). **Live `entity_aliases` 42 → 58.** **Post-reseed audit**: every check **PASS or WAIVED except A3's 87 candidate-layer cycles**, unchanged — i.e. identical to the pre-pass gate state. A5 PASS (0 findings), A11 PASS, A1 unchanged at 41 waived pairs, live `parent_of` graph still acyclic. **Live behaviour confirmed, not just row counts**: `Ajax (son of Oileus)` now holds one coherent 8-edge biography (father Oileus, brother Medon, kills Cleobulus and Satnius, drags Cassandra, drowned by Poseidon, buried by Thetis) where the same facts were previously scattered across six entities with the parentage pointing backwards; `GET /api/v1/conflicts/Telamonian%20Aias` resolves through the new alias and returns the Telamonian's claims. **Eval**: `--runs 3 --label gap004-gap006-entity-merge` — **20/25 = 80%** (≥75%), zero transport errors, `compare.py` vs. DEV-119's accepted run reports **zero stable regressions, exit 0**. The single delta is **Q2** (*"What was Prometheus punished for?"*) stable-pass → flaky: run 1 omitted the `eagle`/`liver` keywords, runs 2-3 had them. It is a pure RAG question over `narrative_chunks` — a table this pass never touched, on a subject with no entity or relationship edit — so it is the same chat-model synthesis variance already recorded for Q3 (DEV-094/118) and Q21 (DEV-117), not fallout. Q8/Q9/Q11/Q21 were already stable-fail or flaky in the baseline and are unmoved. Suites: ingestion **344**, `./gradlew :core-api:test` green. **New leads recorded rather than worked**: (a) **A11 has no non-parentage sibling** — five of the six direction errors here were `killed_by`, which no check looks at, and they were found only because a merge happened to walk those rows; (b) **`Ops parent_of Pisenor`** is reversed in the same way (the Odyssey says Ops is *son* of Pisenor), noticed and left; (c) **32 A6 waivers are stale for reasons predating this pass** — measured by re-running A6 against the pre-pass candidate files rather than assumed, so the 6 removed here are exactly the ones this pass caused, and the other 32 are someone else's cleanup. **Still carried forward**: A3's 87 candidate-layer cycles, GAP-002's 347-name residual triage, GAP-001 root cause 3's ~690 non-top-20 dropped rows, GAP-005's in-narrative deception shape, Q21's synthesis instability, and the three new leads above. **Addendum, 2026-07-29** — asked afterwards whether every finding from this session was written down, a verification pass (the DEV-115/DEV-119 discipline) found **four that were not**, one of them wrong data currently being served. All four are now written up in `docs/DATA-GAPS.md` under GAP-004+GAP-006 → *"Findings this pass did not fix"*, and the first is filed as **GAP-007**: (1) **`Zeus parent_of Ajax` is LIVE in the seeded graph**, not a dropped rival — ADR-020's co-mention carve-out reads the Homeric vocative formula *"Aias, sprung from Zeus, thou son of Telamon"* (Iliad 7.233) as one passage naming two co-parents, so `Ajax` is seeded with **both** Telamon and Zeus and appears in gold Q7's "which heroes are children of Zeus" answer. This is a **new failure mode for ADR-020's four-part discriminator**, not an extraction error: rule 1 can't help (the extractor never flagged it contested) and rule 2 can't help (Telamon *is* the winner and Zeus is in the pair) — it is precisely what rule 4's deny-list exists for, which is still seeded with `Io` alone. (2) **GAP-006's prediction about DEV-110 is disproved, and the real mechanism is more interesting**: after merging all 16 entities `Ajax` still has **zero** `marriage`/`epithet` candidates, so fragmentation was not splitting that evidence — the epithet material was never shaped as `epithet` claims at all, it was extracted as *entity names* (`Telamonian Aias`, `Great Ajax`), which is exactly where this pass has now put it, as `entity_aliases`. (3) **8 `Telamon | parentage | child of Ajax` candidates** sit at `trust_tier=3` — F3's reversed-direction shape, unreviewed so not live, and untouched here. (4) **A1's 88 threshold misses a measurable class, not just one pair**: `Oileus`/`Oïleus` **83.3** and `Helios`/`Helius` **83.3** (DEV-108) score *identically*, with `Diomed`/`Diomedes` at **85.7** (DEV-100) — three independent real duplicates clustered in 83-86, each found by a different check after the fact. That is an argument for a diacritic-folding pre-pass rather than a threshold nudge, since folding would take `Oileus`/`Oïleus` to 100 without loosening anything else. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-29 |

### DEV-122 — GAP-007 closed: ADR-020's deny-list gains its second entry, the formula sweep it asked for is run and bounded to three edges, and two reversed `parent_of` edges A11 structurally cannot see are fixed

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Post-P4, seventh of DEV-115's carried-forward items — the first one that was *wrong data being served*, filed as GAP-007 by DEV-121's own verification addendum. Bundled with three cheap, already-diagnosed leftovers from that same addendum's "new leads, recorded rather than worked" list. |
| **Original Plan** | `docs/DATA-GAPS.md` GAP-007, "Open — P5 (deny-list sweep)", plus DEV-121's leads (b) `Ops parent_of Pisenor` reversed, (c) 32 stale A6 waivers, and finding (3) the 8 `Telamon | parentage | child of Ajax` rows at `trust_tier=3`. |
| **What Changed** | **The sweep was run before the fix, and it is what makes this a bounded change rather than an open-ended one.** GAP-007 asked for "a sweep, not a single deny-list row" but did not size it. Measured across all six corpus files: the divine-epithet formulae (`"sprung from Zeus"` and relatives) occur **49× in the Iliad, 43× in the Odyssey, 1× in the Homeric Hymns, and 0× in Apollodorus, Hesiod's *Theogony*, and Ovid** — so only edges resting on Homer/Hymns can carry the defect. Of **534** seeded children with ≥2 parents, **98** have a major god as one, and only **3** have a divine parent attested *solely* by those sources; a second pass over **single**-parent children (a slice the original gap never mentioned, and the reason the sweep found more than it was looking for) added **3** more. All six were read against their cited passage. **`Ajax <- Zeus` is the one false positive** — and the formula is live at **three** refs (Iliad 7.233, 9.643, **11.456**), not the two GAP-007 named. **`Molione <- Poseidon`** is genuine and explicitly non-formulaic (*"their father, the wide-ruling Shaker of Earth"*, 11.737, a real dual mortal/divine parentage alongside `Actor`); **`Pandia <- Zeus`** genuine (Hymn 32.14); **`Naiads`/`Nymphs of the fountain <- Zeus`** genuine (*"Naiad Nymphs, daughters of Zeus"*, a claim Homer repeats). **`Ate <- Zeus` turned out to be a different defect entirely and is now GAP-008** — see below. **The deny-list entry is keyed on child + parent pair, not passage**, so one row covers all three formula refs. **Both halves of the class now agree**: `Ajax` also carried **five** unreviewed formula rows in `variant_claims` (`child of Zeus` ×2, `sprung from Zeus` ×2, `son of Telamon, sprung from Zeus`), all verified against the same passages and rejected to `trust_tier=2`, extending DEV-112/F2's identical verdict on `Patroclus \| parentage \| sprung from Zeus`. **Two reversed `parent_of` edges fixed as swaps, not deletes** (DEV-118's rule): `Ops parent_of Pisenor` → `Pisenor parent_of Ops` (Odyssey 1.421, *"Eurycleia, daughter of Ops, son of Peisenor"*) and, **found while checking that one and not previously recorded anywhere**, `Clitus parent_of Pisenor` → `Pisenor parent_of Clitus` (Iliad 15.442, *"Cleitus, the glorious son of Peisenor"*). **Why A11 missed both, diagnosed rather than assumed**: A11's rule (reversed reading attested, correct reading never attested) would have fired on each, but its `_spellings` alternation is built from `entity_aliases`, and the entity is `Pisenor` while every corpus occurrence is `Peisenor` — the same translation-spelling blind spot as `Athene`/`Athena` (DEV-118) and `Oileus`/`Oïleus` (DEV-121), now a third instance. **The obvious repair for that was drafted and then discarded, which is the most transferable result of this pass**: adding `Peisenor`→`Pisenor` and `Cleitus`→`Clitus` spelling aliases would restore A11's recall, but checking the rows first (DEV-096/098/121's rule) showed **`Clitus` conflates three distinct men** (Apollodorus 2.1.5's son of Egyptus/Tyria who married Clite; the Iliad's son of Peisenor; the Odyssey's son of Mantius, carried off by Dawn) and **`Pisenor` at least two** — so the alias would have asserted a false identity. Not added; both recorded as GAP-002 bucket-2 entries instead. **The `Telamon` reversed-direction rows were 10, not the 8 DEV-121 counted** — the two `child of Teucer` rows are the same shape (Iliad 13.170 and 15.436, *"Teucer, son of Telamon"*) and were missed because DEV-121 only looked at rows naming Ajax. All 10 rejected to `trust_tier=2`, verified by A11's own criterion: the Iliad says `"son of Telamon"` **22 times** and `"Telamon, son of"` anyone **never**. **32 stale waivers removed** (31 A6 + 1 A3 — DEV-121 said "32 A6", measured here as 31 A6 plus the A3 `Eurymachus`/`Polybus` cycle waiver, stale since DEV-118 made the live graph acyclic). Classified rather than bulk-deleted: **7** are stale because DEV-118 flipped the edge and the finding now exists in reversed form (already re-waived by DEV-119), **24** because the finding is simply gone. |
| **Reason** | The user picked this bundle off a ranked list, on the argument that GAP-007 was the only open gap serving wrong data and the other three were already fully root-caused by DEV-121 — so the whole set fits one `seedgen --strict` → `reseed-local.sh` → `audit` → `runner --runs 3` → `compare.py` loop. Working GAP-007 first also matched its own framing: the deny-list is what ADR-020 rule 4 exists for, so this is the mechanism working as designed rather than a rule change. |
| **Impact** | **Data only; no application code, no schema, no new migration V-number** — and therefore no `CLEAR_HISTORY_SQL` edit, the Flyway ordering trap DEV-100/DEV-108/DEV-121 each hit once. Files: `ingestion/extraction/parentage_deny_list.json` (1 → **2** entries — its first addition since ADR-020 shipped it seeded with `Io` alone), `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (3 rows swapped, 6 lines), `variant_claims_candidates.json` (**15** rows `trust_tier` 3→2; distribution now `{3: 6970, 1: 324, 2: 135}`), `ingestion/audit/promotion_log.json` (+2 rejection batches, keyed for unit revert), `ingestion/audit/audit-waivers.json` (−32 stale, +2 new A6 with per-row true reasons), `V11__seed_relationships.sql` (3364 → **3363**). **The −1 is fully accounted for, not netted**: `Zeus parent_of Ajax` removed by the deny-list, `Clitus parent_of Pisenor` removed because after the swap `Pisenor` becomes a *rival* parent in `Clitus`'s contested group and loses to Apollodorus's spine-ranked Egyptus+Tyria couple, and `Pisenor parent_of Ops` **added** — it had previously been dropped as a rival, since `Ops`/`Clitus` were competing to be `Pisenor`'s parent and the Odyssey outranks nothing on the spine. **V12 is byte-identical** (296 rows): every rejected row was at `trust_tier=3` and so had never been seeded — the rejections change what a future reviewer sees, not what runtime serves. **Post-reseed audit: every check PASS or WAIVED except A3's 87 candidate-layer cycles — identical to the pre-pass gate state.** A11 PASS, A5 PASS, A1 unchanged at 41 waived pairs, live `parent_of` graph still acyclic. A6 went FINDINGS → WAIVED after the two new findings this pass caused were given per-row reasons (`Ajax <- Zeus`: not a lost rival, the drop *is* the fix; `Clitus <- Pisenor`: a real claim whose child is a namesake collision, so a GAP-002 lead rather than a promotion candidate). **Live behaviour confirmed, not inferred from row counts**: `Ajax` now has exactly one parent, `Telamon`, from four sources, and gold **Q7** returns *"Arcas, Arcesilaus, Castor, Dardanus, Epaphus, Heracles, Iasion, Perseus, Pollux, Rhadamanthys, Zethus"* — `Ajax` gone, both required keywords present. **Eval**: `--runs 3 --label gap007-denylist-and-direction-fixes` — **22/25 = 88%**, the project's best result to date (up from DEV-121's 80%), **zero transport errors** (so the run is valid evidence per TODO2's cross-cutting rule), all floors met (`CONFLICT` 7/7 = 100% against the 0.6 floor, `DATA` 71%, `REFUSAL` 100%), `compare.py` **zero stable regressions, exit 0**. **The two deltas are reported with their causes rather than claimed as wins**: Q21 (REFUSAL) recovered stable-fail → stable-pass with `conflicts[]` 4 → 0, but **this pass cannot be the cause** — V12 is byte-identical, so no `variant_claims` row Q21 could read changed; it is the chat-model synthesis variance DEV-117 diagnosed, resolving in the favourable direction this time, and DEV-117's item stays open. Q2 flaky → stable-pass is a single-run delta the gate ignores. Q9 remains flaky, unmoved from baseline. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-29 |

**Findings this pass did not fix** (the DEV-115/119/121 discipline, run before writing this entry rather than after being asked):

1. **`Zeus parent_of Ate` cites the wrong passage — now GAP-008.** Iliad 9.496-9.528's "daughters of great Zeus" are the **Prayers** (Litai), not Ate; Homer does state Ate's parentage, but at **19.90**. Found by the single-parent half of GAP-007's sweep. Left unfixed deliberately: dropping the row discards a true claim and repointing the ref is what DEV-121 called inventing provenance, so it needs a decision made once for the class. The class matters because the misattributed-passage shape has been found **20 times** in *dropped* or *review-gated* rows (DEV-119's 18, DEV-121's 2) and **never once swept in the seeded table**.
2. **`Clitus` (3-way) and `Pisenor` (2-way, 3 with the Odyssey herald) namesake collisions** — filed under GAP-002 bucket 2. Note the sub-class is new for that bucket: these are *already-confirmed entities*, not unknown names, so A2's unknown-name drilldown will never surface them.
3. **A folding/aliasing pre-pass on A1 needs a namesake guard.** DEV-121 finding (4) proposed diacritic folding over a threshold nudge, on three duplicates clustered at 83-86. This pass found the boundary condition: folding and aliasing are only safe where the surface form denotes exactly one figure, and `Cleitus`/`Peisenor` are exactly the case where it does not.
4. **`Telamon`'s own parentage holds an unreviewed genuine variant tradition.** Alongside the 10 reversed rows, `Telamon | parentage` carries `child of Aeacus` (×4, two sources) against `child of Actaeus` + `child of Glauce`, the latter pair being Apollodorus 3.12.6 explicitly reporting Pherecydes: *"Telamon was not the brother of Peleus but a friend, son of Actaeus and Glauce daughter of Cychreus."* That is a promotable cross-source conflict of exactly the shape DEV-119 promoted for Agamemnon/Plisthenes, left at `trust_tier=3` because promotion is not this pass's scope.
5. **The extractor is inconsistent about the same formula across layers.** Iliad 11.456 produced an `Ajax | sprung from Zeus` *variant claim* but no `Zeus parent_of Ajax` *relationship* row, while 7.233 and 9.643 produced both. Harmless here (the deny-list is passage-independent), but it means neither layer's row count can be used to bound the other's.

**Still carried forward**: A3's 87 candidate-layer cycles, GAP-002's 347-name residual triage plus the two new bucket-2 collisions, GAP-001 root cause 3's promotion half (a′, ~690 rows), GAP-005's in-narrative deception shape, **GAP-008** (new), Q21's synthesis instability (DEV-117, unresolved despite passing this run), and DEV-121's lead (a) — a `killed_by` direction check, A11's missing non-parentage sibling.
