### DEV-123 — A12 (reversed `killed_by`) and A13 (citation resolvability) added; A11's comma-less-patronymic recall bug found and fixed; GAP-008's proposed detector built, measured at 82% noise, and recorded as a dead end

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Stage** | Post-P4, eighth and ninth of DEV-115's carried-forward items — DEV-121's lead (a) ("A11 has no non-parentage sibling") and GAP-008 (filed by DEV-122). Plus DEV-122's finding (4), the Telamon Pherecydes variant, taken as a follow-on. |
| **Original Plan** | DEV-121 lead (a): "a `killed_by` direction check is the obvious A11 companion." GAP-008: "for each seeded `parent_of` edge, check that the cited passage contains the child's name and the parent's name *inside one kinship construction*… A11's `_KINSHIP` regex is most of the machinery already." |
| **What Changed** | **A12 (`audit/kill_direction.py`, 12 tests) — built, and it earns its place: `killed_by` is 872 distinct candidate edges and no check had ever read one.** That gap was not theoretical — five of DEV-121's six direction fixes and both of DEV-122's were `killed_by`, and *every one was found by accident* while walking rows for another reason. Same evidentiary rule as A11 (`correct == 0`, alias-aware, reports only), both voices matched because Homer prefers the active ("Idomeneus slew Phaestus") and Frazer the passive ("Parthenopaeus was slain by Periclymenus"). **Two calibration findings, both kept as tests:** (1) the filler between the two names must **exclude capitalised words** — a third proper noun there is almost always the verb's real subject, and Iliad 15.47's "…Patroclus shall goodly Achilles slay Hector…" was being read as "Patroclus slew Hector", wrong twice over. Since every translation writes epithets in lower case, excluding capitals keeps the epithets and drops the intervening-subject case; the documented cost is that a patronymic in that position is no longer matched — lost recall, never a wrong report. (2) `(?-i:[a-z])` is load-bearing: the patterns compile with `re.IGNORECASE` so spellings match regardless of case, which silently made `[a-z]` match capitals and undid rule (1) entirely. A character-budget first cut (`[^.;:]{0,60}`) reported **16**; word budgets took it to 7; the case-scoping fix to **6**. **All 6 read against their passage: 5 genuine, 1 false positive.** **A13 (`audit/passage_support.py`, 7 tests) — GAP-008's proposed rule was built and it does not work.** Measured against the full corpus it flags **82% of 5,259 citations** (4,355). Two rounds of widening moved it 87% → 82% only, because the residue is structural, not lexical: **enumeration** (Apollodorus 3.12.5 lists ~40 sons of Priam after one verb — 252 characters from "Priam had sons" to "Echephron"), **relative clause** ("Helios whom mild-eyed Euryphaessa … bare to"), **coordination** ("Aeetes, son of the Sun…, and brother of Circe and Pasiphae" implies the parentage across two clauses), and **periphrasis** (Iliad 19's "the valiant son of Menoetius"). What shipped is the decidable part of the same scope: **a citation must resolve to a real segment.** Not trivial — `passage_ref` is the provenance the product shows a user, `seedgen` copies it through unchecked, and `relationships` has no review gate. **A11's own recall bug, found by A13's calibration run and fixed:** `_KINSHIP` began `(?:,\s*)?`, so nothing consumed the space in the **comma-less** patronymic and "Amphilochus son of Alcmaeon" — as common in Frazer and Murray as the comma'd form — scored **zero**; the possessive "Andraemon's son Thoas" was missed outright. Both fixed, with 3 regression tests. **The fix immediately surfaced a real reversed edge invisible to every prior run: `Echeclus parent_of Agenor`, against Iliad 20.474's "Agenor's son Echeclus".** **Telamon's Pherecydes variant promoted** (the follow-on): Apollodorus 3.12.6 states both traditions in one sentence — "Aeacus married Endeis … by whom he had two sons, Peleus and Telamon. **But Pherecydes says** that Telamon was a friend, not a brother of Peleus, he being a son of Actaeus and Glauce" — so `child of Aeacus`/`child of Endeis` and `child of Actaeus`/`child of Glauce` were promoted to `trust_tier=1`, the same shape DEV-119 promoted for Agamemnon/Plisthenes. |
| **Reason** | The user picked A12 and A13 off a ranked list, then added the Telamon promotion as a follow-on. A12 ranked first on measured evidence rather than symmetry: two consecutive passes had stumbled into reversed `killed_by` edges by accident, which is what an unchecked relation looks like from the outside. |
| **Impact** | **Two new checks + one check fixed + data; no application code, no schema, no new migration V-number** (V10/V11/V12 regenerated in place). Files: `ingestion/audit/kill_direction.py` (**new**, A12), `ingestion/audit/passage_support.py` (**new**, A13), their two test modules (**new**, 19 tests), `ingestion/audit/parentage_direction.py` (`_KINSHIP` separator + new `_POSSESSIVE`, `_attests` reads both word orders), `ingestion/audit/tests/test_parentage_direction.py` (+3), `relationships_candidates_cleaned.json` (6 direction swaps, 4 citation corrections, 5 rows repointed to a split entity), `entities_candidates_confirmed_v1.json` (1989 → **1990**), `variant_claims_candidates.json` (4 rows 3→1; `{3: 6966, 1: 328, 2: 135}`), `audit/promotion_log.json` (+1 batch), `audit/audit-waivers.json` (+1 A12 waiver), `audit/README.md` (A12/A13 sections, count 11 → 13). **Seeded: relationships 3363 → 3367, variant_claims 296 → 300, entities 1989 → 1990.** **The row-level V11 delta is fully accounted for**, including a regression this pass caused and then fixed: swapping `Copreus killed_by Iphitus` gave `Iphitus` two killers, and the contested collapse — choosing alphabetically within one spine source — dropped **`Iphitus killed_by Heracles`, attested in both Apollodorus and the Odyssey**, in favour of a single minor Copreus row. Root cause was a namesake collision (`Iphitus` son of Eurytus, whom Heracles kills, vs the Phocian son of Naubolus, father of Schedius), so the five rows whose own passages tie Iphitus to Eurytus were split off as **`Iphitus (son of Eurytus)`**, per the DEV-078/121 precedent; the ambiguous Copreus row stays on the bare name rather than being assigned on a guess. A second side effect was a **gain**: unswapping Patroclus's group un-contested it, so `Patroclus killed_by Hector` now seeds from both Homer and Apollodorus, which it previously did not. **Post-reseed audit: every check PASS or WAIVED except A3's 86 candidate-layer cycles** (87 → 86, the Echeclus swap) — i.e. the pre-pass gate state. A13 **PASS** after all four citations were corrected; A12 **WAIVED** on its one false positive. **Live behaviour confirmed, not inferred from row counts**: `GET /api/v1/conflicts/Telamon` now returns the Pherecydes disagreement as four attributed versions — `child of Aeacus` / `child of Endeis` against `child of Actaeus` / `child of Glauce`, all cited to Apollodorus 3.12.6 — which is the disagreement-surfacing this product exists for. **Eval**: `--runs 3 --label a12-a13-checks-and-telamon-variant` — **22/25 = 88%**, holding DEV-122's best-to-date result, **zero transport errors**, all floors met (`CONFLICT` 7/7 = 100%, `DATA` **86%** up from 71%, `REFUSAL` 100%), `compare.py` **zero stable regressions, exit 0**. Two informational flips, both single-run deltas the gate ignores and neither attributable to this pass: **Q8** (DATA) stable-fail → **flaky**, an improvement on a question this pass did touch (`Zeus`/`Danae parent_of Perseus` had their citations corrected); **Q2** (FACT) stable-pass → flaky, a pure-RAG question over `narrative_chunks` on a subject with no entity or edge edit here — the same chat-model synthesis variance already recorded for it in DEV-121 and for Q3/Q21 elsewhere. **Suites**: ingestion **366** (was 344; +22 new), evaluation **75**, `./gradlew :core-api:test` green. |
| **Date** | 2026-07-29 |

**A12's one known false-positive shape, waived rather than engineered around — pronoun anaphora.** Iliad 5.655 has both men strike: *"Sarpedon smote **him** full upon the neck, and the grievous point passed clean through, and down upon his eyes came the darkness of night"* (Tlepolemus dies — the seeded edge is **correct**), then *"And Tlepolemus smote Sarpedon upon the left thigh"* (a wound). A12 sees the second and not the first, because the kill names its victim with a pronoun. No regex over entity names resolves that reference, so `correct == 0` cannot suppress it. Waived with the passage quoted, and flagged in the README as the check's designed limit rather than a bug to fix later.

**Findings this pass did not fix** (the DEV-115/119/121/122 discipline, run before writing this entry):

1. **GAP-008 is still open, and now has a recorded dead end rather than an untried idea.** The construction-matching rule cannot be made precise with surface patterns; the four failure modes are written up in GAP-008 and the A13 README section specifically so it is not re-attempted with a wider regex. `Zeus parent_of Ate` is still live and still cites the wrong passage.
2. **`Iphitus` is a namesake collision this pass only half-resolved.** `Iphitus (son of Eurytus)` was split off on evidence; the bare `Iphitus` still conflates the Phocian son of Naubolus (father of Schedius and Epistrophus) with whoever Copreus killed at Apollodorus 2.5.1 and with the Trojan Iphitus of Iliad 8.128 (father of Archeptolemus). Filed as a GAP-002 bucket-2 lead, not guessed at.
3. **A11's recall fix has not been re-measured against the `variant_claims` half.** DEV-114/F3 used the same comma-dependent reading of the patronymic to triage reversed `variant_claims` rows. If that triage shared the assumption, its recall is understated by the same bug, and rows were left at `trust_tier=3` that should have been rejected. Not checked here.
4. **A12 covers `killed_by` only.** `married_to` (344 seeded edges) and `sibling_of` (49) still have no direction or support check of any kind. `married_to` at least has stable surface vocabulary ("wedded", "wife of"), so it is the natural next sibling.

**Addendum, 2026-07-29 — asked whether every finding was documented, the verification pass found one that was not, and it is an uncomfortable one: I wrote eight misattributed citations into permanent documentation.** Every book/line reference quoted from memory or estimated from a grep offset, rather than resolved against the corpus's own `[n]` markers, was checked. Eight were wrong:

| written | actual | where |
|---|---|---|
| Iliad 15.68 | **15.47** | DEV-123, `kill_direction.py`, `audit/README.md`, A12 test |
| Iliad 16.732 | **16.712** | `kill_direction.py` comment |
| Iliad 5.657 | **5.655** | A12 waiver, DEV-123, `audit/README.md` |
| Iliad 11.465 | **11.456** | DEV-122, GAP-007 resolution, promotion_log rationale |
| Iliad 15.445 | **15.442** | DEV-122, GAP-002 bucket-2 entry |
| Iliad 11.750 | **11.737** | GAP-007 resolution (the Moliones) |
| Odyssey 1.429 | **1.421** | DEV-122 |
| Odyssey 15.249 / 2.38 | **15.238** / **2.35** | GAP-002 bucket-2 entry |

All corrected. One more was found in **DEV-121**, predating this work and left by a different pass: `Oïleus killed_by Agamemnon` is cited as Iliad **11.93**, actually **11.84**. Corrected in place rather than left standing.

**This is the same defect class as GAP-008** — a true claim attached to a citation that does not point where it says. The irony is the point: a pass that built a check for misattributed provenance in the *data* introduced eight instances of it in the *prose*, because prose citations have no check at all. `passage_ref` values in the candidate JSON are safe (they are copied from the extraction segment and now verified by A13); free-text references in `docs/` are not, and nothing verifies them. Worth a lint if this recurs — the corpus files carry the `[n]` markers needed to resolve any `Book.line` pair mechanically, which is exactly how these eight were caught.
